somewhere between gridiron and stardust

by adasbearlover

Summary

She was ice and perfection; he was high school lights and hopeless devotion. When dark pasts crash into neon arenas, two rising stars learn love is the ultimate high-stakes gamble.

Chapter 1

The court may whisper in the gilded dark,

And trade in rumors of our blackened names;

They call us dangerous, a spark

To set their weary kingdoms in a flame.

Let foolish voices chatter in the street,

And weave their brittle lies of who we were;

They do not know how fiercely our hearts beat,

Nor how we conquer what the we stir.

For you and I are painted in the stars,

A stormy royalty that time shall prove.

Though rumors strike like battle scars,

They cannot break the fortress of our love.

Let all the world conspire to see us fall,

We rule the wreckage, standing tall and grand;

Through every quiet night and wilder brawl,

I’ll hold your sovereign, blood-stained hand.

They built their traps, but we survived the tide,

A glorious ruin they could never tame.

Now side by side, with nothing left to hide,

We write our history in spark and flame.

No bitter gossip tears this crown apart,

No petty king shall see our kingdom fall;

For in this war, thou art my beating heart,

Thou art my start, my sum, my end of all.

The morning light was practically violent. It bounced off the polished concrete pathways, sliced through the leaves of the oak trees, and drove small, agonizing spikes directly behind Leon’s right eyeball.

He sat slumped on the weather-worn wooden bench near the edge of the central quad, his knees spread wide, hands resting uselessly on his thighs. He wore a plain white t-shirt that had seen better days, a pair of faded denim jeans with frayed hems, and his navy baseball cap pulled on backward to keep his messy blonde hair out of his face. To top off the disaster, he was wearing a pair of thick, black-framed glasses he usually kept hidden in the bottom of his locker.

"You look like a disgraced librarian who got kicked out of a frat house," Chris said, leaning against the back of the bench. He had a grin slapped across his face that was far too bright for eight in the morning.

"Shut up," Leon mumbled. His voice sounded like gravel being scraped along a driveway.

"No, really," Claire chipped in, standing next to her brother with her arms crossed over her red hoodie. "The glasses are a choice. Is this the new varsity quarterback look? The wounded intellectual?"

"I am actively dying," Leon stated, closing his eyes behind the lenses. "My brain is swelling. It is pressing against my skull. If I die here, tell my coach I died for the team."

"You didn't die for the team, you idiot. You almost drowned yourself in cheap tequila because someone said you couldn't do a thirty-second keg stand," Chris pointed out. He nudged Leon’s shoulder with his elbow. "I told you at midnight to stop."

"You tried to fight the punch bowl, Leon," Claire added, totally merciless. "Chris and I literally had to drag you away from the kitchen counter. We held you back by your belt loops while you tried to explain the physics of beer pong to a freshman."

"I was making good points," Leon groaned, squeezing his eyes shut tighter. "The angle of trajectory matters."

"You were slurring so badly she thought you were speaking German," Chris laughed.

"I hate both of you. Why are you so loud? Why is the sun so bright? Why is the grass so green? It’s unnatural."

A small plastic bottle hit Leon square in the chest, followed immediately by a foil packet of pills that bounced off his lap and landed on his jeans.

"Stop whining, Kennedy. You're embarrassing the entire athletic department," Helena said, walking up from the direction of the student center. She had her gym bag slung over one shoulder and an iced coffee in her hand.

Leon blinked, slowly reaching down to pick up the painkillers. "Helena. You're an angel. An ugly angel, but an angel."

"Take the ibuprofen before I throw another bottle at your face," she replied, sitting down on the opposite end of the bench with a sigh. "I saw you last night. You were a total mess. Chris, how did you even get him back to the house?"

"Claire carried his feet, I grabbed his shoulders," Chris said. "He kept singing varsity fight songs the whole way up the driveway."

"I have a vast reservoir of school spirit," Leon muttered, twisting the cap off the water bottle. The plastic made a sharp crack that made him flinch. He tossed two pills into his mouth and downed half the water in one long gulp.

"You have a vast reservoir of bad choices," Claire corrected. She pulled out her phone, checking the screen. "Anyway, the video of you falling into the hydrangeas is already on three different group chats."

Leon groaned, letting his head drop backward over the top rail of the bench. His backward cap shifted, bumping against the wood. "Delete it. Use your computer science magic and delete it from the internet."

"I'm a major in political science, Leon. I can't delete your shame from the cloud."

"Chris, make her delete it."

"Hey, I think it highlights your natural agility," Chris joked, slapping Leon on the back.

Leon winced instantly. "Don't touch me. My skin hurts. My hair hurts."

"That's just the hangover talking," Helena said, taking a sip of her iced coffee. "Give it twenty minutes. The meds will kick in, or your heart will just stop. Either way, the headache goes away."

"Thanks for the sympathy, guys. Really feeling the love from my inner circle."

"We saved you from getting arrested by campus security," Claire pointed out, poking him in the arm. "We deserve a medal. Or at least a free breakfast once you can walk in a straight line again."

"I can walk fine," Leon protested weakly, though he made no effort to stand up. He adjusted his glasses with one finger, pushing them up the bridge of his nose. "I just choose to sit. The earth is spinning too fast today."

"Right. Sure," Chris said, turning his head as footsteps sounded along the concrete path nearby. "Hey, isn't that Rebecca?"

Leon didn't bother opening his eyes. "Who?"

"Rebecca from biology," Claire said, leaning forward to look past Chris. "The girl who tutors the freshmen linebackers so they don't fail out of the program."

"Oh yeah," Chris nodded. He cupped his hands around his mouth. "Hey! Rebecca!"

Down on the path leading from the science hall toward the open field, Rebecca paused, mid- sentence. She was walking with another girl, gesturing with a stack of folders. She turned her head toward the bench and waved cheerfully when she saw Chris.

The girl beside her, however, took a slower moment to react.

Helena leaned forward, her eyes narrowing. "Wait. Is that Ada?"

"Who?" Chris asked, glancing at the second girl.

Walking beside Rebecca was Ada Wong. She was wearing a tailored, long-sleeve red top with the top two buttons undone, paired with fitted short red skirt. Her look was completed by sleek black boots, a pair of chic, red-rimmed eyeglasses perched on her nose, and a leather messenger bag on one shoulder.

Helena raised two fingers to her mouth and let out a long, low whistle that echoed across the open space. "Damn," Helena said, lowering her hand. "Is that who I think it is? Ms. Star player figure skater?"

"Miss...huh...wha...figure skater?" Claire repeated, looking surprised. "Ada Wong? She’s the one who took gold at the regional competition last year, right?"

"Yeah, that’s her," Helena confirmed, nodding. "Seeing her out here on the main lawn is rare. She’s usually living in the rink or the research building. Seeing her actually walking across the grass is like spotting a rare bird in the wild."

"What is she doing with Rebecca?" Chris asked.

"Rebecca's in her research group," Claire offered. "They're working on some big department project."

As Chris had called out, Ada glanced up from her conversation with Rebecca. Through her red-rimmed glasses, her cool, dark eyes swept over the quad, passing over the bushes, the trash cans, and finally settling directly on the bench where the four of them were gathered.

Leon froze. He felt an odd, sharp prickle at the back of his neck.

Ada locked eyes with Leon, her gaze lingering on his backward cap, his faded jeans, and the thick, black frames on his nose. A tiny, almost imperceptible smirk touched the corner of her lips.

Then she adjusted her glasses, turned her head away, and continued walking alongside Rebecca toward the administration building, her boots clicking softly against the pavement until she vanished around the corner.

The bench was silent for a full three seconds.

"Did she just look at you?" Chris asked, turning to Leon with a surprised grin.

"She definitely looked at you," Claire agreed, her eyebrows raised. "And she smiled. Well, she almost smiled. That's practically a marriage proposal coming from her."

"She was judging him," Helena countered, though she was smiling too. "She was looking at the state of the varsity football team and wondering how this university stays accredited."

Leon sank back against the bench, resting the back of his head against the wooden slats once more. His heart was doing a strange, fluttering tap-dance against his ribs, making his headache throb with a fresh, sharp pulse.

"She wasn't judging me," Leon muttered, though he couldn't quite stop the small, dopey grin from spreading across his face.

"Oh boy," Chris sighed, shaking his head. "Here we go. He's hooked."

"He's helpless," Claire agreed. "Leon, she will eat you alive. She eats guys like you for breakfast and doesn't even save the leftovers."

"I'll let her. I can be charming, you know," Leon said again, closing his eyes. "I'll talk to her."

"You can barely form sentences right now," Helena reminded him, taking another drink of her coffee. "Focus on surviving the afternoon first. Take your meds, drink your water, and try not to fall off the bench."

"I am a picture of health," Leon stated firmly, though his voice cracked slightly at the end.

Chris laughed, clapping a hand onto Leon’s knee. "Come on, lover boy. Let's get you back to the house before you try to write a poem or something equally tragic."

"I don't write poems," Leon grumbled, letting Chris and Claire hook their arms under his shoulders to hoist him up off the bench. His legs felt a little shaky, the world tilting just a bit as he stood straight, but he kept his glasses pushed up his nose and his backward cap secure on his head.

He looked one last time down the empty pathway where Ada had vanished, the quad quiet again under the bright morning sun.

"I'm telling you," Leon said, taking a slow step forward between his friends. "Next time, I'm wearing a better shirt."

"Next time, don't drink a bathtub full of tequila," Claire said softly, guiding him toward the dorms.

"Yeah, yeah," Leon sighed, squinting into the sunlight. "Lesson learned."

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

The locker room smelled of cheap body spray, stale laundry detergent, and the damp scent of wet grass from yesterday's rain. Fluorescent lights hummed overhead, glaring across the row of dented metal lockers that lined the varsity football facility.

Leon stood frozen in front of the tall mirror bolted to the wall beside the showers. He adjusted the collar of his fresh crimson polo shirt, smoothed down the front, then lifted his hand to mess with his damp blonde hair. He pushed a fringe to the left, frowned at the mirror, then pushed it back to the right.

"If you stare at your face for one more second, you are going to turn into a statue," Chris called out from three lockers down. He was leaning back against his locker door, securely tying his cleats. "Seriously, man. You look stupid."

"I am adjusting," Leon stated, not taking his eyes off his reflection. He pulled his navy baseball cap from his back pocket, placed it on his head, turned it backward, and then took it off again. "Does the hat make me look like I’m trying too hard?"

"Yes," Chris said immediately. "And so does the shirt. And so does the fact that you have checked your appearance in four different reflective surfaces since we walked into the building."

"It’s called taking pride in my personal hygiene," Leon argued. He ran a palm along his jawline, checking for missed spots from his morning shave. "First impressions matter."

"First impressions?" Claire’s voice echoed from the doorway as she walked in, unbothered by the fact that she was in the men’s locker room. "Leon, she saw you yesterday sitting on a park bench with a hangover so bad you were wearing prescription glasses from 2021 and begging the universe for death. The first impression is long gone."

"That was a trial run," Leon insisted, turning away from the mirror for half a second before glancing right back into it. "Yesterday was a baseline. Today is the comeback."

"You don't even know if she’s going to be at the student center today," Chris pointed out, leaning his head back against the metal lockers. "You’re dressing up for a girl who might be across campus in a lab or at the ice rink doing backflips."

"Quadruple toe loops," a sharp voice corrected from inside Chris’s open locker.

Jill Valentine pulled her head out from behind a stack of Chris’s folded athletic shorts, her short dark hair slightly messy from diving through his gear. She was wearing her blue varsity tracksuit, holding a frayed blue notebook in her hand.

"Found it," Jill said, slapping the notebook against Chris’s chest. "You swore you didn't have my biology notes, Redfield."

"I forgot I put them in the bottom compartment," Chris muttered, taking the notebook with an apologetic grin. "My bad."

Jill turned her gaze toward Leon, crossing her arms over her chest as she leaned her hip against the bench. "Give it up, Kennedy. Ada is not going to take your ass."

Leon adjusted his collar one more time. "You don't know that. I have a lot to offer."

"You have a C-minus in microeconomics and a reputation for falling into rose bushes at three in the morning," Jill said smoothly. "Do you know who tried to ask her out last semester?"

"Who?" Chris asked, suddenly interested.

"The governor’s son," Jill said, holding up one finger for emphasis. "The guy brought a bunch of red roses to her figure skating practice, asked her out to some insane dinner date at that steakhouse downtown, and offered to drive her home in his sports car."

Leon stopped fussing with his hair. He turned slowly toward Jill. "And?"

"She looked him dead in the eye, told him his cologne was giving her a migraine, and skated away," Jill said with a wicked grin. "Didn't even take the roses. Left the guy standing at the rink barrier holding a bouquet like a complete fool. So if you think a backward cap and a clean shirt are going to break through her defense, you are sorely mistaken."

"That guy was a tool," Leon said, though his confidence flickered for a fraction of a second. "I have charm. I have conversational skills."

"You have a concussion from drinking tequila," Claire added, leaning against the doorframe with her arms crossed.

"I didn't get a concussion," Leon grumbled.

"Hey, are you guys talking about the Ada?"

The door to the equipment room swung open, and Carlos walked out carrying a mesh bag packed with inflated footballs. His broad shoulders barely cleared the door frame, his tight jersey damp with sweat from his morning drills. He dropped the bag on the floor with a loud thud and wiped his forehead with the back of his forearm.

"Ada Wong?" Carlos clarified, looking between the three of them.

Chris, Claire, Jill, and Leon all turned their heads simultaneously, sharing a long, silent look.

"Why?" Chris asked, narrowing his eyes at Carlos. "How do you know her?"

"We're blockmates," Carlos said nonchalantly, popping his neck. "She’s in my nine o'clock criminology lecture. Sitting right across the aisle from me."

Leon practically lunged across the locker room floor, grabbing Carlos by the shoulder of his jersey. "You share a class with her? Since when?"

"Since the start of the semester, man," Carlos said, looking down at Leon’s hand on his shoulder with mild amusement. "Calm down, quarterback. Why are you so hyped up?"

"He’s in love," Claire announced from the doorway.

"He’s delusional," Jill corrected.

"I am making a strategic acquaintance," Leon corrected them both, glaring at the girls before turning back to Carlos with absolute focus. "What is she like in class? Does she talk to anyone? Does she mention liking football? Does she mention liking guys who are athletes?"

Carlos laughed,"Bro, she doesn't talk to anyone. She walks in five seconds before the professor starts talking, writes down everything on a leather notepad, and leaves before the bell even finishes ringing. She’s like a phantom."

"A beautiful phantom," Leon murmured, adjusting his backward cap again.

"A phantom who will slice you into pieces with her ice skates," Jill pointed out.

"She sits right by the window," Carlos continued, ignoring Jill. "Always wears these really nice outfits. Today she’s wearing those red glasses again."

Leon’s heart gave a strange, unexpected thump against his ribs. "The red ones?"

"Yeah," Carlos nodded. "The pretty ones."

"See?" Leon turned to Chris, pointing an finger at his chest. "Fate. It is total fate."

"It’s not fate, it’s a course schedule," Chris said, standing up and tossing his duffel bag over his shoulder. "Carlos, don't encourage him. He’s going to embarrass the entire team."

"Hey, I think it’s funny," Carlos chuckled, leaning against the row of lockers. "If you want, Kennedy, I can save the seat next to me in class tomorrow. You can sit in and pretend you care about international criminal law."

"I care deeply about international criminal law," Leon lied instantly. "It’s my favorite subject."

"You're a history major," Claire said dryly. "You don't even know what criminology means."

"I can learn!" Leon insisted, throwing his hands up in the air. "I am adaptable! I am a leader on and off the field!"

"You're a golden retriever in a varsity jacket," Jill said, walking past him toward the exit. She patted him on the shoulder as she passed, offering a sympathetic shake of her head. "Good luck, Kennedy. You are going to need every single prayer in the book."

"Thanks for the support, Jill," Leon called after her.

"Don't die out there!" Claire added, following Jill out into the hallway.

Chris shook his head, looking at Leon with pity and amusement. "We have practice in ten minutes, lover boy. Are you going to change into your pads, or are you going to keep admiring your polo shirt?"

Leon looked down at his shirt, then back at his reflection in the mirror one final time. He reached up, took off his backward cap, smoothed down his blonde hair, and placed the hat back on his head, angled slightly to the side.

"I'm ready," Leon declared, unbuttoning his polo to start changing into his football gear. "Practice first. Strategic planning second."

"Just don't drop the ball during drills because you're busy thinking about figure skaters," Chris warned, heading toward the field door. "Coach will make you run laps until your legs fall off."

"I never drop the ball," Leon said confidently.

Carlos chuckled as he headed out after Chris, throwing one last remark over his shoulder. "She likes coffee, by the way. Black. No sugar. I saw her with a cup from the espresso bar near the library."

Leon froze mid-action, his jersey pulled halfway over his head. He popped his head out through the neck hole, his messy hair sticking up in three different directions. "Black? No sugar?"

"Not a single drop of milk," Carlos confirmed with a nod before disappearing through the double doors leading to the sunny field outside.

Leon stood in the middle of the empty locker room as he processed this fresh piece of information, running a hand through his blonde locks once more before grabbing his helmet from the top shelf of his locker.

"Black coffee," Leon whispered to himself, a small smirk forming on his face. "Got it."

He slapped his helmet onto his head, strapped the chin guard tight, and marched out toward the field, determined to survive practice so he could begin phase two of his mission.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

Barry Burton stood at the fifty-yard line, a silver whistle clutched between his teeth and a thick leather clipboard resting under his arm. His crimson coach's polo stretched over a frame that looked like it could still tackle a linebacker, and his eyes narrowed as the varsity squad assembled into formation.

"Move it, move it!" Barry bellowed over the low rumble of neighboring practice fields. "We have three weeks until kickoff against Raccoon State, and right now you look like a collection of sleepy toddlers. Redfield, get your helmet buckled before I make you lap the stadium until sundown!"

Chris yanked his chin strap into place with a quick snap. "Got it, Coach."

"Kennedy!" Barry’s gaze pinned Leon, who was currently doing a quick wrist flex near the snap line. "You look oddly awake today. Did you actually sleep, or did you just drink four energy drinks before stepping onto my grass?"

"Fully rested and locked in, sir," Leon replied, slapping his padded gloves together with a crisp leather crack.

"Good. Prove it," Barry barked, raising his whistle. "First team offense, set the line! We are running the West Coast route. Redfield on the post, Oliveira on the slant. If anyone drops a clean pass, the whole squad gets fifty burpees."

Carlos winked at Leon from the wide receiver split. "No pressure, quarterback."

Leon dropped into his stance behind the center, his fingertips resting lightly against the gridiron leather. The nervous energy that usually buzzed in his chest during morning prep was completely gone. Whatever jokes Jill and Claire had tossed at him in the locker room vanished the second his cleat bit into the white turf chalk.

"Set! Blue forty-two! Blue forty-two!" Leon barked out. "Hut!"

The snap hit his palms perfectly. Leon took a swift three-step drop, his soles carving small divots into the fake grass. The defensive line surged forward, a wall of navy jerseys charging through the gaps, but Leon did not flinch. He stepped smoothly into the pocket, tracked Chris cutting sharply across the middle, and released the ball with a fluid whip of his arm.

The football spiraled cleanly through the air, tight and perfectly angled, dropping straight over the safety's outstretched fingertips into Chris’s breadbasket. Chris caught it in stride, crossing the goal line without slowing down.

"That's what I'm talking about!" Barry shouted, slapping his clipboard against his thigh. "Again! Back to the line! Kennedy, nice drop."

"Thanks, Coach," Leon said, jogging back to the huddle with a small grin.

For the next forty-five minutes, Barry ran them through a brutal gauntlet of passing plays, blitz pick-ups, and hurry-up offenses. The sun beat down relentlessly, soaking their jerseys with sweat, but Leon remained in total control. He read the defense like a book, calling audibles at the line when he noticed a cornerback playing too far back, tossing short touch passes over linebacker heads, and firing bullet throws right into the chest of his receivers.

"You're on fire today, man," Carlos said, breathing heavily as he trotted back to the huddle after snaring a fifteen-yard out route along the sideline. "What did you eat for breakfast?"

"A healthy dose of determination," Leon said, wiping sweat from his forehead with the sleeve of his jersey.

"He's running on pure delusion," Chris muttered, though he offered Leon an approving bump of their shoulder pads. "Keep throwing like that, and Coach might actually let us leave five minutes early."

"Don't count on it," Carlos laughed. "Burton doesn't believe in early dismissals."

As if hearing his name, Barry blew his whistle three times in short, sharp bursts. "Water break! Five minutes! Get your helmets off and get some liquid in you!"

The team let out a collective groan of relief, instantly breaking formation and heading toward the hydration station near the bleachers. Sweat dripped down Leon's neck as he unbuckled his chin strap and pulled his helmet free, shaking out his blonde hair. He grabbed a plastic squeeze bottle from the metal rack, spraying cold water directly into his mouth before pouring the rest over the top of his head to cool off.

"Nice drive on that third-and-long," Chris said, leaning against the chain-link fence as he gulped down water. "You saw the blitz coming?"

"The linebacker shifted his weight to his front foot right before the snap," Leon explained, wiping his dripping face with a small white towel. "Gave it away immediately."

"Look at you, quarterback extra," Carlos chuckled, taking a seat on the wooden bench. "Actually paying attention in film study."

"I always pay attention," Leon insisted.

"Right," Chris said dryly. "Except when you're looking for lost hair product or trying to figure out how to talk to girls who figure skate."

"That was one time," Leon grumbled, taking another sip of water. "And for your information, tomorrow's strategy is already set. I am going to buy a black coffee, walk into that criminology class like I belong there, and sit next to Carlos."

"I am not letting you copy my notes," Carlos warned, raising a finger. "I barely understand the professor myself."

"I don't need your notes, I just need the seat," Leon said. He paused, squinting toward the far side of the athletic complex.

Beyond the football field lay the varsity track, wrapped around a manicured lawn that led directly toward the glass dome of the university ice rink. Students occasionally ambled along the concrete path, carrying backpacks or sports bags, but one figure across the field instantly caught his eye.

A tall woman was walking past the clearing fence near the bleachers. She wore a tailored black coat despite the heat, her dark hair cut into an elegant bob that swung slightly with each step. And perched on her face was a distinct pair of red-framed glasses that caught the bright glare of the afternoon sun.

Leon's breath hitched in his throat. He lowered his water bottle, his hand freezing mid-air.

"Hey," Leon whispered, nudging Chris hard in the ribs with his elbow.

"Ouch! What?" Chris complained, rubbing his side.

"Is that... is that her?" Leon asked, pointing a gloved finger toward the concrete walkway fifty yards away.

Chris and Carlos both leaned forward, squinting through the chain-link fence.

"Where?" Carlos asked.

"By the gate near the track," Leon said quickly, his heart hammering against his ribs. "The one in the black coat."

Carlos peered closely for a second, then nodded. "Oh yeah. Ada."

Leon stood up straight, his post-drill exhaustion instantly vanishing. He smoothed down the front of his football jersey, completely forgetting he was wearing massive shoulder pads and pants covered in grass stains. "She's looking over here. Is she looking over here?"

"Leon, she is walking toward the library," Chris said, exasperated. "She isn't even facing the field."

"She glanced this way," Leon insisted, taking a small step toward the sideline fence. "I felt it. It was a mutual observation."

"You are losing your mind, Kennedy," Chris laughed, shaking his head.

Before Leon could take another step, a shadow fell over the three of them. Coach Burton materialized like an immovable wall, his hands resting on his hips, his clipboard tucked beneath his arm.

"Is there something fascinating about that fence, Kennedy?" Barry asked, his voice low and dangerous.

Leon snapped his head back around. "No, Coach!"

"Because unless that fence is learning how to run a proper slant route, I suggest you turn your focus back to this field," Barry said, pointing a thick thumb over his shoulder toward the yard lines. "Break time is over. Helmets on. We are running team drills until the sun goes down."

"Yes, Coach," Leon said quickly, shoving his helmet back onto his head and buckling the strap with rapid speed.

As Barry walked away to yell at the defensive line, Leon stole one final glance over his shoulder toward the open path.

The space near the bleachers was empty. The figure in the tailored coat was gone, having melted away into the bustling campus crowd near the library steps, leaving behind only the bright sunlight reflecting off the concrete.

"Focus, lover boy," Chris teased softly, jogging past him toward the huddle. "Save the romance for criminology tomorrow."

Leon adjusted his chin strap, a determined smirk breaking across his face as he trotted back onto the turf. "Tomorrow," he promised himself quietly. "Tomorrow."

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

The living room was a disaster zone of discarded red plastic cups, half-empty pizza boxes, and someone’s forgotten laundry, but Leon barely registered the mess. He practically floated past the couch where two of his housemates were sprawled out playing video games. "Hey, Kennedy," one of them called out over the synthetic screech of tires from the television screen. "How was practice? Coach Burton run you into the dirt again?"

"Practice was great," Leon said, his voice bright, almost dreamlike. "Practice was completely amazing."

"Whoa," the other guy said, turning his head to squint at Leon’s goofy smile. "Did you take a helmet to the forehead today or what?"

"I am in my peak," Leon declared, pointing a finger at them as he backed up toward the staircase. "Peak physical and mental condition!"

He bounded up the stairs two at a time, humming an off-key tune under his breath. Once inside his room, he tossed his athletic bag onto his desk, stripped off his sweaty polo shirt, and grabbed a clean towel from his closet. The communal bathroom down the hall was empty for once, illuminated by a single warm bulb above the long row of sinks.

Leon stepped into the stall, twisted the rusted chrome handle, and let the warm stream of water cascade over his hair and shoulders. He leaned his forehead against the wet tile, closing his eyes, and immediately the image of those red-framed glasses flashed across his mind.

A soft, foolish giggle escaped his chest before he could stop it. He clamped a hand over his mouth, splashing water onto his face to try and snap out of it, but the memory stayed put.

"She is so pretty," Leon muttered to himself, shaking his head as the warm water rinsed the shampoo from his blonde hair. "Like, ridiculous. Who looks like that while walking past a muddy football field? Nobody."

He giggled again, sound echoing off the slick plastic curtain. He couldn't help it. She was sharp, she was intimidating, and she was so pretty he could die. It was completely intoxicating.

After scrubbing down, he dried off, pulled on a fresh pair of gray sweatpants, and trotted back to his bedroom. He didn't even bother checking his phone or turning on the desk lamp. He collapsed onto his mattress, pulled the duvet up to his chin, and stared up at the popcorn ceiling with a massive grin plastered across his face.

"Black coffee," Leon whispered into the dark room, his smile stretching until his cheeks ached. "Tomorrow morning. Criminology."

His eyes closed, and within minutes, he was sound asleep, still wearing that same dopey, smitten smile.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

Alarm clocks were usually Leon’s worst enemy. On any given Tuesday, he would hit the snooze button at least four times before dragging his body out of bed at the absolute last possible second.

Not today.

At five forty-five in the morning, before the sun had even fully cleared the horizon, Leon’s eyes snapped wide open. He sat bolt upright, threw his covers aside, and jumped out of bed like he had just been hit with a lightning bolt. He threw on his best fitting jeans, pulled on a crisp white crewneck, and spent ten minutes perfecting his hair in his vanity mirror until every strand fell just right.

He grabbed his denim jacket, marched out into the silent hallway, and walked straight down to the end of the corridor where Carlos’s room was located.

Leon raised his hand and pounded three times on the thick wooden door. "Carlos! Wake up! It's time for operational prep!"

Silence greeted him from inside.

"Carlos!" Leon called out again, knocking a little louder. "Rise and shine! We have to hit the espresso bar before class!"

Behind him, a floorboard creaked loudly.

Leon turned his head toward the sound. A few doors down, the lock to Chris’s bedroom clicked, and the dark wood swung open just an inch or two. A small, slender figure stepped out into the dimly lit hallway, moving on tiptoes with her canvas sneakers held tightly in her right hand.

It was Rebecca. Not just any Rebecca. But Rebecca Chambers. She was wearing an oversized varsity sweatshirt that clearly belonged to a broad-shouldered linebacker, her short brown hair completely messy, and her eyes wide with total panic as she froze mid-step.

Leon froze too, his hand still hovering in the air near Carlos's door.

Rebecca slowly raised her gaze, meeting Leon’s wide eyes. Her face flushed a dark, fiery shade of crimson within two seconds flat.

Leon blinked. Rebecca blinked back.

Neither of them said a word. The silence stretched out between them with unspoken realization.

Slowly, very slowly, Rebecca lifted her left index finger and pressed it firmly against her lips, letting out a tiny, pleading sound. "Shh!"

Leon’s jaw dropped. He pointed a finger at her, then pointed at the door she had just closed, his eyes expanding to the size of dinner plates.

Before Leon could utter a single syllable, the door to Chris’s room swung open much wider.

"Hey, Becca, you forgot your—" Chris’s voice cut off instantly.

Chris was standing squarely in the doorway, completely, totally naked, holding out a small silver chain necklace in his hand.

"Aah!" Rebecca shrieked, whirling around and dropping her sneakers onto the hardwood floor with a loud clatter. "Chris! Put clothes on!"

"Whoa! Holy crap!" Leon gasped, covering his eyes with both hands and lunging backward against Carlos’s door. "Chris! What is wrong with you?!"

"What the—!" Chris yelled, his face turning bright red as he slammed the door shut so fast the frame rattled, disappearing into his room with a frantic scamper of bare feet.

"Shh! Quiet!" Rebecca hissed, jumping in place and waving her hands wildly at Leon. "You are going to wake up the whole entire house! Stop shouting!"

"I am not shouting, you are shouting!" Leon whispered back loudly, keeping one hand clamped over his eyes while pointing blindly with the other. "Why is Chris naked?! Why are you coming out of his room at six in the morning?!"

"Keep your voice down!" Rebecca pleaded, scrambling to pick up her dropped shoes. She ran over to Leon, grabbing the sleeve of his jacket and tugging it forcefully. "Please, Leon, do not make a scene!"

The door to Chris’s room cracked open again, and this time Chris poked his head out, his hair wildly ruffled and a pair of gray athletic shorts pulled on completely backward in his rush. "Leon," Chris growled in an urgent, embarrassed whisper. "Shut your mouth right now."

Leon slowly lowered his hand from his face, staring back and forth between the two of them. The pieces finally clicked together in his head. "You guys are... wait. You two? Since when?!"

"For three months," Rebecca confessed in a tiny voice, looking down at her socks. "Please don't tell the team. Coach Burton will literally make Chris run laps until his lungs collapse if he thinks it's a distraction."

"Three months?!" Leon squawked, his voice cracking slightly. "Chris! You were making fun of me yesterday in the locker room for having a crush, and you’ve been sneaking the head athletic trainer into your bedroom all semester?!"

"It's a secret!" Chris snapped, stepping out into the hall and grabbing Leon by the shoulders with a stern grip. "Do you understand the concept of a secret, Kennedy? If this gets back to Jill or Claire, I will never hear the end of it. Ever."

"Shh!" Rebecca whispered again, slapping Chris’s arm. "Both of you, stop!"

All three of them stood in a tense circle in the dimly lit hallway, holding their breath and listening for any signs of movement from the surrounding bedrooms. When no doors opened, they all let out a collective sigh of relief.

"Okay," Leon whispered, raising his hands in surrender. "Okay. I won't say anything. Your secret is safe with me, Redfield."

Chris let out a long breath, rubbing the back of his neck. "Thank you."

Leon narrowed his eyes, a clever spark igniting behind his blue eyes. He turned his full attention to Rebecca, who was trying to tuck a stray lock of hair behind her ear.

"Wait a minute," Leon said, stepping closer to her. "Rebecca. You know Ada. Ada Wong, right?"

Rebecca stopped fixing her hair, looking at Leon with confusion. Her brow furrowed, and she tilted her head, genuinely bewildered by the jump in conversation. "Wait... what? Ada? Why are you asking me about Ada?"

"Because you know her!" Leon insisted, gesturing with his hands. "You're in biology, and I think she's mostly around the science buildings. Plus, you walked right past us two days ago on the quad together! I need intel!"

Rebecca opened her mouth to express her confusion, but Chris cleared his throat loudly from the doorway.

"Becca, just so you know," Chris interjected, crossing his arms over his chest as a mischievous smirk broke across his face, "he's been obsessed since two days ago."

Rebecca glanced at Chris, rubbing her temple in total bewilderment. "Chris, to be completely honest with you, I couldn't really remember shit from two days ago. I was so stressed out."

Chris blinked. "Wait, really?"

"Yes!" Rebecca groaned softly, dropping her hands. "Ada was so mad that morning. So, so mad. The chemistry department lagged on returning our lab data sets, and it threw off our entire statistical timeline for our research project. She was pacing around the science building in complete silence which is silence awful, by the way, and by eight in the morning she looked like she wanted to set the lab on fire. I was praying the ground would swallow me whole so I wouldn't mess up any of the numbers."

"Wait, so you don't remember seeing us on the bench?" Chris asked.

"I barely remembered my own name!" Rebecca admitted, throwing her hands up in a small, exasperated gesture. "I was just trying to keep up with her walking pace while trying de- escalate the situation. I didn't register a single face on the quad!"

Leon stared at her, thoroughly scandalized. "She looked right at me! It was a pivotal moment!"

"Leon, she was probably looking at you thinking you were an obstacle in her walking path," Rebecca said dryly, shaking her head. "She was furious about the project."

"She smiled!" Leon insisted defensively, stepping closer to Rebecca. "It was a pretty girl smile! And you two work together, so you have to help me! You work in the science department, you must know her routines!"

"Well, yes," Rebecca acknowledged, letting out a tired breath. "We're co-researchers."

Leon's eyebrows shot up into his hairline. "Co-researchers? You and Ada?!"

"Yes!" Rebecca nodded, leaning in closer so her voice stayed low. "We're currently working on an advanced interdisciplinary research project together for the university honors college. It's a joint study between biochemistry and athletic performance, focusing on blood lactate recovery times in high-intensity athletes. Ada handles the statistical modeling and data analysis, and I handle the lab testing."

Leon stared at her like she had just handed him a map to buried treasure. "You work in a lab with her?! In close quarters?!"

"We spend at least eight hours a week together in the science complex," Rebecca explained. "We were working on data entry late last night, actually."

Leon grabbed Rebecca’s sleeve, his expression turning deathly serious. "Rebecca. You are my savior. I need to know everything."

"Leon, I can't just leak private details about my research partner—"

"I am keeping your secret!" Leon pointed out, gesturing wildly between her and Chris. "I just saw Chris in his rawest form! That is emotional trauma! I deserve compensation in the form of research intel!"

Chris groaned, covering his face with both hands. "Please just help him so he stops talking about seeing me naked."

Rebecca looked between the two of them, sighing softly as she realized she was entirely cornered. "Fine! Fine, okay! But you have to promise to be extremely subtle, Leon. If Ada finds out I gave you her schedule while she's already stressed about this project, she will kick me off the team."

"I am the definition of subtle," Leon swore solemnly.

"You really aren't," Chris muttered from down the hall.

Rebecca snatched Leon firmly by his arm, dragging him toward the small wooden desk sitting near the top of the stairwell. She grabbed a stray blue ballpoint pen from a plastic cup and snatched a clean white napkin from a takeout bag sitting on the ledge.

"Listen to me very carefully," Rebecca whispered, forcing Leon to lean down so she could write directly against the wooden surface. "Ada is extremely organized, especially when she's stressed about our research. She manages her hours down to the exact minute. If you try to act like a typical loud fraternity guy, she will shut you down immediately."

"I have depth," Leon insisted softly. "I can be intellectual."

"She doesn't care about pickup lines," Rebecca said, her pen scratching furiously across the napkin. "She likes efficiency. She likes people who don't waste her time or disrupt her work."

Leon leaned over her shoulder, watching as she wrote in neat, tight handwriting.

"Her schedule is very specific," Rebecca explained out loud as her pen moved. "She practices at the rink every Monday, Wednesday, and Friday from seven to nine in the morning. Then she goes directly to the science building for our research lab check-in at nine-thirty. If she has class, like her criminology lecture with Carlos, she goes straight from the lab."

"Lab at nine-thirty," Leon repeated, taking in every word. "Got it."

"After classes, she goes to the campus library," Rebecca continued, drawing a little box on the napkin. "She sits on the third floor, in the corner desk right by the history section, because it has the best lighting for reading research journals. That's where we analyze our data sets together."

"Third floor, history section," Leon murmured. "That's my major's floor!"

"Don't get overly excited," Rebecca warned, tapping the pen point against his chest. "Now, as for her tastes. When we're working late on our research project, she drinks black coffee from the espresso bar. No sugar, no cream, not even a tiny dash of vanilla. If you bring her anything sweet, she will literally toss it into the hazmat trash bin in the lab right in front of you."

"Carlos warned me about that," Leon noted, nodding along.

"And if it's past four in the afternoon," Rebecca added, scribbling another note, "she drinks Earl Grey tea. Plain. No honey. She also reads international law journals for fun, she hates small-talk about sports, and she cannot stand loud noises while she's reviewing data sheets." "No sports talk, black coffee, plain tea, history section, research lab," Leon recited, his mind racing to process the wealth of information.

"And most importantly," Rebecca said, stopping her pen and looking him right in the eye, "if you interrupt our research project meetings because you're trying to flirt, I will personally make sure Coach Burton doubles your conditioning drills. Understood?"

"Understood!" Leon promised immediately, holding his hands up. "I respect science! Science comes first!"

"Good," Rebecca said, slapping the penned napkin right against Leon's chest.

Leon caught it, holding the delicate piece of paper like it was an ancient, sacred artifact. He scanned the notes, a triumphant grin slowly spreading across his face.

"Rebecca," Leon said earnestly, placing a hand over his heart. "You are an absolute angel. I will carry your secret to my grave."

"You better," Chris muttered, pointing a warning finger at him. "Now go away. Some of us are trying to get dressed."

"Right!" Leon said, folding the napkin with extreme care and slipping it into his jacket pocket. He turned back toward Carlos’s room, knocking hard on the door once more. "Carlos! Get up! We have a coffee run to make!"

Inside the room, a loud thud echoed, followed by Carlos’s groggy, muffled voice. "Kennedy, if it is before eight in the morning, I am going to throw my pillow at your head."

"It's six fifteen!" Leon announced cheerfully, slapping the door frame. "And love waits for no man! Get dressed!"

As Chris retreated back into his room to put his shorts on properly and Rebecca quietly made her escape down the stairs, Leon stood alone in the hallway, adjusting the collar of his denim jacket. He reached into his pocket, his fingers brushing against the paper napkin, his heart giving a happy, energetic thump against his ribs.

"Phase two," Leon whispered to the empty hallway, smiling bright enough to light up the dark house. "Here we go."

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

The campus espresso bar at seven in the morning was a battlefield of exhausted students, hissed steam from overworked machines, and the scent of roasted beans. Leon stood near the pick-up counter with his spine straight, his denim jacket neatly buttoned over his shirt, and his eyes locked onto the barista like a hawk watching its prey.

In his hands, he held two paper cups. One was a sweet vanilla iced latte for himself. The other felt almost dangerously warm against his palm—a paper cup filled with two shots of pure, dark espresso poured directly over hot water. No sugar. No milk. Not a single drop of syrup. It was bitter, dark, and smelled like crushed charcoal, but Leon held it with the care of a medieval knight protecting a holy relic back from a long crusade.

"If you drop her coffee, I am not waiting for you to buy another one," Carlos mumbled, leaning against the wooden counter while rubbing his bleary eyes. He was wearing an oversized gray hoodie, his dark curly hair sticking up in random directions from sleep. "Why are we even here this early? Class doesn't start for another forty minutes."

"Preparation is the key to success, Carlos," Leon said smoothly, offering his friend a bright, energetic grin. "In football, if you show up to the stadium five minutes before kickoff, you lose. In romance, it is the exact same principle."

Carlos let out a long sigh, shaking his head as they stepped out of the coffee shop into the crisp morning air. "Man, you are completely out of your mind. She doesn't even know your name. She probably thinks you're just some guy who spends all his time running into other guys on a grass field."

"She will know my name by ten o'clock," Leon declared confidently, taking a small step past a group of sleepy freshmen walking toward the quad. "I have a plan. I have a strategy. And I have her exact coffee order."

"The 'strategy' is currently crumpled in your coat," Carlos pointed out, pointing a finger at Leon's pocket. "And if you spill that black sludge on your jeans, your entire strategic plan goes straight into the trash."

"I am not going to spill it," Leon said, adjusting his grip on the two paper cups. "My hands are steady. My focus is clear."

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

Leon followed Carlos up the central aisle, his eyes scanning the empty rows. The room was arranged in an amphitheater style, with the professor's wooden podium sitting at the very bottom near a giant white board.

"There," Leon whispered, nudging Carlos with his elbow. "Middle row. Right near the aisle. Near the window."

"That's where I always sit," Carlos said, sliding into a seat on the left side of the row and unzipping his backpack. "Sit down before you draw attention to yourself."

Leon sat down in the wooden folding chair right beside Carlos, setting the dark, unsweetened coffee carefully on the tiny flip-up desk attached to the empty chair beside him across the aisle, right where Ada theoretically would sit. He placed his own iced latte on his own desk, smoothed down the front of his jacket, checked his reflection in the dark screen of his phone, and adjusted a strand of blonde hair that had fallen across his forehead.

"You look like you're about to go on a job interview for a corporate executive position," Carlos teased, pulling a blank notebook from his bag. "Relax your shoulders, quarterback. You're making me nervous just watching you."

"I am relaxed," Leon stated, though his fingers were tapping fast against his knee. "I am completely calm."

"Sure you are," Carlos chuckled. "Your knee is vibrating so fast it's making the desk shake."

Leon forced his leg to stop moving, taking a deep breath of the air conditioned room. "What time is it?"

Carlos checked his wrist. "Eight fifty-two. We have eight minutes."

"Right. Eight minutes." Leon stared at the clock mounted on the side wall above the whiteboard. The red second hand ticked forward with agonizing slowness. One minute passed. Then two. Then three.

Students kept trickling into the room in small groups, filling up the back rows and chatting in hushed voices about upcoming midterms and weekend parties. Leon's eyes remained glued to the double doors at the top of the aisle, watching every single person who walked through.

Eight fifty-seven. Eight fifty-eight.

"Told you," Carlos murmured, glancing over at Leon's rigid posture. "Maybe she skipped today. Maybe she's at the rink."

"She doesn't skip," Leon said firmly, remembering the notes Rebecca had written down. "She is precise."

The digital clock on the wall shifted to eight fifty-nine.

At that exact second, a distinct sound echoed from the tiled hallway outside the lecture hall door. It was a click of heels striking the floor.

Leon's posture straightened even further.

A figure stepped through the threshold, stepping into the room precisely sixty seconds before the nine o'clock bell was scheduled to ring.

Ada walked down the concrete steps of the aisle with elegance that seemed to freeze the entire room around her. She was wearing tailored black trousers that hugged her tall frame perfectly, ending right at the ankles to reveal a pair of pointed-toe red heels that matched the striking shade of her top. The top was an off-shoulder batwing design in a rich crimson fabric, draping gracefully across her collarbones while leaving her delicate shoulders exposed. Her dark hair was styled into her signature sleek bob, and perched on the bridge of her nose were red-rimmed glasses.

Leon nearly forgot how to breathe. His eyes followed her as she glided down the steps, her leather messenger bag slung over one shoulder.

"See?" Carlos whispered beside him, leaning over with a sly grin. "Told you she wears pretty outfits."

"She looks..." Leon started, his voice cracking slightly before he quickly cleared his throat. "She looks incredible."

"Don't drool on your shirt, man," Carlos snickered. "She's coming down our row."

Ada didn't look at the students sitting around her. She walked with purpose, stepping into the middle row and taking her seat three chairs down from Carlos, right across the aisle beside the large glass window.

As she sat down, her eyes briefly flicked toward the warm paper cup sitting on the flip-up desk right beside her leather bag. Her dark eyes swept over the lid, noticing the lack of cream or sugar markings, before sliding over to Leon, who was offering her what he hoped was an easy, charming smile.

She didn't say thank you. She didn't touch the cup. She simply set her leather bag down, pulled out a slim leather notebook along with a fountain pen, and set them flat against the desk right beside the untouched coffee.

Leon's heart did a strange flutter, but he kept his composure, shifting his eyes back toward the front.

"She didn't reject it," Leon whispered triumphantly to Carlos. "That is a win."

"She didn't drink it either, man," Carlos whispered back, snickering into his palm.

Before Leon could argue, the double doors at the back of the hall swung open again, and Professor Mercer stepped into the room. Mercer was a tall, lean man in his late fifties with a messy beard, thick elbow patches on his tweed jacket, and a mountain of manila folders tucked under his arm. He marched straight to the podium, dumping the folders onto the wooden surface with a loud bang.

"Good morning, class," Professor Mercer announced, his deep voice booming through the acoustics of the amphitheater. "Settle down, settle down."

The chatter in the room quickly died out as students opened their laptops and readied their pens.

"Today," Mercer continued, pacing behind the podium, "we are diving directly into the complexities of international jurisdictional conflicts. Specifically, how state sovereignty interacts with extraterritorial criminal enforcement."

He turned to the whiteboard, picking up a black pen and writing three large words across the green surface: UNIVERSAL JURISDICTION / EXTRADITION

"Now," Mercer said, turning back around to face the rows of students. "Before we get into the dry statutes, let us examine a hypothetical scenario to see how your analytical minds function today."

Mercer walked around the front desk, resting his hands against the wooden edge as he surveyed the seating arrangement. His eyes drifted over the middle rows, passing over Carlos and the usual faces before stopping dead on Leon.

Mercer squinted through his glasses, staring at Leon’s clean denim jacket, styled blonde hair, and rigid posture.

"Well, well," Professor Mercer said, leaning forward slightly. "You are a fresh face in my lecture hall. I don't believe I have seen you in this section before, young man."

Leon blinked, pressing a hand against his chest. "Uh, yes, sir. I'm... auditing today."

Carlos instantly pulled his hoodie higher over his face, shaking with silent laughter.

"Auditing, are we?" Mercer said, an amused glint in his eye as he pointed a piece of chalk directly at Leon. "Excellent. A volunteer. Let us put your spontaneous academic enthusiasm to the test."

Leon cleared his throat, sitting up as straight as his varsity posture allowed. "Ready, Professor."

Across the aisle, Ada's pen paused for a fraction of a second against her leather notebook. She didn't turn her head, but her gaze shifted slightly toward the center of the row.

"Here is your scenario," Mercer began, pacing slowly across the front stage. "A foreign national operates a cyber-fraud network from a vessel anchored in international waters. The victims of this fraud are citizens residing in three different countries: France, Japan, and the United States. The vessel itself flies the flag of Panama. The operator is eventually apprehended by a private security firm hired by one of the victim corporations and brought to American soil."

Mercer stopped pacing, turning his full attention back to the fresh face in his room.

"The defense attorney moves to dismiss all charges, claiming the arrest was illegal under international law, and that the United States has no legal standing to prosecute a foreign national for actions committed on a Panamanian vessel in international waters. You, our surprise visitor, as the prosecuting counsel... what specific legal framework or doctrine do you utilize to justify keeping this defendant in custody and proceeding with the trial?"

The room went dead silent. Dozens of heads turned toward the middle row to see who the new guy was and how he was going to handle the complex question.

Carlos leaned over, whispering out of the side of his mouth. "You're dead, man."

Leon swallowed hard. His mind raced back to the napkin Rebecca had written for him, reminding him that Ada appreciated efficiency and intelligence. He couldn't fail here. He couldn't look like an uneducated athlete who had wandered into the wrong building. He needed to dig deep into his history major background and whatever criminology terminology he had skimmed the afternoon before.

Leon cleared his throat, leaning forward against his desk.

"Well, Professor," Leon began, "First, the defense's claim regarding the vessel's flag under Panama falls apart under the principle of passive personality jurisdiction. Since American citizens suffered direct financial harm from the cyber-fraud, the United States has a legitimate interest in prosecuting the offender to protect its nationals."

Professor Mercer raised an eyebrow, looking genuinely impressed by the fresh face. "Go on." Leon took a small breath, gaining confidence as the words began to flow. "Furthermore, regarding the private security firm making the apprehension in international waters, we can apply the Ker-Frisbie doctrine. Under American domestic law, the court's power to try a person for a crime is not impaired by the fact that the person was brought within the court's jurisdiction by reason of a forcible abduction, provided that no specific treaty terms were violated in a manner that creates a private right of action for the defendant."

A few murmurs echoed through the back rows. Carlos opened his eyes wide, looking at Leon like he had just witnessed a miracle.

Professor Mercer smiled slightly, nodding his head. "An exceptional response for someone just auditing the course. Utilizing the Ker-Frisbie precedent alongside the passive personality principle is a sharp prosecutorial approach."

Leon felt a surge of pride bloom in his chest. He casually glanced across the aisle toward Ada, hoping to catch her eye, hoping to see a hint of approval or interest on her face, maybe even see her pick up the black coffee he had bought for her.

Professor Mercer turned his eyes toward the window row. "However... let us see if your argument holds up under a sharper legal lens. Miss Wong."

Ada lifted her head calmly, her dark eyes looking down at the professor through her red- framed glasses.

"How would you disassemble our guest's prosecutorial defense?" Mercer asked, tapping his chalk against his palm. "Where are the flaws in his reliance on passive personality and the Ker-Frisbie doctrine?"

Leon held his breath, turning his body slightly to face her direction. This was it. This was their first real moment. She was going to critique his answer, he was going to offer a clever rebuttal, and they would share an intellectual moment in the middle of a Tuesday lecture.

Ada sat in silence for a long moment.

She looked across the aisle, her gaze briefly crossing over Leon's expectant face. Her eyes lingered for a fraction of a second on the warm paper cup sitting untouched on her desk.

Then, without saying a word, Ada slowly reached out, closed her leather notebook, and slid her fountain pen back into the side pocket of her messenger bag. She picked up her bag, leaving the black coffee sitting alone on the wooden armrest.

She stood up smoothly from her chair, the red heels clicking softly against the floor as she stepped out into the aisle.

"Professor Mercer," Ada said, her voice smooth and low as she addressed the front of the room. "Please excuse me. I need to step out."

Professor Mercer blinked, looking thrown off by the sudden interruption from one of his top students. "Miss Wong? We are in the middle of a discussion."

"I am aware," Ada simply replied, "I will review the lecture slides later."

She didn't wait for permission.

She swung her leather bag over her shoulder, turned around, and walked up the concrete steps of the central aisle. Her heels struck each step with a crisp, clear sound that echoed off the high ceiling of the amphitheater.

The double doors at the top of the room opened, and she disappeared into the bright hallway outside, the doors closing behind her with a dull thud.

The lecture hall remained completely silent for several seconds.

Professor Mercer cleared his throat, adjusting his tweed jacket. "Well... moving on to page forty-two of the syllabus..."

Leon sat frozen in his seat, his eyes fixed on the empty double doors at the top of the aisle. The bright, confident energy that had been filling his chest just seconds ago vanished completely, replaced by a sinking feeling in the pit of his stomach.

The cup of black coffee he had spent his morning securing sat across the aisle on her empty desk, untouched, growing colder by the minute.

Carlos looked over at Leon, his teasing smile completely gone, replaced by a look of genuine sympathy. He reached over, patting Leon's shoulder gently. "Hey... man. Don't worry about it. She's just... like that, you know? She leaves early all the time when she's stressed."

Leon didn't answer right away. He lowered his gaze to the untouched paper cup sitting on the empty desk across the aisle.

"She didn't even take a sip," Leon murmured softly, his voice trailing off into the quiet hum of the air conditioner. "She just left."

"She's just busy, man," Carlos offered, trying his best to cheer him up. "It wasn't about you or the coffee."

Leon let out a small, tired breath, leaning his back against the hard wooden chair. He looked toward the window where the morning sun was streaming through, casting a warm light over the empty desk where she had been sitting just moments before.

He pulled the folded napkin out of his denim jacket pocket, staring at Rebecca's neat handwriting outlining Ada's daily schedule.

"Third floor library," Leon whispered to himself, a small, stubborn flicker of determination trying to fight its way through the disappointment in his chest. "History section."

He carefully refolded the napkin, tucked it back into his pocket, and picked up his own iced latte, taking a slow sip as Professor Mercer continued his lecture at the front of the room.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

"Woah, woah, hold on, quarterback!" Carlos shouted, scrambling to shove his laptop into his bag. He swung one strap over his shoulder, almost tripping over his own desk as Leon bolted into the central aisle. "Leon! Wait a second! Where are you going?!"

Leon did not slow down. He took the concrete steps two at a time, his sneakers squeaking sharply against the floor. "Library!" he threw over his shoulder, his voice echoing off the high ceiling.

"The library isn't going anywhere, man!" Carlos yelled, bursting out of the lecture hall doors right behind him into the crowded corridor. Students were milling around, heading to their ten o'clock classes, creating a maze of moving bodies. "You don't even know if she went straight there! She left class twenty minutes ago!"

"She follows the schedule, Carlos!" Leon called back, weaving through a group of communications majors standing near the water fountain. "Nine-thirty lab, ten o'clock library!"

"It's on the complete opposite wing of the campus, you lunatic!" Carlos shouted, trying to keep pace with Leon's long athletic strides. His bag bounced uselessly against his hip. "You're going to pull a hamstring running through the humanities courtyard in denim! Slow down!"

"I don't care about my hamstring!" Leon yelled, taking a left turn toward the glass exit doors that led to the central breezeway. "I have to get to the third floor before she picks her desk!"

"Leon! She isn't a rare pokemon that vanishes if you don't throw a ball at her!" Carlos gasped, slowing his jog down to a fast walk as he reached the glass doors. He wiped a bead of sweat from his forehead, leaning his hand against the doorframe while watching Leon sprint across the open courtyard. "You are completely crazy, Kennedy! Completely crazy!"

Leon didn't hear him.

The cool morning air rushed past his face as he bolted down the concrete pathway, his denim jacket flapping slightly around his waist. He cut across the manicured grass, completely ignoring the sign that warned students to stay on the designated walkways. His mind was hyper-focused on one single destination.

The social sciences and humanities library, located in the east wing of the campus, nearly three full quad lengths away.

He pushed through the double glass doors of the library complex, breathing heavily as the blast of air conditioning hit his warm face. He ignored the elevator, taking the staircase three steps at a time until he reached the third floor.

The third floor was dedicated entirely to history, international relations, and political science. It was a sprawling space filled with endless rows of wooden bookshelves, carpeted aisles, and large floor-to-ceiling windows that overlooked the pine trees lining the eastern edge of the university grounds.

Leon slowed his pace to a careful walk, trying to regulate his breathing so he wouldn't sound like a winded marathon runner. He pulled the folded napkin from his jacket pocket, double- checking the neat handwriting Rebecca had scribbled earlier that morning.

Third floor. Corner desk near the European history section. By the glass window.

Leon navigated the narrow aisles, his eyes scanning every wooden study cubicle, every table, and every cushioned chair nestled between the tall bookshelves. He passed three students buried under mountains of textbooks, a freshman sleeping peacefully on an open notebook, and an elderly professor searching through archived journals.

He reached the corner desk near the European history section.

It was completely empty.

Leon stood there for a moment, his hands resting on his hips as he looked around the surrounding area. The wooden desk was clear, its polished surface reflecting the bright sunlight streaming through the tall glass window.

But there was no sight of Ada.

"Okay," Leon whispered to himself, reassuring his pounding heart. "Okay. She's just running late. Lab check-in probably took longer than usual."

He pulled out the chair directly across the aisle, sitting down to wait. He crossed his ankles, leaning his back against the wooden seat, and fixed his gaze on the main walkway leading into the section.

Ten minutes passed. Then twenty.

Leon adjusted his posture, resting his chin in his palm. The distant ticking of a wall clock somewhere near the reference desk was the only sound accompanying his thoughts. Students walked past occasionally, carrying stacks of research papers, but none of them were Ada.

An hour slipped by. Then two.

By eleven-thirty, Leon had shifted positions at least fifteen times. He had counted the number of pine trees visible through the glass window, memorized the titles of the books on the nearest shelf, and checked his phone roughly forty times.

His stomach let out a low, complaining rumble, reminding him that he had consumed nothing all morning except a few sips of an iced latte. Yet, he refused to stand up. He refused to leave the seat. If he left now to get lunch, he reasoned, that would be the exact five-minute window when she walked in.

At twelve fifteen, his phone buzzed violently against his thigh. He pulled it out quickly, his heart leaping for a fraction of a second thinking it might somehow be a message related to her, but the screen displayed a flood of notification banners from his messaging app.

It was his group chat.

The group chat notification header read.

The Real MVP's (And Leon)

Leon unlocked his phone, tapping into the chat thread.

Chris: Hey, lover boy. Are you still alive? Carlos said you sprinted out of the lecture hall like the building was on fire.

Jill: He probably got lost in the library. History majors are notoriously bad at navigation.

Leon: I am at the third floor desk. Waiting.

Chris: Waiting? It's been over two hours, man. Did she even show up?

Leon: She's busy...but she'll be here :)

Before Leon could type another response, the screen lit up with a fresh notification as a video file dropped directly into the group chat, sent by Claire.

Claire: Look who we just found!

Leon tapped the video thumbnail instantly, his thumb practically smudging the glass screen.

The video was taken from the upper bleachers of the university ice rink. The icy surface gleamed under the bright stadium lights, reflecting the clear white of the rink floor. In the center of the ice, a single figure was gliding across the surface like she's defying gravity.

It was Ada.

She was wearing a sleek black practice suit that hugged her form and her dark hair pulled back neatly. In the video, she leaned deep into an edge, launched herself into the air, then spun three complete rotations in a blur of motion, and landed cleanly on a single blade, gliding backward with grace.

Leon's breath hitched in his throat. He watched the five-second clip four times in a row without blinking.

Claire: She is so pretty, it's actually ridiculous.

Claire: Like, how does someone look that good while landing a triple loop? I look like a wet rabbit when I run on a treadmill.

Another video file dropped into the chat right beneath Claire's message, this one sent by Helena.

Helena: @Jill come get your girlfriend. She is fangirling so hard right now it's getting embarrassing.

Leon tapped Helena's video attachment. Instead of the ice rink, the short clip showed Claire sitting in the bleachers, resting her chin on her hands with a giant, giddy smile stretching across her face, completely mesmerized as she cheered for Ada's practice run.

Jill: @Helena Hey, let her be! But also, I can't even blame her. Ada really does look so pretty out there. It's almost irritating.

Jill: The open practice is packed today, by the way. Heard half the figure skating team is here watching her.

Leon stared at his phone screen, his thumb hovering over the keyboard as mixed feeling settled in his chest.

Chris: So... @Leon what's your next move? You still sitting in the history section like a ghost?

Claire: Yeah, Leon, what are you doing? Are you coming down to the rink? xD

Helena: Please don't come down here in your varsity jacket and stare at her from the glass barrier like a stalker. She will throw an ice skate at your head.

Carlos: Prayers + you owe me 20.

Leon squeezed his phone tightly in his hand, feeling a surge of self-consciousness. He looked around the quiet, empty library section. The polished wooden desk across the aisle remained unoccupied, sunlight still washing over the empty chair.

He started typing, his fingers moving slowly across the screen.

Leon: I thought she had lab check-in at nine-thirty.

Jill: @Leon listen to me, Bud

Jill: You need to breathe if you really like her.

Jill: You are overthinking every single second of her day. She is a top-tier athlete and an honors student. Her schedule changes when her training changes. You can't track her like a football playbook.

Chris: Jill is right, man. You're going to give yourself an ulcer before you even get her phone number.

Claire: [Like]

Leon sat back in his seat, reading Jill's message over and over. 'You need to breathe if you really like her.'

He let out a long, slow breath, closing his eyes for a moment. He reached into his breast pocket, his fingers brushing against the texture of the folded napkin. He didn't pull it out this time. He just let his hand rest there, feeling the simple paper between his fingertips.

He unlocked his phone again, typing a brief message back to the group.

Leon: I'm getting lunch.

Claire: [Like]

Chris: Meet us at the dining hall near the complex. I'll buy you a sandwich.

Jill, Carlos, Claire, Helena seen.

Carlos like.

Leon tucked his phone back into his pocket, standing up slowly from the wooden chair. His knees gave a small crack after hours of remaining still.

He looked one last time at the corner desk by the glass window before he turned his heels. He didn't feel angry. He didn't even feel fully defeated anymore. If he wanted to step into Ada's world, he couldn't just chase her down hallways or sit in corners waiting for her to appear.

He turned away from the window, walking down the carpeted aisle toward the staircase.

As he pushed open the glass doors of the library and stepped back out into the bright, warm afternoon sun, Leon adjusted the collar of his denim jacket, running a hand through his blonde hair. He pulled his phone from his pocket, opened the video Claire had sent one more time, and watched the dark-haired girl glide effortlessly across the ice.

A small smile touched the corner of his lips.

"Phase two needs a rewrite," Leon muttered to himself, stepping onto the concrete path toward the dining hall. "A total rewrite."

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

Leon sat in the center of a corner booth, his broad shoulders slouched slightly against the vinyl cushion. In front of him sat a half-eaten turkey club sandwich and a tall red cup of iced water, though he was mostly busy staring into space, his thumb mindlessly tracing the rim of his cup.

Across the table, Chris was currently defending a massive plastic platter loaded with golden, seasoned waffle fries. On Chris’s left sat Jill, resting her chin in her hand while her free fingers hovered dangerously close to Chris’s food. Claire sat right beside Jill, her arm casually draped over the back of Jill's chair, while Carlos occupied the far end of the booth, scrolling through his phone with an amused smirk.

"I am just saying," Chris grunted, swatting Jill’s hand away from his plate for the third time in two minutes, "you need to stop treating this whole thing like a military operation, Leon. You ran across three quads, sat in an empty room for hours, and now you look like someone stole your favorite football."

"I did not look like someone stole my football," Leon grumbled, finally lifting his head. "I was reflecting. Strategic reflection."

"Strategic obsession is more like it," Chris countered, taking a bite of his burger. "Take things slow, man. You have been tracking her for less than thirty-six hours after you first laid your eyes on her. You are going to give yourself a heart attack before you even figure out her favorite color."

"I agree with Redfield," Carlos interjected from the end of the table, not looking up from his screen. "Calm down, quarterback. You're moving at a hundred miles an hour. Ada operates on her own frequency. If you crowd her space, she's just going to step right over you. Again."

Leon flinched slightly at the memory of the red pointed heels clicking up the lecture hall steps. "She's just busy..."

Jill snickered softly. Before Chris could react, her hand darted across the table with lightning speed, snatching a handful of crisp, golden fries directly off his platter. She popped two into her mouth with a triumphant grin, completely ignoring Chris’s outraged gasp.

"Hey! Those are my fries!" Chris protested, pulling the platter closer to his chest. "Get your own lunch!"

"You weren't eating them fast enough," Jill replied smoothly, wiping a speck of salt from her lip. She turned her sharp gaze back toward Leon, leaning forward against the wooden table. "Leon, answer me honestly. Do you actually like Ada? Like, her actual personality? Or are you just obsessed with the fact that she's completely out of your reach?"

Claire chuckled beside her, resting her head against Jill's shoulder. "Careful, Babe. Don't break his fragile heart."

Jill reached up, running her fingers softly through Claire’s hair without taking her eyes off Leon. "I'm serious. Because right now, you're acting like a guy who just wants to win a game. Ada isn't a trophy at the end of a field. She's intimidating, she's intensely smart, and she will crush your ego into dust if you approach her just because you are fixated."

Leon sighed, leaning his elbows on the table and rubbing his face with both hands. "I know she isn't a game, Jill. I know she's smart. Everything about her screams beauty and brains."

"She kind of does," Claire offered with a small smile. "She took top honors at the regional figure skating qualifiers last year while maintaining a four point zero GPA."

"See?" Leon pointed a finger at Claire. "Thank you. She is incredible. And I am just trying to get a foot in the door."

"By bringing her black coffee she didn't drink?" Chris teased, taking a fry from his now guarded pile. "Smooth move, lover boy."

"She didn't reject it either," Leon insisted, though his voice lacked its morning confidence. "She left it on her desk. That means it was an open variable."

"It means it got cold," Jill corrected dryly, stealing another fry right from Chris's fingers before he could get it to his mouth.

"Jill! Stop it!" Chris barked, moving his entire plate onto his lap. "Claire, control your girlfriend!"

Claire laughed out loud, pressing a sweet, quick kiss to Jill’s cheek. "She's an elite athlete, Chris. She needs the carbohydrates."

"She has her own salad sitting right there!" Chris whined, pointing at Jill’s untouched green bowl.

Before the argument could escalate any further, a shadow fell over the edge of their booth. A tall guy wearing a faded grey hoodie, worn jeans, and a slightly tired expression stepped up to the table, holding a stack of neon green flyers in his hand.

It was Ethan. Ethan Winters.

And Ethan looked like he had been running errands across campus all morning, his blonde hair messy and a cup of lukewarm convenience store coffee clutched in his left hand.

"Hey, guys," Ethan said dryly. He slapped a green flyer onto the middle of the table right on top of Chris’s discarded napkin.

Chris squinted down at the paper. "What's this?"

"Party tonight," Ethan announced, pulling a second flyer from his stack. "It's over at the Sigma Phi house. Mia told me their president cleared it with the student council yesterday. It's a joint mixer."

Chris instantly groaned, dropping his head back against the vinyl booth frame. "Oh, man. Absolutely not. I am not going. I am still not over the total disaster from two weeks ago."

"What happened two weeks ago?" Carlos asked, looking up from his phone with genuine curiosity.

"Chris tried to do a cannonball into their backyard plastic pool," Claire explained with a grin, "and ended up breaking their wooden porch railing. We had to drag him out before their house treasurer called campus security."

"The railing was already loose!" Chris shouted defensively, throwing his hands up. "It was a hazard!"

"You were built like a tank and jumped off a cooler, Redfield," Jill pointed out smoothly, leaning back in her seat. "The railing didn't stand a chance."

Ethan chuckled, shaking his head as he looked around the booth. "Look, all I know is Mia said everyone is showing up. Half the soccer team, the football guys, and a bunch of people from the science departments because of the honors college invite."

Leon looked down at the bright green flyer, his finger tracing the bold black ink. He didn't jump up like he would usually do. He didn't lean forward with bright, eager eyes either. Instead, a small, doubtful line formed between his brows.

"I don't know, Ethan," Leon said softly, slumping back against the vinyl seat. "I doubt she's even going to be there."

Ethan paused, holding his stack of flyers mid-air. He looked at Leon, then over at Chris, his face a complete blank slate. "Wait... who? Who are you talking about?"

"Ada Wong," Carlos supplied instantly with a wide grin.

"Ada... Wong?" Ethan repeated, his brow furrowing as he tried to connect the dots. A spark of curiosity lit up his tired eyes. Ethan was usually the quiet guy in the house, but fresh fraternity gossip was a rare commodity. "Wait, hold on. Kennedy is moping over the Ada Wong?"

"He's been moping for some time now, yes..." Jill said.

"Hold that thought. Do not say another word," Ethan ordered, setting his stack of flyers down on the edge of the booth. He whipped his phone out of his hoodie pocket, tapped the screen frantically, and pulled up a contact.
