"Daddy!" Ada screamed, her small voice shattering into a desperate, terrified sob that echoed off the cold bedroom walls. She grabbed his broad lapels, shaking his massive, unmoving frame with all the strength in her body. "Daddy, wake up! Please! Wake up! Don't do this! Daddy!"

She slammed her fists against his chest, tears spilling over her eyelashes, soaking into the cotton of his pajamas. She pulled at his cold hands, trying desperately to drag his massive weight up from the mattress, her small voice breaking into high-pitched, helpless shrieks that died unanswered in the vast, empty house.

"Please!" she sobbed, burying her face into his cold neck, her hands clutching his shirt as her body shook violently. "Please wake up! I'll be perfect! I'll hold the edge! Just wake up!"

The emergency room waiting area of the county hospital smelled of harsh industrial bleach, rubbing alcohol, and cheap floor cleaner. The bright fluorescent light strips buzzing high overhead cast a sickly, yellow glare over rows of hard blue plastic chairs.

Ada sat frozen on the middle chair of the back row.

She was still wearing her light blue baby doll dress, the hem rumpled and damp with her own dried tears. She had no footwear. Her small, bare feet were tucked up onto the plastic rung of the chair, her toes blue from the air-conditioned chill of the building, her skin pale and covered in a fine layer of dust from the hospital floor. Her red-framed glasses sat crooked on her nose, her dark hair a messy, unbrushed tangles around her face.

She didn't speak. She didn't cry anymore. She simply sat staring with wide, unblinking dark eyes at the swinging double doors leading to the trauma bays.

A few feet away, near the glass reception desk, two triage nurses in light blue scrubs were filling out administrative charts, speaking in hushed whispers that carried easily across the quiet waiting area.

"Was that the girl on the news last year?" the older nurse asked, clicking her pen against her clipboard.

"Yeah," the younger nurse replied softly, looking over her shoulder with a sad, pitying expression at the silent girl in the blue dress. "The paramedics brought her in an hour ago. She called nine-one-one herself, but he was already gone when they arrived."

"What was the cause? He was only in his early fifties, wasn't he?"

"Sudden arrhythmic death syndrome," the younger nurse whispered, shaking her head. "The doctor said his heart just stopped beating in his sleep. No warning, no underlying condition. Just cardiac arrest while he was resting."

"That's terrible," the older nurse sighed softly. "A man that healthy... it doesn't make any sense."

"The paramedic who worked the scene said something strange," the younger nurse added, lowering her voice even further. "He looked at the house, saw all the single-parent records, and said it almost looked like his heart just gave out from years of holding everything together alone. Like it broke from the inside until it just stopped."

"Heartbreak," the older nurse murmured sadly, looking at her chart. "The body can only carry so much strain before the wire snaps."

Sitting on the cold blue plastic chair, Ada heard every single word.

The words hovered in the sterile air around her head. Heartbreak. Cardiac arrest. Gone.

A suffocating wave of darkness seemed to collapse over her mind, icy and unrelenting. The world around her narrowed until the bright hospital lights, the white noise of the machines, and the hushed voices of the nurses dissolved into a distant, meaningless hum.

Slowly, deliberately, Ada pulled her knees straight up against her chest.

She wrapped her slender arms tightly around her shins, tucking her bare, freezing feet underneath the hem of her light blue dress. She lowered her head, burying her face completely into her knees, hiding her eyes, her pale cheeks, and her trembling lips from the sterile room.

She curled up into the exact same tight, defensive ball she had assumed on the carpeted floor the night her mother packed her bags and walked out into the rain.

The protective walls she had built over the years evaporated into thin air. The gold medals, the applause, the applause of the stadium crowds—none of it mattered. None of it could bring back the hand that wrapped her ankle, the voice that corrected her posture, or the man who held her ice cream cone in the dark parking lot.

Ada sat alone in the cold hospital light, a small, helpless child left once again in the dark, clutching her knees while the vast, empty world spun mercilessly on without him.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

The funeral home's primary parlor was lined with modest bouquets of white lilies, soft lighting, and rows of dark wooden chairs that remained mostly unoccupied. The private viewing was kept small, dignified, and closed off from the eager glare of the regional sports media, a protective boundary organized entirely by the Graham family.

Ada stood near the side of the polished mahogany casket, her posture perfectly erect, her hands clasped loosely in front of her dark velvet dress. Her face was an unreadable mask, her dark hair neatly styled into her signature bob, her red-framed glasses catching the faint glow of the tall wax candles flickering nearby. She didn't cry. She didn't tremble. She simply stood there, staring at Wesker's pale, motionless face resting against the white satin lining.

The oak doors at the back of the parlor opened with a faint, whispering sound against the carpet.

A woman walked into the room, her designer leather heels clicking with sharp precision against the floor tiles near the entryway. Eleonor wore a sleek black trench coat, dark silk gloves, and expensive sunglasses pushed up onto her styled hair. She looked untamed by time. Eleonor didn't rush forward. She didn't break down into weeping, nor did she approach the casket with open arms. She walked to the edge of the wooden railing, looked down at her former husband's face for a brief moment.

She turned her dark eyes onto Ada, inspecting her daughter with the same analytical scrutiny she might apply to an unfamiliar specimen.

"He looks smaller than I remembered," Eleonor said unbothered by the sacred atmosphere of the room.

Ada didn't turn her head. She kept her eyes fixed on Wesker's still face. "He was five inches taller than you."

"A physical detail," Eleonor replied dismissively, reaching into her leather handbag. She pulled out a small, textured white card printed with dark silver lettering. She extended her hand, holding the paper between two manicured fingers. "My personal research office in Zurich. If you require legal signatures for your inheritance trust or academic guardianship transfers, my administrative team will handle the filings."

Ada didn't reach out to take it.

Eleonor didn't wait for her to accept it either. She simply set the crisp calling card down on the corner of the mahogany casket, right beside a single white lily, and smoothed down the lapels of her black coat.

"Your figure skating records were published in the international sports journal last month," Eleonor added, "He certainly succeeded in constructing his ideal athletic project."

"He was my father," Ada said, her voice dropping into a sharp register.

"He was a man obsessed with control," Eleonor corrected smoothly, turning her back on the casket without a single backward glance. "Do not confuse discipline with affection, Ada. I have a flight to catch."

She walked out of the parlor, her heels clicking briskly against the floor until the oak doors closed behind her, leaving the room once again in absolute stillness.

Wesker was buried two days later on a cool, overcast Tuesday morning.

The Graham family handled every administrative detail with flawless, high-society discretion. Ashley’s father, the former governor, personally ensured that no press photographers or university representatives breached the perimeter of the private cemetery plot on the hill. Only four people stood near the grave as the casket was lowered into the dark earth. The minister, Mr. Graham, Ashley, and Ada.

Ashley stood beside Ada the entire time, her small hand reaching out to hold Ada's cold fingers, tears streaming down her pale cheeks as she squeezed Ada's hand with loving strength. Ada didn't squeeze back. She stood like a marble statue, watching the polished wood disappear beneath the surface, her jaw set in a tight, unbroken line.

The moment the burial concluded, Ada turned on her heel and walked down the winding stone path toward the waiting sedan. She didn't look back at the mound of fresh earth, nor did she linger for the formal reception at the Graham estate.

The very next morning, at four o'clock sharp, Ada’s alarm clock sounded in her dark bedroom.

She didn't stay in bed. She didn't stare at the ceiling. She swung her legs over the edge of the mattress, stood up, and smoothed down her dark flannel pajamas.

She walked down the stairs to the immaculate kitchen. The space was freezing, but Ada didn't adjust the thermostat. She pulled out a small cutting board, sliced two pieces of whole-wheat bread, fried two eggs in a cast-iron skillet with precise, practiced movements, and poured a single cup of green tea. She ate her meal sitting at the high wooden island, her eyes fixed on the empty spot where Wesker's dark coffee mug usually rested.

When she finished, she washed her plate, her fork, and her pan with hot, soapy water, dried them with a clean towel, and placed them in parallel alignment on the drying rack.

She changed into her dark thermal gear, laced up her athletic sneakers, and walked three miles to the local transit station, catching the four-forty-five morning bus into the city center.

Her daily routine resumed with a terrifying velocity.

Six hours of academic honors classes, followed by two hours of elite gymnastics conditioning, three hours at the ballet barre, and two hours of solo ice practice every single evening. Without Wesker standing at the barrier with his stopwatch, Ada became her own merciless taskmaster. She timed her own laps, she set her own edge angles, and she pushed her body past the point of exhaustion until her muscles trembled violently under her clothes.

She didn't skip a single drill. She didn't alter her dietary intake by a single calorie. She moved through her days like a clockwork automaton, entirely closed off from the world around her.

Two weeks after the funeral, the main ice facility was freezing and dark at seven in the evening.

Ada was on the ice alone, drilling her triple lutz entrance over and over. Her dark hair was damp with sweat, her grey thermal shirt soaked through, her chest rising and falling in rapid, shallow gasps as she brought herself to a sudden, spraying stop near the center red line.

Her ankle throbbed with a deep, persistent ache, her left knee swollen under her tights from an awkward landing twenty minutes earlier, but she turned on her blade, preparing to launch into another pass.

"Ada! Stop!"

The loud call echoed across the wide, empty arena from the wooden access gate.

Ashley was sprinting across the rubber matting, her yellow cheer jacket unzipped, her blonde hair messy as she pushed open the wooden door and stepped onto the ice in her shoes. She slid slightly on the frozen surface, balancing herself with wild arm movements as she marched straight toward the center of the rink.

"Ashley," Ada noted as she adjusted her red-framed glasses. "You are wearing shoes on pristine ice. You are damaging the surface."

"I don't care about the ice!" Ashley yelled, her blue eyes wide, red-rimmed, and brimming with furious, worried tears. She stopped two feet away from Ada, her chest heaving as she pointed a trembling finger at Ada's pale, exhausted face. "Look at you! You've been here for four hours! Your coach at the ballet academy called my dad today because you fainted during the pointe exercise!"

"I did not faint," Ada corrected smoothly, her posture remaining perfectly straight. "I experienced a minor drop in blood pressure due to miscalculated caloric timing. It has been corrected."

"You are starving yourself and working until your legs give out!" Ashley cried, her voice cracking in desperation. She reached out, her hands catching Ada's cold forearms, pulling on her dark thermal sleeves. "Ada, please! Look at me! You haven't talked to me in two weeks! You don't answer my texts, you don't let me come over to your house, and you're killing yourself on this ice!"

Ada gently, firmly, pulled her forearms out of Ashley's grip, stepping back one full foot onto a clean edge. "My schedule is structured, Ashley. I have regional qualifiers in twenty days. My preparation requires focus."

"This isn't preparation! This is torture!" Ashley screamed, tears finally spilling over her eyelashes, tracking down her flushed cheeks. "You're trying to act like nothing happened! You're trying to act like your dad didn't just pass away!"

The temperature in the arena seemed to drop ten degrees instantly.

Ada’s dark eyes narrowed into two razor-sharp slits behind her red-framed glasses, her expression turning so cold that Ashley instinctively took a half-step backward on her slippery shoes.

"Do not speak about my father," Ada commanded.

"I have to speak about him!" Ashley sobbed, taking a brave step forward, her hands clenching into tight fists at her sides as she looked into Ada's icy face. "Because I loved him too! Because he cared about you! And I know Mr. Wesker wouldn't want this for you, Ada! He wouldn't want you destroying your body and pushing everyone away like this! He might be strict, and he might have pushed you to your limits, but at the end of the day, he was there! He took care of you! He loved you!"

Something inside Ada's meticulously controlled composure snapped with violent, irreversible force.

"You know nothing about my father!" Ada screamed, her voice exploding through the arena like a thunderclap, echoing wildly off the high steel rafters.

Her face turned a bright, furious red, her eyes wide, wild, and dark with raw fury that Ashley had never seen in her entire life. She stepped directly into Ashley's personal space, towering over her, her chest heaving.

"You know nothing about what he wanted!" Ada yelled, her voice breaking with an ugly, bitter strain as tears of pure rage welled up in her dark eyes. "You know nothing about his rules! You know nothing about what it takes to survive in this life! You are just a spoiled, pampered cheerleader who thinks she can fix everything with sweet words and glittery signs!"

Ashley froze completely, her jaw dropping open, her breath catching in her throat as if she had been struck hard across the face. "Ada... I..."

"Get out," Ada commanded, her voice dropping into a raspy whisper that cut deeper than any scream. "Get out of my sight, Ashley. Leave me alone."

Without waiting for a response, without looking back at the crying girl standing frozen on the cold ice, Ada turned on her blades, glided swiftly toward the exit gate, stepped onto the rubber matting, and marched down the dark corridor toward the changing rooms, her footsteps echoing into the darkness of the facility.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

Ada sat on the edge of her narrow bed , her dark thermal shirt damp with sweat, her hands resting flat on her knees. Her pulse hammered against her throat, hot and uneven, echoing the bitter, poisonous words she had spat at Ashley in the arena less than an hour ago.

'You know nothing about my father.'

The words burned in her mouth like acid. She stared at the dark hardwood floorboards of her room, her chest rising and falling in shallow cycles. Her mind was a roaring fire of frustration, fatigue, and an agonizing, wordless hurt that refused to be disciplined by stopwatch metrics or precise edge holds.

She stood up abruptly, her wooden chair scraping violently against the floorboards.

She marched out of her bedroom, her bare feet slapping hard against the corridor floor as she stomped her way down the hallway toward the master suite. Her posture was no longer regal or controlled; it was aggressive and jagged with rage.

She reached the mahogany door of Wesker's room, shoved it open with a crashing thud, and stepped into the pitch-black space.

"Where are you?!" Ada screamed into the dark, empty room, her voice cracking with raw, unvarnished fury.

She reached out, grabbing a heavy brass desk lamp off the side table, lifting it high with the intent to smash it against the far wall, to tear the curtains down, to shatter every framed diploma and rip the sheets off his unmade bed. She wanted to create a ruin that matched the ruined cavern inside her own chest.

She wanted to destroy everything.

Yet, as her fingers tightened around the cool metal of the lamp base, her arm froze in midair. A deep, instinctual conditioning locked her muscles in place. Even in the peak of her agony, she could not bring herself to throw the lamp. She could not break the rules he had built into her foundation.

She slammed the lamp back down onto the nightstand with a dull clatter, her hands clutching the edge of the dark wood as a high-pitched, desperate shriek tore from her throat.

"How could you leave me?!" Ada screamed at the empty air, her voice echoing off the bare walls, tears finally bursting from her eyes in hot, scalding streams. "How could you be so selfish?! You spent sixteen years forcing me to be perfect! You made me skate! You made me run! You made me memorize every law, every bone, every movement!"

She stomped her foot against the floor, her chest heaving as she paced frantically around the perimeter of his king-sized bed.

"You built me into a machine!" she sobbed, her voice breaking into raspy, desperate gasps as she kicked the wooden frame of the bed. "And then you just die in your sleep?! You just leave me here alone in this stupid, empty house?! You told me to never be scared to take a leap! How am I supposed to leap when you aren't holding the barrier?!"

She swung around to stomp toward the closet, her mind consumed by the blinding storm of her own grief, but her right pant leg caught sharply on a small, hidden metal hook protruding from the dark wooden baseboard beneath the nightstand.

"Ah!" Ada gasped, her balance throwing her forward as the dark fabric snagged tight.

She stumbled, her knees hitting the rug hard. Breathless, her chest aching with desperate sobs, she bent down to untangle the hem of her dark training pants from the metal latch.

Her fingers brushed against a small, concealed sliding latch mounted to the lower frame of the bed. The hook was part of a hidden mechanism.

When her pant leg pulled the latch, a soft metallic click sounded beneath the wood, and a large, flat compartment hidden under the bed frame popped open by two inches.

Ada went completely still. Her breathing caught in her throat, her wet, red-rimmed dark eyes widening behind her glasses as she stared at the dark opening beneath the mattress.

Slowly, her hands trembling with a sudden, icy uncertainty, she reached out and pulled the sliding drawer all the way forward.

The compartment was lined with soft black velvet, completely protected from dust and light. Inside sat a massive, meticulously organized archive that occupied the entire length of the bed.

On the left side of the drawer lay twelve thick, leather-bound photo albums. On the right side stood seventeen thick, matching black leather journals, each one stamped with gold foil numbers on the spine ranging from one to seventeen.

Ada reached out her trembling hand, her fingers gently touching the smooth leather cover of the first photo album.

She pulled it onto her lap, sitting cross-legged on the cold rug beneath the bed frame, and opened the front cover.

The first page contained a crisp, high-definition photograph of her as an infant, wrapped in a pink blanket, sleeping soundly in Wesker's broad arms while they sat in a quiet hospital room. Beneath the photograph, written in Wesker's precise, neat block handwriting, was a single caption.

Ada. Day One. Weight: 6 lbs, 4 oz.

Ada swallowed a hard lump in her throat, her fingers tracing the ink as a fresh tear spilled onto the plastic sleeve.

She turned the pages slowly. Every single event of her life was documented with terrifying, loving precision.

Photographs from her first steps on the ice at four years old, featuring close-ups of her tiny white skates. High-resolution prints from every single regional competition, every gymnastics meet, every ballet recital, and every school journalism broadcast. There were candid photos she had never known he took. Photos of her sleeping in the passenger seat of his car with her head resting against the door, photos of her eating her fast-food meals at the kitchen island, and photos of her laughing with Ashley near the bleachers.

Beside every single photograph were detailed notes written in his handwriting, documenting not her mistakes or her deductions, but her accomplishments, her smiles, and her small victories.

Ada set the album aside with trembling hands and reached for the seventeenth black journal, the one marked with the current year's date.

She opened the thick volume to the very first page.

The journal was not a coaching log. It was a daily record of his thoughts, his plans, and his unconditional love for his daughter, written every single night after she went to sleep.

Ada’s dark eyes scanned the neat, black ink lines.

Entry 4,112. March 12.

Ada executed her triple lutz sequence today with exceptional form. She complained of tiredness, but her determination remains unshakeable. I prepared her preferred chicken dish for dinner. She ate well. Watching her grow into such a resilient, intelligent young woman is the single greatest achievement of my life.

Ada let out a soft, choked whimper, pressing her palm against her mouth as she turned the pages frantically, her eyes devouring his words.

The journal contained meticulous reading materials and clipped articles from academic medical journals detailing pediatric nutrition, adolescent bone development, psychological resilience in elite performers, and comprehensive university educational plans. There were hand-drawn diagrams of her meal plans, calculated down to the last gram of protein, designed specifically to protect her joints from long-term athletic wear.

Ada turned to the final pages of the file folder resting behind the seventeenth journal.

Inside was a formal blue document labeled.

Future Directives and Long-Term Security for Ada.

Ada traced her fingers over the third paragraph, where Wesker had written out his thoughts regarding her adult life.

'Ada’s academic trajectory guarantees her acceptance into any international university she selects. Her trust fund is fully secured and legally insulated from external claims.

Regarding her personal future: Ada possesses a sharp mind and a guarded heart, but she feels affection deeply. Should she choose to marry in the future, her partner must demonstrate absolute devotion, unyielding patience, and total respect for her independence.'

The next lines were written with a darker, firmer press of his pen.

'If her future partner ever fails to take care of her, if they ever causes her intentional distress, or if they proves incapable of protecting her happiness, I will personally intervene. I will take my daughter and my grandchildren away from them without a single moment of hesitation. No individual will ever diminish her value while I draw breath.'

'Ada is my heart. She is the only reason I have built this life. Every hour on the ice, every book assigned, every meal prepared was designed to ensure that when I am no longer beside her, she will never need to rely on anyone else to survive.'

The final entry in the seventeenth journal was dated the night before he passed away:

'Ada smiled today when Miss Graham brought her those red glitter signs at the arena. It is good for her to have someone who brings warmth into her quiet world. I am tired tonight, but my work is complete. She is ready for the world. She is strong, she is brilliant, and she is loved.'

Ada sat on the cold floor in the dark bedroom, holding the black leather journal tightly against her chest.

She bowed her head, her dark hair falling forward over her face, and wept.

It was not the silent, disciplined crying she had practiced for weeks. It was a deep, wracking, open sob that tore through her entire body, shaking her shoulders as sixteen years of suppressed grief, love, and overwhelming gratitude finally broke through her guarded walls.

He hadn't been a cold, unfeeling warden. He hadn't been a distant coach who viewed her as a project. He had been a man who sacrificed every second of his existence to build a fortress around her, a father who loved her so fiercely, so completely, and so absolute that he documented every breath she took so she would never doubt her worth.

She cried until her lungs were completely empty, until her eyes were swollen and sore, until the dark room was filled only with the rhythm of her breathing.

Slowly, Ada wiped her wet face with the back of her sleeve. She carefully slid the seventeen journals, the twelve photo albums, and the blue legal file back into the soft black velvet drawer, locking the hidden latch with a soft, solid click beneath the bed.

She stood up on her bare feet, her posture straightening, her spine aligning into its familiar posture.

She walked out of his bedroom, down the stairs, and opened the front door of the house, stepping out into the cool, damp night air beneath the starlit sky.

She picked up her phone from her pocket, her fingers hovering over the screen for two seconds before she tapped the speed-dial contact for Ashley.

The call connected on the very first ring.

"Ada?" Ashley’s voice came through the speaker, raspy, frightened, and thick with tears, as if she had been sitting by her phone in the dark ever since leaving the arena. "Ada, is that you?"

Ada took a slow, deep breath of the cold night air, her purring voice coming through low, clear, and steady.

"Ashley," Ada said softly. "I am sorry for what I said to you at the rink."

Silence held the line for a long second before Ashley let out a breathless, sobbing gasp. "Ada... oh my gosh, Ada..."

"Stay where you are," Ada instructed smoothly, a tiny, soft curve touching her lips in the quiet night. "I am coming over."

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

Leon shifted beneath his faded cotton blanket, his long arm automatically reaching out across the mattress to pull a familiar, warm body against his chest. His fingers met only cold, flat fabric. He patted the space beside him once, twice, his sleep-fogged brain refusing to process the absence.

His eyes snapped open.

The bed was completely empty. The pillow beside him bore a faint indentation, smelling distinctly of vanilla, but the sheets were cool to the touch.

Leon sat up so fast his head throbbed. "Ada?" he called out, his voice thick with sleep.

No answer. The small room was entirely silent save for the hum of his ceiling fan.

Panicked, his blue eyes scanned the space, instantly landing on his wooden desk. Resting right beside his stack of notes was a fresh, white paper bag. The top was neatly folded, and even from his bed, he could smell the rich, mouth-watering aroma of freshly toasted bagels, crispy bacon, and hot coffee drifting through the air.

A cold, bitter knot twisted violently in his stomach. She was gone. She had fed him, let him use her, and then slipped out into the morning like a ghost, leaving a bag of breakfast as a parting gift. She had left him forever. He was sure of it.

A wave of hot, suffocating distress washed over his chest.

"Damn it!" Leon cursed aloud, throwing his cotton blanket off his legs.

He didn't bother checking his reflection. Dressed only in the black t-shirt printed with Ada's face across the front and his rumpled athletic shorts, his blonde hair sticking up in wild, chaotic tufts, Leon yanked his bedroom door open and stormed down the hallway like a man possessed. His bare feet slammed against the wooden floorboards, the noise echoing sharply through the second-floor landing as he practically flew down the stairs toward the main floor.

He was going to find her. He was going to march straight across campus to the science complex, fall to his knees, and beg for her to take him again, promising her that he wouldn't demand anything anymore if she just let him stay by her side.

"Ada!" Leon shouted as he rounded the bottom of the staircase, his voice booming across the first-floor entryway. "Ada!"

He skidded to a dead halt at the entrance of the living room, his jaw dropping open.

The entire Omega Chi fraternity was gathered around the long wooden coffee table and the kitchen island. Chris was sitting on the couch with a giant breakfast burrito in his hands, grease practically dripping onto his paper napkin. Carlos was perched on the edge of the armchair, happily devouring a stack of buttermilk pancakes from a black plastic container. On the table rested four massive, grease-stained restaurant boxes stamped with the bright red logo of 'The Campus Diner'—the most expensive, highly sought-after breakfast spot near the arts quad that usually had a line out the door by seven in the morning.

The room fell dead silent as every single player turned their heads to stare at Leon standing in the doorway, panting for breath, wild-eyed, and looking completely unhinged.

Chris froze with his burrito halfway to his mouth. "Uh... good morning, quarterback?"

Leon blinked, his brain completely stalling out as he looked from Chris's burrito to Carlos's pancakes, then to the mountains of food covering the table. "What... what is all this?" Leon stammered out, his posture stiffening as his panic faltered into utter confusion. "Where did all this food come from?"

Carlos set his fork down, leaning back in his chair with a wide, roaring grin that stretched from ear to ear. "It came from heaven, man. Or, more accurately, it came from your houseguest."

Ethan walked out of the kitchen holding a cardboard cup carrier filled with four fresh coffees. He stopped beside the couch, looking up at Leon with a dry, thoroughly amused expression.

"If you are looking for Ada, you missed her by about an hour," Ethan said smoothly, setting a coffee down in front of Chris. "She walked down these stairs at seven-thirty looking completely pristine, handed me her phone, and asked me to order full breakfast catering for the entire house."

Leon’s breath hitched sharply in his throat. "She... she ordered food for the whole house?"

"She didn't just order food, Kennedy," Chris chimed in, pointing his half-eaten burrito at Leon with respect. "She ordered from TCD. Do you know how long the waitlist is for delivery from that place on a Friday morning? She somehow talked the manager into sending a private courier."

"She is an absolute legend," Carlos declared loudly, raising his coffee cup in a dramatic toast toward the ceiling. "I retract every bad thing I ever said about figure skaters. She just fed thirty starving football players single-handedly."

"She said to make sure you ate your breakfast," Ethan added, picking up his own bagel. "She explicitly told us that if you tried to skip another meal today, we were legally authorized to tackle you into the carpet and force-feed you."

Leon stood frozen near the bottom step, his fists slowly unclenching at his sides. His face burned with overwhelming flush of pure embarrassment as the reality sank in. She hadn't left him forever. She hadn't abandoned him. She had literally bought out the entire diner menu just to make sure he and his friends were fed before practice.

"I... I thought she left," Leon muttered under his breath, looking down at his bare feet, his shoulders slumping in utter defeat.

"She left to go to her eight o'clock seminar, Leon," Ethan noted with a dry raise of his eyebrow. "Some people actually have academic obligations."

"Your girlfriend is incredible, man," a freshman called out from the dining table, his mouth full of bacon. "Seriously, Kennedy, how did you land her?!"

"Yeah, Kennedy!" another player shouted, laughing. "She's so cool! You better not mess this up!"

"She's not..." Leon started, his cheeks burning a deeper, brighter shade of crimson as he rubbed the back of his neck. "We don't... we're not..."

"Oh, please, don't even try it," Ethan interrupted dryly. He reached into his hoodie pocket, pulled out his smartphone, and tapped the screen twice. "Before you say another word, quarterback, you should really check your face in a mirror. Or better yet, look at this." Ethan stepped forward, flipping his phone screen around and holding it directly under Leon's nose.

"What is—" Leon began, squinting at the screen.

His voice died squarely in his throat.

The phone displayed a perfectly lit photo that Ethan had clearly taken the exact second Leon had rounded the staircase. Dressed in the shirt printed with her own face, his blonde hair was sticking up in three different directions, his eyes were wide and watery, and his face...

His face was a complete disaster area.

Covering his cheekbones, his jawline, and the smooth skin of his throat were at least six distinct, bright red, perfectly shaped lipstick marks. Ada’s signature deep crimson shade was stamped boldly across his left cheek, near his ear, and directly over his collarbone, looking like a vibrant, undeniable map of ownership.

Leon stared at the photograph in horror.

"She... she did this?" Leon squeaked out, his voice cracking so sharply it echoed off the high ceiling.

"She certainly did," Ethan confirmed with a slow, ruthless nod, sliding his phone back into his pocket. "She walked out of your room, looked at us sitting in the living room, and didn't even bother to hide her smile. You look like you got attacked by a stamp pad, Kennedy."

One of the seniors burst into a loud, booming bark of laughter that made him choke on his orange juice, doubling over in his armchair until tears streamed down his face. "Oh well, wearing her face on his shirt while covered in her lipstick. Looks like a marked man to me!" "A very well-fed marked man," Chris corrected with a wide grin, tossing a paper napkin toward Leon's chest. "Go back upstairs, wash your face, and eat your breakfast, quarterback. You have film review in two hours, and Burton will kill you if you show up looking like a crimson painting."

Leon caught the napkin against his chest, standing in the middle of the hallway while his teammates cheered, laughed, and continued to gorge themselves on Ada's breakfast delivery.

He looked down at the paper napkin, then raised his hand to gently touch the warm skin of his cheek right over one of the bright red marks. A slow, helpless, completely smitten smile slowly broke across his flushed face, his blue eyes shining bright in the morning light.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

Chapter 2: so high school

The afternoon heat rose off the turf in visible, shimmering waves, baking the synthetic rubber granules until the entire varsity practice complex smelled like burnt tires and fresh line paint.

"Line it up! Red thirty-four! Run it again!" Barry bellowed from the fifty-yard line, his face turning an alarming shade of deep maroon as he clutched his electronic stopwatch. "Kennedy! Your drop-back was sloppy! You're holding the ball like it's a bag of groceries! Step into the throw or I will personally make you carry every piece of equipment onto the bus tomorrow!"

Leon planted his cleats firmly into the black rubber turf, wiping a thick layer of sweat from his forehead with the back of his gloved forearm. His lungs were burning, his heart hammering against his ribs, but his eyes were bright and locked onto the defensive line.

"Set! Blue forty-two! Blue forty-two!" Leon barked out, lowering his hips into his quarterback stance, his voice echoing across the wide field. "Hut!"

The snap hit his gloves with a pop. Leon executed a crisp three-step drop, fake-handing the football to Carlos before pivoting sharply on his right foot. He spotted Chris breaking free past the nickel cornerback, wound his arm back, and fired a bullet forty yards down the left sideline. The ball spiraled tightly through the air, dropping squarely into Chris’s outstretched hands right as he crossed the pylon for a touchdown.

"Better!" Barry yelled, though his clipboard remained raised in disapproval. "Still slow on the read, quarterback! Do it again! Defense, set the front six!"

Carlos trudged back to the huddle, his practice jersey soaked in sweat, his hands resting on his hips as he panted heavily. "I swear Burton wants us to die on this field today. My legs are completely numb, Leon."

"He's just paranoid about Raccoon State," Chris said, jogging back with the football under his arm, his chest heaving as he wiped his brow. "Their secondary is fast, and Burton knows it." "Keep your heads up," Leon instructed, spinning the football between his palms with a grin touching his lips despite his exhaustion. "We run two more sets, nail the red-zone execution, and then we get to pack."

As the defense reset their line formation, Leon’s eyes drifted toward the tall metal bleachers towering over the eastern sideline.

Sitting near the middle section, elevated high above the noise of thirty shouting football players, were three incredibly striking figures.

Ada sat with her long, slender legs crossed elegantly over one another, holding a white paper cup of hot coffee in her red laced gloved fingers, taking slow sips. She was wearing a breathtaking red batwing top that draped loosely over her shoulders in soft, fluid folds, paired with tailored black pants that accentuated her frame, and sharp, pointed red high heels rested firmly against the steel steps. Her red-framed reading glasses caught the harsh sunlight as her dark, unblinking eyes tracked Leon's every movement across the turf.

Beside her sat Rebecca, dressed in the exact same style of loose batwing top, but in a rich, vibrant forest green, paired with dark pants and green heels. A silver laptop was resting on Rebecca's lap.

Completing the trio on Rebecca’s left was Mira, wearing the identical batwing top in a deep, electric sapphire blue, matching black pants, and blue heels. Mira seemed completely engrossed in an iPad game.

Leon stood at the line of scrimmage, his breath catching in his throat as a wide, helpless smile spread across his flushed face under his helmet.

'The Powerpuff Girls are back,' Leon thought to himself.

"Kennedy! Stop staring at the spectators and call the play!" Coach Barry’s roar shattered his train of thought, followed by another ear-splitting whistle blast. "Five-minute water break! Get off my field and hydrate!"

The team instantly broke formation, groaning in collective relief as players trudged toward the water coolers near the team benches.

Leon unbuckled his chin strap, pulled his helmet off his head, and ran a hand through his damp, messy blonde hair. Instead of heading toward the team water station, he turned on his heel and walked straight toward the eastern bleachers, his cleats clicking against the track boundary.

Seeing him approach, Ada set her coffee cup down on the metal bench beside Rebecca. She stood up smoothly, the soft fabric of her red batwing top catching the breeze, and stepped down the metal stairs with grace until her red heels touched the black rubber track right in front of him.

Leon stopped two feet away, holding his helmet under his arm, his blue eyes looking down into her face with a mixture of warmth, relief, and a lingering, lingering trace of lingering guilt.

"Hey," Leon offered softly, his voice raspy from shouting plays for two hours.

"Hello, quarterback," Ada replied smoothly that made his pulse spike. Her dark eyes swept over his sweat-stained practice jersey, taking in his flushed face and damp hair. "You look completely exhausted."

Leon rubbed the back of his neck, looking down at her red high heels before meeting her bare eyes through her glasses. "Yeah... Burton is giving us a hard time today. Last preparation push before rest day for the cup." He paused, swallowing hard, his chest tightening as his mind flashed back to the previous night in his bedroom. "Listen, Ada... about last night..."

Ada raised a dark eyebrow, watching him attentively. "What about last night?"

"I... I wanted to apologize," Leon stammered out, his cheeks turning a bright shade of pink beneath his tan. "When I was shouting... I...I didn't mean to be so aggressive. I was just... a mess."

Ada looked at his sincere, worried face for two long seconds. The cool distance in her expression softened into a wicked glint.

"You were a brat, Leon," Ada noted calmly. "And your apology is accepted."

Before Leon could form another sentence, Ada stepped directly into his space. She reached out, her fingers hooking firmly into the collar of his practice jersey, and pulled his long frame down to her level. She pressed her soft, warm lips firmly against his in a deep possessive kiss right there on the track.

A sudden chorus of loud, chaotic whistles, catcalls, and cheering exploded from the team bench.

"Oooooh! Look at the quarterback!" Carlos yelled at the top of his lungs, waving a water bottle in the air. "Securing the championship motivation!"

"Get it, Kennedy!" two freshmen tackles roared, laughing as they slammed their helmets together.

Leon’s mind went entirely blank, his hands reaching up automatically to rest against her waist, pulling her closer as he melted into the kiss. Her mouth tasted like warm vanilla and dark coffee, completely overwhelming his senses until the entire football field seemed to dissolve around them.

Ada slowly pulled back, her dark eyes glittering with satisfaction behind her glasses as she looked up at his flushed, thoroughly dazed face.

"Now," Ada murmured. "If you want to actually win your games at Raccoon State tomorrow, you need to fix three distinct flaws in your offensive execution."

Leon blinked, his brain slowly rebooting from the kiss. "Flaws? What flaws?"

Ada rested one hand casually against her hip, her red batwing top draping elegantly.

"First," Ada said smoothly, holding up one slender finger, "your left guard is leaning his weight backward three seconds before every single pass play. Raccoon State’s defensive end will read that instantly and execute an inside swim move every time."

Leon’s blue eyes widened in genuine surprise. "Wait... really?"

"Second," Ada continued, holding up a second finger, "Chris is breaking his route two steps too early on the quick slant. He is turning his shoulders before he secures positioning, leaving a wide-open gap for the free safety to make an interception."

Leon stared at her, his jaw slightly slack. "I didn't even notice that."

"And third," Ada finished, holding up a third finger, "you are holding the ball three inches too low on your play-action fake. When you hand off to Carlos, your left elbow flares out, exposing the ball to a strip-tackling linebacker. Fix those three things, or Coach Burton’s strategy will fail within the first quarter."

Leon stood completely frozen, staring down at her in awe. "Ada... how did you even catch all of that from the bleachers?"

"I pay attention," Ada noted dryly, a tiny smirk touching her lips. She reached up, gently tapping the tip of his nose with her index finger. "Now go back to your team, quarterback." Without waiting for his response, Ada turned on her red high heels, the red fabric of her batwing top swaying gracefully as she stepped back up the metal bleachers, returning to her seat beside Rebecca and Mira.

Leon stood on the track for a moment, his chest pounding, fire-fueled motivation igniting in his veins. He turned around, strapped his helmet back onto his head, and marched straight back toward the fifty-yard line.

"Gather round! Offense! On me!" Leon roared, his voice cutting across the entire practice complex with such intense power that even Coach Barry paused mid-whistle.

Chris, Carlos, and the rest of the starting unit rushed over, forming a tight circle around him.

"Listen up!" Leon commanded, his blue eyes sharp as steel as he looked around the huddle. "We are fixing three things right now before we leave this field!"

"What are you talking about, Leon?" Chris asked, catching his breath.

"First, left guard!" Leon pointed a gloved finger directly at the starting lineman. "You are leaning your weight back on your heels before pass plays! Stop giving away the snap count! Keep your center of gravity low and neutral!"

The left guard blinked, his eyes widening. "Wait... I am?"

"Second, Chris!" Leon turned his focus onto Redfield. "You are cutting your slant route two steps too early! Run the full five yards, plant your cleat hard, and drive your shoulders forward before turning for the ball! Otherwise their free safety is going to jump the route!"

Chris paused, processing the advice, before giving a firm, resolute nod. "Got it. Full five yards."

"And third, Carlos!" Leon looked at his running back. "On the play-action fake, stay tight to my hip! I am keeping the ball tucked high against my chest so their linebacker can't strip it on the sweep!"

Carlos grinned wide, clapping his gloves together hard. "Let's run it! Right now!"

Coach Barry Burton walked over, his whistle hanging loose from his neck, his eyebrows raised in mild shock as he watched Leon seamlessly reorganize the offensive line.

"Alright!" Barry yelled, crossing his arms over his chest. "Let's see if this quarterback speech actually works! Red thirty-four! On my whistle!"

The team slammed into their positions. Leon dropped low, his cleats digging deep into the synthetic rubber turf.

"Set! Blue forty-two!" Leon shouted, his voice ringing out clear and powerful across the complex. "Hut!"

The snap hit his gloves. The left guard held a perfect, immovable stance, completely neutralizing the defensive rush. Chris ran the full five yards, planted his foot with violent force, and cut hard across the center, securing position. Leon tucked the ball high, executed a flawless play-action fake that completely fooled the linebacker, and fired a thirty-yard strike directly into Chris’s chest.

It was a completely picture-perfect execution.

"Now that," Barry shouted, clapping his broad hands together, "is how a varsity team plays football! Great work, Kennedy!"

Through her red-framed glasses, she met his proud, smitten gaze across the turf, offering him a tiny, almost imperceptible nod of absolute approval before returning her focus to her drink.

She leaned into a deep edge, her red-framed glasses absent, her posture radiating power, grace, and effortless control. Ashley stepped up right beside him at the glass barrier, leaning her arms against the top ledge.

"Yeah," Leon whispered back, his voice thick with unadulterated devotion, his eyes never leaving the girl on the ice for even a single second. "She really is."

"I have you," Ada repeated softly, stepping backward on the ice, pulling him along with her at a slow, gentle pace. "Look at me, not your feet."

Leon locked his blue eyes onto her face, ignoring the terrifying feeling of the slippery floor beneath him. As long as he looked at her dark eyes and her gentle smile, he felt entirely safe.

"See?" Ada murmured, her posture perfectly balanced as she glided backward, pulling him smoothly across the center of the rink. "You are skating."

"I am being pulled like a sled," Leon corrected, a nervous, breathless chuckle escaping his chest as his legs slowly found a basic balance. "There is a massive technical difference."

"You are moving across the ice without falling," Ada countered, her eyes crinkling with joy. "That is a victory in my book."

For the next thirty minutes, they moved around the empty, dimly lit arena. Slowly, with Ada’s patient guidance, Leon managed to take small, independent pushes, his long legs gliding forward while Ada skated circles around him with breathtaking grace. She spun around his broad frame, her black skirt flaring out, her silver stopwatch necklace catching the faint blue lights as she laughed at his overly serious, focused expression.

"Look, Ada! I am doing it!" Leon called out proudly, gliding six full feet on his own before coming to a clumsy, skidding stop near the center circle.

"Impressive, quarterback," Ada praised softly, gliding up beside him, her hands coming to rest lightly against his waist as they coasted to a halt together in the center of the rink. "Maybe we should add figure skating to your athletic scholarship."

"Don't push your luck," Leon laughed, wrapping his broad arms securely around her waist, pulling her soft frame close against his chest as they stood together on the white surface.

The room fell into a quiet tranquility, broken only by the hum of the ice cooling system and the sound of their shared, peaceful breathing.

Leon rested his chin lightly on top of her head, looking out across the vast, dark arena.

Suddenly, Ada went still in his arms.

She turned her head, her dark eyes staring off into the distance toward the high, empty bleachers sitting in the shadows near the far side of the building. Her expression softened completely, the teasing glint in her eyes melting away into a nostalgic tenderness. A small, soft, incredibly gentle smile touched her lips as she looked at the empty seats, as if seeing a fond, distant memory written across the rows of blue plastic chairs.

Leon noticed the shift instantly. He tilted his head down, looking at her soft profile with curiosity.

"Ada?" Leon called out softly, his voice gentle and full of warmth. "What is it?"

Ada blinked, her gaze lingering on the empty bleachers for one more second before she smoothly turned her head back to look up at Leon. The nostalgic softness in her face shifted back into her signature, teasing, radiant smile, her dark eyes flashing with playful mischief.

"Nothing, quarterback," Ada murmured softly, her fingers lightly tapping against his chest.

Before Leon could ask another question, Ada smoothly pushed off his chest, gliding backward across the slick ice with incredible, breathtaking speed. She raised a dark eyebrow, a competitive, wicked smirk spreading across her lips.

"Now," Ada called out, her voice echoing playfully across the empty arena as she spun around to skate backward away from him, "you have three seconds to turn around, or I am going to chase you down and tackle you onto the ice!"

Leon’s eyes widened in sheer, energetic panic. "Hey! That's not fair! I can barely stop!"

"One!" Ada called out, laughing loudly as she picked up speed, her silver stopwatch necklace bouncing against her black top as she charged toward him.

"Ada, wait!" Leon yelled, letting out a loud, happy laugh as he clumsily turned his skates around, frantically pushing off the ice to escape her, his heart soaring with joy under the bright blue lights of the arena.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

The chilly, pre-dawn fog settled over the parking lot like a thick gray blanket, smelling faintly of exhaust fumes, wet asphalt, and lukewarm gas station coffee. High above the rows of parked buses, the yellow streetlights flickered against the gloomy sky, illuminating the sleepy figures of student-athletes dragging their duffel bags across the pavement.

It was six o'clock in the morning, and almost every single person present looked like a walking corpse.

Except for Chris.

Chris was bouncing on the balls of his feet, holding a giant stainless-steel clipboard in one hand and a whistle in the other, radiating enough unholy energy to power a small city block. "Alright, people! Move it! Move it!" Chris bellowed, his voice echoing loudly across the damp parking lot and making table tennis athletes wince in unison. "The primary sports transport van leaves in ten minutes! We have a three-hour drive to Raccoon State, and if we hit traffic on the interstate, Coach Burton is going to make us do push-ups on the shoulder!"

"Chris, if you don't stop yelling before sunrise, I am going to stuff a sock in your whistle," Carlos groaned from where he was slouched against the open side door of the white passenger van. His eyes were half-shut, his dark hair sticking up in messy curls, and he was holding a paper cup like it contained the fountain of youth. "It is far too early for this level of enthusiasm."

"There is no such thing as too early for athletic excellence, Carlos!" Chris replied sharply, snapping his clipboard pencil against the metal frame. "If we miss our departure window, the entire schedule collapses!"

"The only thing collapsing right now is my patience," Carlos barked back, rubbing his temples in utter agony. "You've been pacing the lot for forty-five minutes, Redfield. Take a breath or go drink a gallon of water."

"I don't need water, I need my defense in their seats!" Chris snapped back, pointing his clipboard at Carlos's face. "And you still haven't loaded your secondary gear tub!"

"It's already loaded, you micromanaging maniac!" Carlos shot back, throwing his hands up in complete disbelief. "Check off your little list and leave my ears alone!"

Right on cue, Piers came shuffling past the rear tire of the van, dragging a massive duffel bag crammed with basketball gear over his shoulder, and he looked completely out of it. His eyes heavy with sleep as he blindly stepped over an uneven patch of pavement. His sneaker caught the edge of the curb, and his broad frame lurched forward violently, his long arms windmilling through the cold air as he nearly face-planted straight into the asphalt.

"Whoa! Watch your feet, basketball star!" Carlos barked out, catching Piers by the back of his hoodie jacket just in time to pull him upright.

Piers blinked sluggishly, shaking his head to clear his brain. "Thanks, Carlos... I didn't see the curb."

"Yeah, no kidding," Carlos muttered, shaking his head before glaring back at Chris. "See what your yelling does? You're distracting the other teams now!"

"He tripped on concrete, Carlos! That has nothing to do with my voice!" Chris yelled back defensively.

A few yards away, mayhem was unfolding around the support team equipment truck. Claire and Jill were sprinting back and forth in matching crimson windbreakers, frantically loading plastic tubs, emergency medical kits, and giant printed banners into the storage bed.

"Claire! Where are the extra helmets?!" Jill yelled, dodging a bleary-eyed athletic trainer. "Did you put them in tub three or tub four?!"

"Tub four!" Claire shouted back, her ponytail bouncing wildly as she shoved a cooler into the trunk space. "And make sure you grab the megaphones! If we don't have the cheer section set up before kickoff, Coach Burton will lose his mind!"

Leon stood near the middle of the loading line, leaning his shoulder against a concrete pillar while holding his black gym bag. His blonde hair was messy, his eyes were small with residual fatigue, and he was taking slow sips from a lukewarm travel mug.

He glanced to his right, instantly catching sight of Grace trailing behind her girlfriend. Grace looked like she was genuinely about to pass out on the asphalt, her eyes closed, her feet dragging slowly through the damp morning air. Walking right beside her, pacing back and forth with high-strung, frantic energy, was Bela.

Bela was holding a bow case in one hand, gesturing wildly with her other hand as she ranted endlessly about archery regulations. "I am telling you, Grace, the target distance at Raccoon State is five meters further than our practice range! The wind resistance near their stadium is going to throw off my entire draw weight! If the referees don't recalibrate the target stands, I am going to file a formal protest with the athletic board!"

"Mmhmm," Grace mumbled, her head lolling to the side, her eyes never opening for a single second as she blindly reached out to hold the strap of Bela's jacket. "You're going to do great, sweetie..."

"Are you even listening to me?!" Bela huffed, though she carefully adjusted her jacket so Grace could lean her forehead against her shoulder. "I am talking about wind drift!"

Leon let out a soft, amused chortle, shifting his weight only to glance toward the side of the transport van.

Standing right beside the rear bumper, completely unbothered by the loud noise around them, were Helena and Mira. Helena had her hands pressed against Mira's waist, pulling her close against the metal van frame, while Mira—dressed in her blue university gymnastics warm-up jacket—had her arms looped tightly around Helena's neck. They were making out in the wild, ignoring the world entirely.

Carlos turned his head, spotting the couple, and let out a loud, theatrical whistle. "Hey! Save some oxygen for the gymnastics meet, Mira! Helena needs her lungs to run defense!"

Helena broke the kiss just long enough to flip Carlos off over her shoulder without looking, before pulling Mira right back into her space.

"They have zero shame," Carlos chuckled to Leon, shaking his head. "Zero."

On the far side of the lot, Ashley was standing on top of a concrete bench, wearing a bright yellow cheer fleece jacket over her uniform. She held a silver megaphone, her sharp voice cutting through the morning air like a siren.

"Alright, cheer squad! Listen up!" Ashley barked, clapping her hands together twice. "We are doing a quick ten-minute stretch routine right now! I want high knees, arm circles, and split jumps before we load onto the bus! No slumping, no whining, and definitely no sleeping!" A collective groan echoed from fifteen cheerleaders, but they immediately fell into line, starting their arm circles under Ashley's stern gaze.

Leon shook his head, taking another sip of his coffee. He was just about to step toward the van door to load his bag when a low engine purr vibrated through the parking lot asphalt.

Headlights cut through the lingering gray fog.

A sleek, immaculate black porsche rolled smoothly onto the access road, its dark tinted windows catching the yellow glow of the streetlights. The sports car glided to an effortless stop directly in front of the football team loading line, drawing the instant attention of every single athlete present.

The driver's door swung open.

Ada stepped out onto the pavement, and Leon’s heart gave an energetic jump against his ribs.

She was wearing the official crimson-and-white windbreaker for the university figure skater team, zipped completely closed up to her throat, paired with black short shorts that showcased her long, sleek legs. She wore red ballet styled sports shoes on her feet, giving her posture a light, springing grace. Her dark hair was styled into her signature sharp bob.

Through the clear rear window of the porsche, Rebecca could be seen sitting neatly in the back seat. Rebecca was holding two large, insulated cardboard coffee carriers filled with giant paper cups carefully steady against her lap, her face lit up with a bright, morning smile as she waved toward the guys through the glass.

Ada walked smoothly over to the passenger side, opening the rear door so Rebecca could hand out the warm drinks. Ada took one carrier from her and handed it directly into Carlos's reaching hands, along with two large, grease-stained paper bags smelling heavenly of fresh, warm butter and toasted baked goods.

"Good morning, gentlemen," Ada said smoothly, "I brought fresh roast coffee and four dozen warm croissants from the bakery near the quad."

Carlos clutched the coffee carrier against his chest as if it were a rare treasure, bowing his head in dramatic reverence. "I am forever indebted to your grace, your Majesty!"

"Don't get used to it, Oliveira," Ada replied with a smirk.

She walked past the cheer section after giving Ashley her paperbag and coffee, handing the two warm paper bags of croissants directly to Coach Burton, who was standing near the front of the van checking a clipboard.

"Coach Burton," Ada greeted politely, offering a respectful nod. "A small breakfast for you and the staff before the long drive."

Coach Burton’s strict, stern face immediately softened as he smelled the warm, buttery baked goods. "Miss Wong! Well, now, this is exceptionally thoughtful of you. Thank you. We certainly need the energy for the road."

"How is your program's travel arrangement looking?" Ada asked smoothly, resting one hand on her hip as she chatted with the head coach. "I spoke with Mr. Branagh earlier this morning. My coach mentioned that the primary arena ice block opens at two o'clock today for our initial practice sessions."

"Marvin is a good man," Coach Burton nodded approvingly, taking a fresh croissant from the bag. "He told me your division is setting up near the east wing of the complex. We should arrive right on schedule around nine-thirty."

Ada offered a small, elegant smile, her dark eyes shifting sideways to lock onto Leon, who was standing nearby, watching her with wide, smitten, completely hopeless blue eyes. "Speaking of travel, Coach Burton," Ada said, her tone dropping into a smooth, persuasive register. "I was wondering if I might steal your starting quarterback for the drive down? I am driving down with Rebecca, and I would appreciate having someone to keep us awake on the highway. We can stay in direct convoy right behind your lead van the entire way."

Coach looked from Ada's charming, flawless smile over to Leon, who was practically holding his breath with hopeful anticipation.

"Take him," Coach Burton agreed without a single second of hesitation, waving his hand dismissively. "Honestly, Miss Wong, take him. Kennedy has been staring at the fence like a lost puppy for twenty minutes anyway. Just make sure he shows up to the team meeting at ten o'clock sharp."

"He will be there on time, Coach," Ada promised smoothly, her dark eyes flashing with victory.

Carlos burst out laughing, nudging Leon's shoulder hard. "Go on, quarterback! Get out of here before Burton changes his mind!"

"See you guys at the venue!" Leon called out, a massive, unstoppable grin breaking across his face as he grabbed his gym bag from the pavement.

He walked quickly over to the front passenger side of the sleek black Porsche, opening the door.

"Good morning, Leon!" Rebecca piped up cheerfully from the back seat as he slid smoothly into the low, leather bucket seat, leaning forward between the headrests with a bright smile. "Ready for the road trip?"

"Good morning, Rebecca," Leon replied effortlessly, a warm, relaxed smile spreading across his face as he settled into the seat. "Definitely ready."

Leon tossed his bag into the small space, the rich scent of fine leather, fresh coffee, and Ada's vanilla perfume instantly wrapping around his senses.

Ada slid into the driver's seat, closing her door with a solid, satisfying thud. She turned her head, looking at Leon’s flushed, happy face as she adjusted her rear-view mirror.

"You look far too excited for six in the morning, Leon," Ada teased softly, her fingers resting lightly on the gear shift.

"I am riding in a Porsche with the most beautiful girl at this university," Leon replied effortlessly, leaning back against the leather headrest with a bright smile. "I have every reason to be excited."

Ada let out a soft, low chuckle, her eyes warm as she shifted the car into drive, smoothly pulling out of the parking lot to lead the convoy toward the open highway.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

"So," Rebecca began, her bright hazel eyes gleaming with playful mischief as she looked at Ada, "are we going to talk about how you managed to bribe an entire Division One football coaching staff with four dozen croissants, or is that classified state secrets?"

Ada didn't take her eyes off the highway, though a small, wicked curve touched the corner of her lips. "It isn't a secret, Rebecca. It is marketing. Football coaches are remarkably simple creatures. Feed them carbohydrates before sunrise, and they will agree to virtually anything."

Leon let out a soft laugh, turning his head slightly toward the driver's side. "Hey, I am sitting right here, you know. We aren't that easy to manipulate."

"Leon, please," Rebecca chimed in instantly from behind him, her voice brimming with affectionate sarcasm. "Coach Burton literally handed over his quarterback without asking for a single piece fuss. Ada could have told him she was taking you to a different state, and he would have just handed her your playbook and wished you both a nice weekend."

"He trusts Ada," Leon defended, rubbing the back of his neck as a faint pink flush crept up his cheekbones. "Besides, I am far more useful in the front seat of a sports car than squeezed into the back of a team transport van between Carlos and Chris while they argue about protein powder brands."

"You mean you would rather be sitting next to your favorite person," Rebecca corrected smoothly, batting her eyelashes with exaggerated innocence. "It's fine, Leon. You don't have to hide it from me. I saw the way you sprinted to this car like you were breaking a forty-yard dash record."

"I did not sprint," Leon insisted, though his voice cracked slightly on the second word.

"You practically leapt over the curb," Ada added softly, cutting into the conversation. "I was half-afraid you were going to tackle my passenger door off its hinges."

"See?!" Rebecca giggled, leaning even closer into the gap between their seats. "Ada saw it too! You were like a golden retriever seeing the front door open!"

"I was merely exercising standard athletic speed," Leon grumbled playfully, crossing his arms over his broad chest, though a smile was completely ruining his attempt at looking stern. "You two are ganging up on me, and we haven't even hit the first toll booth yet."

"It isn't ganging up if we are simply stating documented facts, quarterback," Ada noted, flicking her turn signal with a flick of her wrist as she smoothly passed a silver freight truck. "Though I must admit, your enthusiasm at six o'clock in the morning was remarkably entertaining."

"I was happy to escape the van," Leon said, casting a warm, devoted look at Ada. "And the company in here is infinitely superior."

"Smooth, Kennedy," Rebecca laughed, shaking her head. "Very smooth. But if you think compliments are going to save you from your mandatory meal, you are severely mistaken."

Before Leon could even ask what she meant, Rebecca reached deep into her large paper bakery bag. She pulled out a thick, freshly baked, golden-brown mini french bread roll that was still steaming. Without warning, she leaned forward over the central console and shoved the end of the french bread directly against Leon's mouth.

"Eat your carbohydrates, quarterback!" Rebecca ordered cheerfully, holding the bread firmly against his lips. "Ada's strict orders! She explicitly told me to feed you the second we hit the highway so you don't skip another meal!"

Leon gasped in utter surprise, his eyes widening as he instinctively opened his mouth to catch the warm, buttery bread before it could drop onto his clean grey hoodie. He took a huge, awkward bite, his cheeks puffing out as he chewed frantically, trying his absolute best to maintain his dignity while holding the remaining piece in his hand.

"Rebecca!" Leon mumbled through a full mouth, his face turning bright crimson as he glanced from the back seat to Ada. "You almost choked me!"

Ada let out a sudden, genuine burst of laughter that filled the cabin of the porsche. She tilted her head, her dark eyes flashing with pure delight as she glanced at Leon’s stuffed cheeks and wide, shocked blue eyes.

"I told Rebecca to make sure you ate, Leon," Ada laughed softly, her voice smooth and rich with amusement. "Considering your recent history of ignoring nutrition, I decided direct supervision was necessary."

"He survived thirty-two defensive tackles last Saturday, Ada," Rebecca countered with a bright grin, sitting back against her leather seat and taking a sip of her iced coffee. "He can handle a little fresh french bread on your command."

Leon swallowed his bite, wiping a stray crumb from the corner of his lips with the back of his hand, though he couldn't stop the happy smile from spreading across his face. Hearing Ada laugh like that, free, light, and entirely unburdened, was worth every bit of teasing.

"I am fine," Leon declared, holding up the remaining half of the french bread roll. "In fact, it's delicious. Did you get these from the bakery near the science building?"

"Straight from the oven at five-fifteen," Rebecca confirmed proudly. "I had to fight two varsity soccer athletes for the last bag of warm rolls, but I prevailed for the sake of Ada's strict feeding schedule."

"Your sacrifice is deeply appreciated, Rebecca," Leon said, taking another bite of the bread and leaning back against his headrest. "So, what is the official schedule once we actually land at the sports complex?"

Ada adjusted her grip on the steering wheel, "The skating division has an initial ice check at two o'clock in the main arena. Marvin wants us to test the surface density and do a light edge warm-up before the official judges' meeting at four. My competition block isn't until seven o'clock tonight."

"Seven PM?" Leon repeated, his blue eyes lighting up instantly. "That's perfect. Our meeting wraps up at five, so I can definitely be in the front row of the stands before your game starts."

"You better be in the front row, Kennedy," Rebecca added with a nod. "Heard Graham's going to be right there in the stands with her cheer minions, loud enough to wake up the whole stadium."

"I wouldn't miss it for anything," Leon said softly.

"And the gymnastics team has their floor apparatus inspection at one-thirty," Rebecca chimed in, checking her digital tablet screen. "Which means I have about three free hours between two and five o'clock while Mira does her balance beam routine."

"What are you planning to do with three free hours, Becca?" Leon asked, turning his head slightly to look at her over his shoulder.

Rebecca’s eyes lit up with instant excitement. She set her iced coffee into the rear cup holder and clapped her hands together. "Are you kidding? The RSA lobby is hosting the entire regional sports merchandise expo this year! They have limited-edition university jackets, official division pins, custom shirts, custom posters, and those collectible figure plushies that sold out in three minutes online!"

Leon raised an eyebrow. "You are going on a merchandise shopping spree?"

"It is not just a shopping spree, Leon, it is an investment in team morale!" Rebecca declared stoutly. "I am going to go through all the vendor tables, buy some really cool stuff, and get the custom tournament lanyards for the whole support squad. Claire and Jill already gave me a list of official football division patches they want for their banners."

She leaned forward again, looking directly at the back of Ada's head.

"Ada," Rebecca asked softly, her tone turning hopeful, "you are coming with me to the merch tables after your ice check, right? You promised last week that you would help me pick out jackets before your performance."

Ada glanced at the rear-view mirror, her dark eyes reflecting the warm morning light as a subtle, soft expression softened her features.

"Of course, Rebecca," Ada agreed softly.

"Yes!" Rebecca cheered, pumping her fist in the air. "We are going to buy out the entire souvenir store before the track team even gets there."

"Just leave some official pennants for Chris," Leon warned dryly, a fond grin playing on his lips. "If he doesn't get a commemorative tournament pin, he will probably cry into his clipboard."

"My boyfriend can buy his own pins," Rebecca teased smoothly. "He had his chance when he was obsessing over the travel itinerary this morning."

The car glided effortlessly down the long stretch of highway, the morning sun fully rising above the tree line, washing the interior of the porsche in warm light. Leon took a final sip of his coffee, looking out at the road ahead, then back at Ada.

"Hey, Ada?" Leon asked softly, leaning a fraction of an inch closer to her side.

"Yes, Leon?" Ada murmured, not taking her eyes off the road, though the corner of her lips twitched upward in anticipation.

"Can we listen to music now, or is the quarterback required to sit in respectful silence for the next two hours?"

Ada let out a low chuckle, her thumb reaching over to tap the touchscreen control panel on the dashboard. "You may choose the station, Kennedy. But if you play anything featuring heavy rock before eight in the morning, I am handing you back to Chris at the next rest stop."

"Deal," Leon grinned, leaning forward to browse the digital radio, while Rebecca's light giggles echoed comfortably from the back seat.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

The vast glass doors of the Raccoon State Complex swung shut behind them, sealing out the highway traffic and enveloping them in the cool, buzzing atmosphere of the regional tournament venue.

High above the polished concrete concourses, giant banners fluttered beneath the rafters, advertising the collegiate athletic divisions competing throughout the weekend.

In the bustling intersection connecting the East and South wings, Ada stepped out of the flow of foot traffic, pulling her small black gear bag onto her shoulder. Beside her, Rebecca was already holding her tablet, scrolling through floor maps and checking team check-in schedules.

"The skating team locker rooms are just down this corridor to the left, Ada," Rebecca noted, pointing a finger toward the brightly lit hallway marked East Wing Skating Division. "Marvin said we need to hand in our equipment tags before two o'clock."

"I am aware, Rebecca," Ada replied smoothly, though her dark eyes were not focused on the tablet screen.

They were focused on Leon.

Leon stood in front of her, his large gym bag slung over his broad shoulder, his blonde hair slightly ruffled from the car ride. He was looking down at her in a way that Rebecca casually stepped three paces to the side, pretending to be intensely fascinated by an emergency exit map posted on the wall.

"So," Leon murmured, his voice dropping into a low, raspy register that was meant for her ears. "I guess I have to let you go to your division wing now."

"You have a strategy meeting at ten o'clock sharp, quarterback," Ada reminded him. "Coach Burton was very explicit about your arrival time."

"I know," Leon whispered. He didn't step back. Instead, he reached out, his broad hand coming to rest gently against the side of her waist, pulling her a fraction of an inch closer. Before Ada could utter another warning, Leon leaned down, pressing his lips firmly against hers in a warm, lingering kiss.

Ada’s eyes fluttered shut for a second, her fingers automatically rising to rest against the lapel of his windbreaker. When Leon pulled back, he didn't actually walk away. He hovered mere inches from her mouth, looked into her dark eyes, and immediately leaned in to kiss her a second time, deeper and sweeter than the first.

"Leon," Ada murmured against his lips, though her grip on his windbreaker didn't loosen even a millimeter. "People are walking past us."

"Let them walk," Leon murmured back, pressing a quick, soft third kiss right against the corner of her mouth, followed instantly by a fourth kiss directly over her cheekbone, making her let out a low, breathless chuckle. "I'm just making sure I have enough energy to survive Chris's lecture."

"You are relentless," Ada teased softly, a rare, radiant smile breaking across her lips as she lightly patted his chest, stepping back just enough to break his hold. "Now go. Do not make your coach regret trusting me."

"I'll see you at seven o'clock," Leon promised, his blue eyes shining bright with devotion. "Front row of the stands."

"I will hold you to that, Kennedy," Ada replied smoothly.

Leon offered a grin, turned on his heel, and took off down the main concourse at a fast, athletic jog. He practically sprinted toward the South Wing Varsity Football Division, dodging around a group of athletes and leaping cleanly over a rolled-up floor mat.

Behind him, Rebecca glided back over to Ada's side, watching the tall quarterback sprint away like a giant, happy dog.

"You really have him completely hypnotized, Ada," Rebecca noted with a bright, giggling laugh. "The man's down bad."

"He is energetic," Ada noted calmly, turning smoothly toward the East Wing corridor, though the subtle, warm blush lingering on her cheekbones. "Come on, Rebecca. Let's find Marvin."

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

Leon rounded the final corner of the South Wing corridor, his breath coming in fast, rhythmic puffs as his sneakers slapped against the polished linoleum floor. He checked his wrist watch —nine fifty-eight. Two whole minutes to spare.

The double doors of Conference Room B were propped wide open. Inside, the entire varsity football roster was already gathered around a long, wooden conference table. Projector screens were lowered against the front wall, displaying detailed offensive diagrams, defensive coverage overlays, and opponent player stats for tomorrow's regional opener.

Coach Burton was standing near the front whiteboard, holding a blue dry-erase marker in one hand and his stainless-steel clipboard in the other.

"Take your seats, gentlemen!" Burton barked out, his voice booming across the fluorescent-lit room as the clock on the wall hit ten o'clock sharp. "The travel phase is officially over! As of right now, we are in tournament mode! Tomorrow morning at nine o'clock, we step onto that turf against Raccoon State's defense, and I want zero errors!"

Leon slid smoothly into an empty chair near the middle of the long table, setting his gym bag down beside his feet.

Carlos, who was sitting right next to him with an official team playbook binder open on the wood, turned his head and gave Leon a sharp, grinning side eye.

"Look who made it," Carlos whispered under his breath, leaning closer to Leon. "And right on time, too. Did Ada drop you off at the door, or did she have to physically drag you away from her porsche?"

"I jogged here, Carlos," Leon replied in a low, dignified whisper, adjusting his windbreaker's collar. "I am entirely focused."

"Sure you are," Carlos smirked, pointing a finger at Leon's jawline. "You still have a smudge of her crimson lipstick right under your left ear, quarterback."

Leon’s eyes widened in instant panic, his hand flying up to frantically rub his skin, making Carlos snort with muffled, shaking laughter.

"Alright, settle down!" Burton commanded loudly, tapping his dry-erase marker sharply against the whiteboard frame, drawing every single eye in the room to the drawn plays. "Let's review the primary offensive package! Raccoon State runs a aggressive four-three defense with a blitz line. Leon!"

Leon snapped his head up, his posture instantly straightening into sharp, quarterback attention. "Yes, Coach?"

"Tomorrow's game relies entirely on your read-option decisions," Burton stated sternly, his eyes narrowing as he swept his pencil across the board, drawing a thick blue arrow through the defensive line. "Their middle linebacker likes to bite early on the inside hand-off. If you catch him sliding toward Carlos, I want you to keep the ball yourself and take the outer lane. Do you understand?"

"Understood, Coach," Leon agreed firmly, his blue eyes locking onto the diagram with absolute clarity. "I read the linebacker, fake the hand-off to Carlos, and pull out to the right sideline."
