"Exactly," Coach Burton nodded, looking thoroughly satisfied with Leon's instant response. "We need total execution. Zero turnovers, zero foolish penalties. Chris and the defensive captains will be managing the field adjustments, but on the field, we run this playbook down their throats."

"What about their secondary coverage?" Carlos chimed in, leaning forward over his open binder. "Are they sticking to man-to-man, or are they dropping into zone on third down?"

"They drop into a cover-three zone the second it's third-and-long," Burton explained, writing down a series of route numbers on the board. "Which means our tight ends need to carve out space in the seam. We keep the short passing game fast, keep their defensive line tired, and control the clock from the first quarter."

While Coach Burton continued to break down the second-half punt return strategies when a commotion echoed from the open hallway outside Conference Room B.

Jill walked past the open double doors, carrying two massive plastic storage tubs stacked directly on top of each other in her arms. The top tub was filled to the brim with spare football helmets and extra shoulder pads, while the bottom tub rattled with metal water bottles and medical kits. Her muscles were visibly tensing under her crimson support team polo shirt, her face set in a determined, stubborn line as she navigated the crowded hallway without asking anyone for help.

Claire was jogging right beside her, carrying a long rolled-up team banner under her arm and a box of medical tape.

"Jill, let me take the top tub!" Claire insisted loudly, reaching out to catch the wobbling corner of the plastic container. "You are going to drop the helmets right in front of the director's office!"

"I am not dropping anything, Claire!" Jill shot back through gritted teeth, balancing the immense weight with impressive ease as she adjusted her grip. "I told Coach Burton I would handle the equipment delivery for the primary locker room, and I am delivering the primary equipment!"

"You are carrying sixty pounds of gear by yourself!" Claire protested, laughing as she walked backward to guide Jill through the doorway toward the training rooms down the hall. "You are just as stubborn as my brother!"

"I am far more organized than your brother!" Jill called back cheerfully, disappearing around the corner with both boxes perfectly balanced in her arms.

Inside the conference room, Coach Burton paused his white-board lecture for a brief second, watching Jill carry the heavy tubs past the door.

"See that?" Burton pointed his marker toward the hallway, looking at his fellow teammates. "That is the level of dedication every single person in this program brings to Raccoon State! The support team is locked in! The coaching staff is locked in! And tomorrow morning, we take the field and we win!"

A loud, booming cheer erupted from the thirty football players gathered around the long table, fists pounding against the wooden surface in unison.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

"Look at these!" Rebecca gasped out cheerfully, stopping dead in her tracks in front of a rotating metal display rack packed with regional souvenir keychains. "Ada, they have the mini metal ice skates! And look at this tiny balance beam for Mira!"

Ada halted beside her, a subtle, entertained curve touching the corner of her lips. "Did you not promise Claire you would focus on the football division patches first?"

"The patches are in the next aisle, Ada," Rebecca defended playfully, spinning the metal rack with her index finger. "This is a necessary pitstop. Look, they even have tiny plush animals wearing varsity hoodies!"

Rebecca reached out, grabbing a small, fluffy brown teddy bear keychain wearing an oversized navy hoodie with the number seven printed boldly on the chest. She held it up right under Ada's chin.

"Oh my gosh," Rebecca giggled, her hazel eyes sparkling with mischief. "Ada, look at this. It looks exactly like Leon."

Ada looked down at the tiny plush bear, taking it from Rebecca's hand. She turned it over in her palm, looking at the clumsy, wide plastic eyes and the slightly oversized hood that draped over its small ears. An incredibly fond expression softened her dark eyes, a smile gracing her face.

"It does have his exact expression when he gets confused during playbook lectures," Ada noted softly with affection.

"You have to buy it for him!" Rebecca insisted, nudging Ada's elbow with her shoulder. "He will probably attach it to his gym bag and stare at it for several hours straight!"

"I suppose two dollars is a small price to pay for his entertainment," Ada murmured smoothly, pulling her wallet from her windbreaker pocket and handing the payment to the vendor without a single second of hesitation.

"You are so sweet to him," Rebecca teased as they stepped away from the counter, Ada sliding the small teddy bear keychain safely inside her pocket. "So, how did the ice check feel? Marvin seemed really pleased with your edge work during practice."

"The ice density is slightly softer near the west barrier," Ada explained calmly, keeping her hands resting inside her pockets as they walked toward the apparel section. "I will need to adjust my entry angle on the triple loop for tonight's performance block at seven, but otherwise, the rink surface is acceptable."

"I know you are going to score top marks tonight," Rebecca said confidently, clutching her shopping bag. "Ashley and Leon are going to lose their minds when you step out there. Ah. The ex and the future coming together. What a drama."

Before Ada could reply, a cold shift in the surrounding air seemed to freeze the space between the vendor stalls.

Walking toward them from the main VIP hallway was a tall, strikingly elegant woman in a tailored dark grey coat. Her blonde hair was styled into a flawless, rigid bun, her dark eyes colder than ice.

The last time Ada had seen those eyes in person was during her father's wake, when Eleanor had simply offered her a crisp calling card and walked away without another word. Ada vividly remembered throwing that calling card straight into the trash bin and scrubbing her hands raw in the sink immediately after.

Seven years had passed.

Eleanor stood few paces away, her gaze sweeping over Ada’s windbreaker, her bare legs, and her red sports shoes with an expression of icy disdain.

Ada didn't flinch. She didn't take her hands out of her windbreaker pockets. She stopped smoothly on the tile floor, her posture perfectly composed, her face a mask of absolute indifference.

Beside Eleanor, an older man in an expensive suit stepped forward, holding a clipboard. Walking right next to him was a girl around Ada’s age, wearing a sleek black track jacket bearing the crest of the rival state academy, identifying her as a competing figure skater. Her blonde hair was pulled into a tight bun, her chin lifted with arrogant pride.

Eleanor spoke first. "So, Albert's creation is still gliding around on ice rinks."

Rebecca blinked, her eyebrows knitting together in immediate confusion as she looked from Eleanor to Ada. "Ex... excuse me?"

Eleanor ignored Rebecca entirely, her cold eyes remaining locked onto Ada’s calm face. "Look at you. Seven years, and you are still clinging to that pathetic program. Albert spent his entire life trying to craft you into something flawless, but in the end, he only managed to build a machine without feelings."

The man in the suit let out a low, mocking chuckle, stepping up beside Eleanor and motioning toward the young skater beside them. "Well, science can only manufacture technical precision, Eleonor. But real artistry requires a human soul."

"Exactly," Eleanor agreed, her voice dripping with venomous condescension. "Compared to Lisa standing right here, you are nothing more than an empty shell, Ada. Lisa has passion, genuine emotion, and the drive of a true champion. Tonight, everyone in that arena will see the vast difference between a living artist and a heartless ghost."

The girl named Lisa smirked, crossing her arms over her chest as she looked Ada up and down. "I saw your practice edge work earlier, Wong. It was mechanically accurate, but completely lifeless. You skate like a programmed clock."

The man chimed in again, fueling the hostility with an arrogant toss of his head. "We all know how Albert raised her. Cold, distant, and completely isolated. It's honestly embarrassing that your university chose you as their primary representative for the regional match."

Through the entire, brutal verbal assault, Ada remained completely still. She didn't raise her voice. She didn't narrow her eyes. Her hands stayed comfortably tucked deep inside the pockets of her crimson windbreaker, her posture relaxed, her dark eyes tracking Eleanor’s face with boredom.

However, standing right beside her, Rebecca’s face was rapidly turning bright, dangerous red. Her hazel eyes flared with intense fury, her small hands clutching her paper shopping bag so tightly the handles groaned.

Rebecca took a sharp step forward, pointing her finger directly at the man and Eleanor. "You know," Rebecca snapped out, her voice loud, fierce, and ringing clearly across the merchandise hall, "you two talk an awful lot about emotion and rationality, but right now, you are being completely, irrationally emotional! You are standing in the middle of a public lobby screaming insults at a performer because you are terrified she is going to wipe the floor with your precious Lisa tonight!"

Lisa’s smirk instantly vanished, her jaw dropping open in utter shock.

The man’s face flushed dark red. "How dare you speak to us like that, you impudent little—"

Before the situation could escalate any further, Ada smoothly extended her right hand from her windbreaker pocket. She didn't strike, nor did she push. She simply placed her slender palm flat against Rebecca’s chest, gently holding her friend back.

Ada stepped forward half a pace, looking directly into Eleanor’s cold, lifeless eyes with a calm smile.

"If you are quite finished with your theatrical presentation," Ada said, her voice dropping low, remarkably polite that completely cut through the tension, "we still have merchs to purchase before our official event prep begins. Good day, Eleanor."

Without waiting for a single response, Ada turned smoothly on her heel, her red sports shoes gliding gracefully across the floorboards as she gently pulled a trembling, furious Rebecca along with her down the wide corridor, leaving the three stunned figures standing alone near the VIP exit.

They walked in silence for fifty yards until they reached an empty alcove near the main arena glass windows, far away from the noise of the vendor hall.

The second they stopped, Rebecca exploded with righteous anger, her face flushed red, her arms flailing wildly.

"Who do those completely horrible, awful, disgusting people think they are?!" Rebecca fumed, her voice trembling with intense rage as she paced back and forth on the tiles. "To say those terrible things to you right before your big performance?! Calling you a machine?! Saying you don't have feelings?! I have never met anyone so utterly rude and bitter in my entire life! Who was that crazy woman?!"

Ada stood by the tall glass window, the bright afternoon sunlight illuminating her sleek dark bob and the composed lines of her face. She reached into her pocket, her fingers lightly brushing against the tiny teddy bear keychain she had bought for Leon.

She turned her head, looking at Rebecca’s furious, protective face with a soft, incredibly gentle expression.

"Rebecca," Ada said smoothly.

"What?!" Rebecca gasped out, breathing heavily from her outburst.

"That was my mother," Ada revealed softly.

Rebecca froze instantly.

Her arms dropped dead to her sides. Her hazel eyes widened into huge, terrified circles, the breath completely catching in her throat. The color drained from her cheeks as the crushing weight of what had just transpired slammed squarely into her brain, leaving her standing in the alcove in utter, speechless shock.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

"Keep your head up, Ada," Wesker instructed gently, his deep voice echoing softly off the high wooden rafters of the arena. "Do not look down at your feet. Trust your balance." "Daddy! Look! I am doing it!" six years old Ada squealed out with pure, ecstatic delight.

Her tiny blades wobbled precariously on the slick surface, her little arms flailing slightly as she tried to maintain her footing. Wesker didn't let go of her hands for even a single millisecond. He skated backward with fluid grace, effortlessly pulling her along with him across the wide center ice.

Ada let out a bright, ringing giggle, her small feet taking tiny, cautious pushes against the frost. "I am flying, Daddy!"

"You are skating, my little sparrow," Wesker corrected softly, a rare, fond smile touching his lips as he watched her little eyes sparkle with pure joy. "And you are doing it remarkably well for your very first week."

"Will I be able to jump high like the skaters on television?" Ada asked eagerly, her tiny gloved fingers squeezing his hand tightly as she attempted a little glide on two blades.

"You will jump higher than all of them," Wesker promised without a trace of hesitation. "You possess natural elegance, Ada. With practice, you will master every single movement on this ice."

"Mommy said I look pretty in my crimson jacket in her letters," Ada piped up happily, looking up into her father's face with bright, innocent curiosity. "Is Mommy coming to watch me skate today?"

Wesker’s backward movement slowed slightly, his sharp eyes catching the light from the rafters. A gentle softness settled over his features as he lowered his broad frame, dropping down onto one knee right there on the cold ice so he was eye-level with his six years old daughter.

He reached out with a gloved hand, gently tucking a stray strand of dark hair behind her small ear.

"Your mother had an important conference to attend in the city today, Ada," Wesker explained gently, "She could not be here at the rink with us this afternoon."

Little Ada’s bottom lip pouted slightly, her small shoulders drooping in her crimson coat. "Does Mommy not want to see me skate?"

"Never think that," Wesker urged softly, his hands coming to rest firmly against her small waist, keeping her completely balanced on her blades. "Your mommy loves you very much, Ada. More than anything in the world."

"She does?" Ada whispered softly, her dark eyes looking into her father's face.

"She does," Wesker confirmed with conviction. "She works very hard, just as I do. But every single thing she does, she does because she wants you to have the absolute best life possible. She thinks about you constantly. She was the one who personally selected those white skates for you, remember?"

Ada looked down at her tiny white skates, her small face brightening instantly as a wide, happy grin broke across her face. "She picked the silver laces!"

"She did indeed," Wesker chuckled softly, his deep laugh warming the chilly air between them. "She spent two hours finding the exact pair with the softest leather so your feet would not hurt while you practice."

"Mommy loves me," Ada repeated happily, her small feet doing a little happy wiggle on the ice, making her blades scrape softly.

"She loves you deeply," Wesker reaffirmed, standing back up to his full height and extending his hands down to her once again. "Now, show me that forward glide again. When your mother comes home tonight, we will tell her all about how far you skated on your own." "Okay, Daddy!" six years old Ada cheered loudly, her bright giggle echoing through the arena as she grabbed her father's fingers and pushed off the ice with joy.

Ada stared at her own reflection in the mirror, her dark eyes unblinking, her chest rising and falling in slow, controlled breaths.

Why had her father lied to her?

She had pondered that exact question a thousand times over the years, analyzing it with the same analytical precision she applied to her technical edge work on the ice. Why would a man so famously strict, so utterly pragmatic, lie to a six years old child about a woman who had already emotionally packed her bags?

Sometimes, when the night was late and the arena was completely empty, Ada wanted to believe he was simply being hopeful. She wanted to picture Albert Wesker as a husband who desperately wished his home was not fracturing at the seams, holding onto a fragile, beautiful illusion for just a little longer.

Other times, however, when she remembered the slight tremor in his voice as he looked up at the rafters, Ada considered a far more human truth. He was not saying those words to reassure a six years old girl. He was telling himself those words rather than to her, trying to force a lie into reality through sheer strength of will.

Ada let out a soft, barely audible exhale, reaching down to pick up the delicate silver stopwatch that rested on the wooden bench beside her gear bag.

She held the vintage timekeeper in her palm, running her thumb gently across the polished casing. It was Wesker's watch. It was the only item she had kept from his study after he passed away.

Ada looked down at her own attire, her gaze trailing down the silk fabric covering her frame. She was wearing a striking, midnight blue performance dress. The back was low and elegant, the delicate straps crossing gracefully over her shoulders, while thousands of tiny sapphire crystals were hand-stitched along the bodice, catching the ambient light with every slight tilt of her body.

She had never worn blue before.

In her entire competitive figure skating career, from her junior division matches all the way through her university tenure, Ada Wong was known exclusively for wearing bold, vibrant crimsons, deep scarlets, and sleek, dramatic blacks. Crimson was her armor. Crimson was her signature.

Blue was a color cursed by history.

Ada closed her eyes for a brief second, the weight of superstition brushing against her mind. The only few times she had ever worn blue in her life were marked by devastating, permanent endings. She had been wearing blue baby doll dress at thirteen years old on the exact evening her mother walked out the front door and never returned. She had been wearing the same dress three years later, sitting on the edge of her bed, when the paramedics quietly informed her that her father had passed away peacefully in his sleep.

Blue was the color of departure. Blue was the color of loss.

Yet, as she opened her eyes and looked at her reflection, Ada didn't feel fear. She picked up the silver stopwatch, carefully tucking it deep inside the inner lining of her blue performance dress, securing it right over her heart, resting safely beneath the sapphire crystals.

"Ada?" Rebecca’s soft voice cut through the room as the side door opened gently.

Rebecca stepped into the dressing room, wearing her red support team polo, holding a pair of polished white skates in her hands. She stopped near the doorway, her hazel eyes widening into huge, admiring circles as she took in the sight of Ada standing in the midnight blue dress.

"Oh, wow," Rebecca breathed out, her voice filled with awe. "Ada... you look breathtaking. That color... I’ve never seen you wear blue before!"

Ada turned smoothly toward her, a small, subtle smirk touching her lips as she took her white skates from Rebecca’s hands. "A change of pace, Rebecca. Sometimes it is good to surprise the judges."

"Surprise them?" Rebecca laughed warmly, sitting down on the bench beside her. "You are going to completely paralyze them! The sapphire crystals catch the light so beautifully!"

"Is the crowd settled?" Ada asked calmly, slipping her right foot into her skate and pulling the silver laces tight, securing the top hooks with practiced, expert speed.

"Settled?" Rebecca chuckled, shaking her head. "It is packed to the ceiling! And speaking of packed, your personal fan club has officially taken over the front row of the stands."

Ada raised a dark eyebrow, looking up from her skate laces. "Oh?"

"Leon is sitting right in the center," Rebecca revealed with a bright, beaming smile. "He is sitting right between Chris and Carlos. Ashley and her cheer squad are three seats down, holding giant pom-poms. The second I walked past the tunnel, Leon asked me four different times if you were ready."

A soft, genuine warmth spread across Ada’s chest, her dark eyes crinkling slightly at the image of the blue-eyed quarterback waiting anxiously in the front row just to see her glide across the ice.

"He is remarkably impatient," Ada noted smoothly, tying her left skate with a firm, decisive double knot.

"He is completely devoted to you, Ada," Rebecca corrected gently, watching as Ada stood up effortlessly on her blades, testing her ankle support on the rubber floor mats. "Honestly, we all are. Now go out there and show that rival team what real talent looks like."

"Thank you, Rebecca," Ada said softly, offering a rare, gentle nod of gratitude.

Ada walked toward the double doors leading out to the arena tunnel. The cold air from the main rink washed over her skin the moment she pushed through the doors, carrying the scent of frozen water and crisp ozone.

The arena was roaring with sound. Thousands of spectators filled the surrounding bleachers, the bright venue lights creating a massive, glowing oval over the pristine white ice sheet in the center.

Standing right beside the entrance gate, holding a black clip board and wearing his official coaching jacket, was Marvin Branagh, her father's distant cousin and her coach.

Marvin looked up as Ada approached. His sharp, experienced eyes swept over her midnight blue performance dress, and noting the subtle sapphire crystals catching the light. He didn't ask her about her mother. He didn't ask her about the rival team's comments in the lobby. He knew his primary athlete better than anyone.

As she stepped onto the wooden ramp right at the edge of the rink, her blades inches from the slick surface, her coach Marvin greeted her with a simple nod.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

Leon had dragged his entire friend group here. Every single one of them. Chris and Carlos were seated to his left in addition of Piers, the trio holding giant printed posters. Grace was sitting right next to Bela, Daniela, and Cassandra, who had brought massive glitter-encrusted banners that Leon had specifically commissioned from them two nights ago.

"Leon, if you don't stop bouncing, you are going to break the plastic stadium seat!" Carlos yelled over the deafening roar of the crowd, laughing as he adjusted his team jacket.

"I can't stop bouncing, Carlos!" Leon yelled back, his face bright red, his blue eyes wide with excitement. "She is coming out any second!"

"We know, quarterback!" Jill called out from two seats down, holding a high-end digital camera with a massive zoom lens attached. "You have reminded us every thirty seconds for the past two hours!"

"Did everyone set their phone cameras to maximum frame rate?!" Leon demanded loudly, turning around to face the group. "Jill! Claire! Daniela! I need every single angle recorded!"

"My camera is already recording, you hyperactive puppy!" Daniela scoffed playfully, waving her phone in the air. "Cassandra and I spent three hours gluing gold foil to this banner, so you better make sure she sees it!"

"Watch this!" Leon shouted, grabbing the zipper of his crimson windbreaker.

With one swift, violent tug, he pulled his windbreaker wide open and ripped it entirely off his shoulders, throwing it into Chris's lap. Underneath, Leon was wearing a custom-printed black t-shirt featuring a massive photo of Ada’s face, framed by glittering red letters that read NUMBER ONE SKATER.

Carlos let out a scream of pure, hysterical laughter, nearly choking on his soda. "No way! Where did you even get that shirt?!"

"I bought it from the triplets yesterday!" Leon declared proudly, flexing his arms as he pointed directly at his own chest. "Best twenty dollars I ever spent!"

"He literally paid us double to rush the printing process," Cassandra piped up from behind Grace, smirking widely. "He is hopelessly smitten."

"Take photos!" Leon ordered again, waving his hands frantically toward the ice entrance tunnel. "Everyone get your cameras ready right now!"

Suddenly, the house lights dimmed down to a deep, dramatic sapphire blue. A collective gasp rippled through the packed arena as the arena announcer's voice boomed over the surround- sound loudspeakers.

"Representing Wrenwood State University... please welcome... Ada Wong!"

The arena erupted into a deafening wall of cheers, whistles, and applause.

Leon froze where he stood, his breath catching so hard in his chest it felt like a physical strike.

Ada stepped smoothly out onto the gleaming white ice, her white blades gliding without a single whisper of sound across the frozen floor.

The venue lights caught her figure, and the entire front row gasped in utter, breathtaking shock.

She was wearing a striking, midnight blue performance dress. The bodice sparkled with thousands of sapphire crystals that caught every beam of light, cascading down to a sheer, fluid skirt that fluttered around her long, bare legs as she glided toward the center circle. Her dark hair was styled into her signature sharp bob.

The arena commentator’s voice echoed loudly over the press feed speakers located right above the front row bleachers.

"Well, folks, take a look at that!" the lead commentator announced, his tone filled with genuine, unscripted shock. "In all my years covering regional collegiate ice events, this is the absolute first time I have ever seen Ada Wong step onto the competitive rink wearing blue! She is famous for her signature crimson, but tonight... tonight she is making a statement before the music even begins!"

Leon stood at the glass barrier, his fingers gripping the top ledge so tightly his knuckles turned white. His blue eyes tracked every single inch of her frame, his soul completely mesmerized. "She looks... oh my god, she looks incredible..."

Ada reached the center of the ice, coming to an elegant halt. She lifted her chin, her hands resting gracefully at her sides, her eyes flashing with a dangerous confidence as she looked directly toward the main broadcast camera hovering above the ice.

Tell me who I am

Do I provoke you with my tone of innocence?

Don't ask too many questions

That is my one suggestion

Ada moved.

She pushed off her back edge with breathtaking, explosive speed, her blue dress flaring out as she carved a sharp, impossible curve across the ice. She didn't begin with a standard technical sequence. Instead, her entire movement was wildly playful, clean, deeply sultry, and effortlessly confident, teasing the judges and the crowd with every flick of her wrist.

You've got a front row seat, and I

I got a taste of the glamorous life

She glided straight toward the front row barrier where Leon was standing. As she passed within three feet of him, Ada turned her head directly toward the quarterback. She lifted her right hand, playfully blowing a soft, teasing kiss straight into the air toward his face, before delivering a slow, wicked wink.

Leon’s brain completely short-circuited. His knees went entirely weak, his head lolling backward as he stumbled two paces back into the crowd.

"Whoa! Whoa! Hold him up!" Jill shrieked, her and Claire instantly grabbing Leon by his shoulders and dragging his heavy frame down into his plastic seat before he could collapse onto the floor.

"She blew a kiss!" Leon gasped out, his face completely flushed, his eyes rolling back in bliss. "She looked right at me and blew a kiss!"

"Stay seated, quarterback, or you're going to pass out before the jump sequence!" Chris yelled, laughing as he shoved Leon back toward the glass.

Out on the ice, Ada was putting on a absolute masterclass of technical dominance. She launched herself into a triple axel, rotating in the air with lightning speed, landing on a single blade with fluid, feather-light precision, flowing seamlessly into a sequence of complex, seemingly impossible edge turns that left the crowd screaming.

The press commentator’s voice cracked with utter amazement over the speakers.

"Unbelievable!" the commentator shouted loudly over the crowd noise. "Look at that edge work! It is the absolute first time Ada Wong has ever moved around the ice with this level of expressive, playful freedom! The technical precision is flawless, but the emotion... the sheer attitude! Ladies and gentlemen, that right there is what an absolute legend looks like on ice!"

Ada spun into the center circle, her body bending back into a breathtaking layback spin, her dark hair sweeping against her shoulders as the sapphire crystals on her blue dress blurred into a blinding ring of light.

And when you shame me

It makes me want it more

It makes me want it more, more

The lyrics bounced off the rafters, echoing the exact victory Ada was securing over the bitter words her mother had thrown at her earlier in the lobby. She didn't look like a machine. She looked like a goddess of fire and ice, reclaiming her story with every strike of her blade.

She broke out of the spin with explosive power, gliding backward toward the judge panel, her arms extending gracefully as the music reached its emotional climax.

Nothing makes me feel as good

As being loved by you

Nothing makes me feel as good

As being loved by you

Ada lifted her dark eyes, looking past the judges, locking her gaze directly onto Leon’s bright blue eyes in the front row. The playful, dangerous smirk on her lips melted away for a brief, beautiful second, replaced by a soft, radiant, incredibly genuine smile meant for him.

Love is a drug that I can't deny

I'm your dream girl, but you're not my type

You've got a front row seat, and I

I got a taste of the glamorous life

She executed her final element, launching into a breathtaking double toe loop combination that launched her high into the air, spinning effortlessly beneath the bright venue lights. She came down with absolute perfection, her blade cutting a sharp white line into the frost as she slid into her final, striking pose. One hand rested on her waist, her chin lifted, her breath coming in soft puffs as the final beat of the song echoed into silence.

For a split second, the entire seven-thousand-seat arena stood in complete, stunned silence. Then, the stadium exploded.

"YES!" Leon screamed at the absolute top of his lungs, leaping over the bleacher seat, his voice cracking with passion. "YES! THAT'S MY GIRL! THAT IS ADA WONG!"

The entire crowd rose to their feet in a roaring standing ovation. Plush toys, flower bouquets, and crimson banners rained down over the glass barriers onto the ice. Chris, Carlos, Jill, Claire, Grace, and the Dimitrescu triplets were all screaming, clapping, and banging on the glass in utter triumph.

Out on the ice center, the giant arena camera panned in extremely close to Ada’s face, broadcasting her image onto the four massive jumbotron screens hanging above the rink.

Ada was standing in her midnight blue dress, her chest rising and falling as she looked up at the cheering crowd, a brilliant, breathtaking, genuinely happy smile spreading across her face.

And there, captured crystal-clear on the giant jumbotron screens for the entire stadium to see, a single, delicate tear slipped through her dark eyes, gliding down her soft cheek in the bright arena lights, shining like a liquid diamond.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

Ada stood motionless in the center of the pristine white ice, her breath coming in deep, rapid puffs that formed small white clouds of vapor in the chilled atmosphere. Her chest rose and fell beneath her bodice. Her sharp bob framed her face, strands of dark hair clinging slightly to her flushed skin as her body gradually eased out of her striking closing posture.

The crowd was a blur of waving banners, flashing camera phones, and falling flower bouquets. Yet, as Ada slowly lifted her chin to sweep her dark eyes across the towering rows of the arena, her focus bypassed the front row where Leon was screaming her name. It bypassed the judges' table, the television cameras, and the sea of red university jackets.

Her gaze traveled all the way up to the very top edge of the highest bleachers, right beside the dimly lit red emergency exit sign.

Standing there, bathed in the soft glow of the exit light, was a tall, broad-shouldered man wearing a tailored dark wool coat.

Ada’s breath hitched in her throat, her dark eyes widening as her heart gave a strange, powerful thud against her ribs.

Wesker stood at the highest point of the stadium, looking straight down at her across the vast distance of the arena. His posture was rigid, flawless, and commanding, exactly as she remembered it from her childhood. He didn't smile, nor did he wave a flag. He simply stood there on the top step, raising his hands, slowly and deliberately clapping for his daughter.

Ada stared, completely frozen on her blades. A single, hot tear slipped from her eye, gliding softly down her cheek, catching the arena lights like a tiny liquid diamond.

"Daddy..."

Albert gave her one final, subtle nod of absolute approval. Then, he turned his back, stepped through the open emergency exit door, and completely vanished into the corridor beyond.

"Ada! Ada, look at the board!"

The hysterical screaming of her teammates shattered the spell.

Ada blinked, her vision clearing as a wave of university skaters in matching crimson windbreakers came pouring out onto the ice through the side gate, their blades scraping wildly across the frost. They rushed toward her like a tidal wave, screaming her name at the absolute top of their lungs.

High above the rink, the massive digital scoreboard flashed in bright, glowing gold letters:

1ST PLACE: ADA WONG - 218.45 (NEW REGIONAL RECORD)

"You won! You broke the record!" Rebecca squealed out at the top of her lungs, practically throwing her arms around Ada's neck as the rest of the figure skating squad surrounded her in a tight, bouncing circle.

"That performance was legendary, Wong!" Coach Marvin called out from the ice barrier, clapping his hands with immense, unadulterated pride. "Absolute perfection!"

The entire stadium was chanting in unison now, a deafening, rhythmic wave of noise that vibrated through the floorboards.

"ADA WONG! ADA WONG! ADA WONG!"

Over at the front row glass barrier, Leon was leaping into the air, his custom t-shirt on full display as Carlos and Chris pounded his back in wild celebration. "SHE WON! THAT'S MY GIRL! SHE DID IT!"

Ada stood in the center of the cheering crowd, her smile polite, graceful, and perfectly composed, though her mind was spinning at a million miles an hour. Her eyes flicked back toward the top bleachers near the exit door. It was completely empty now.

"Thank you, everyone," Ada said smoothly as she gently patted Rebecca’s shoulder. "Thank you so much."

"Ada, the news cameras want an interview near the medal podium!" a university official called out, waving a clipboard frantically from the gate.

"Give me three minutes," Ada requested politely, her dark eyes flashing with a sharp, decisive focus. "I need to fetch my warm-up jacket from the backstage corridor."

"Of course! Take your time, champion!" the official cheered.

Ada didn't wait for another word. She turned on her blades, gliding with swift, incredible speed toward the side exit gate. She stepped onto the black rubber matting, bypassing the media cameras and the cheering crowd, her barefoot hitting the floorboards as she rushed straight toward the private backstage hallway that led up to the upper level exits.

She pushed through the double doors, her midnight blue dress fluttering as she hurried down the dimly lit concrete corridor. Her heart was beating fast against her ribs, her mind fixated on reaching the top floor exit before the man in the dark coat could reach the parking lot.

"Ada! Wait!"

Ada halted her fast pace near the equipment storage alcove, her dark eyes cutting sideways.

Stepping out from behind a row of large rolling equipment trunks was Luis Serra. He was wearing a dark leather jacket over a white collared shirt, his wavy dark hair slightly unkempt, his usual charming, crooked smirk resting on his lips. He was holding a small rolled-up paper blueprint in one hand, looking slightly out of breath as if he had been searching for her through the building.

"Luis?" Ada asked, her eyebrows knitting together in slight surprise as she adjusted her posture. "What are you doing backstage? The spectator access is restricted to the main concourse."

"Ah, you know me, chica," Luis chuckled smoothly, taking two easy steps forward, his boots making a soft sound against the concrete. "Rules are more like polite suggestions when I need to deliver important news."

"I am in a hurry, Luis," Ada stated firmly as she glanced toward the stairwell leading to the top bleachers. "The official award ceremony begins in five minutes, and I have somewhere I need to be."

"I saw the score on the monitor near the lounge," Luis noted, his dark eyes sweeping over her midnight blue dress, the sapphire crystals sparkling under the corridor lights. "First place. A new regional record. You were spectacular out there, Ada. Truly. I have never seen you skate like that."

"Thank you," Ada replied politely, stepping to the side to bypass him. "Now if you will excuse me—"

"Ada, wait, listen to me for one second," Luis interrupted, his tone shifting from casual banter into something strangely intense, urgent, and uncharacteristically serious. He stepped directly into her path, blocking the narrow corridor. "I came here because I needed to tell you something before the night ends. Before the team leaves for the state tournament next month."

Ada paused, her dark eyes narrowing slightly as she studied his face. "What are you talking about?"

"I am leaving the university program, Ada," Luis revealed softly, looking down at her with a look of genuine, unresolved emotion. "My research project was approved by the European board. I am flying to Madrid at the end of the week."

Ada blinked, her composure remaining perfectly intact, though a mild surprise touched her features. "Congratulations, Luis. That is a significant opportunity for your career."

"I don't care about the board, Ada," Luis said, his voice dropping into a raspy murmur as he took another step closer, closing the distance between them until he was standing right in front of her. "I care about what I am leaving behind. I spent the last two years watching you from the sidelines. Watching you keep everyone at arm's length. Watching you play the cold, untouchable queen."

"Luis," Ada warned softly, her voice cool and dangerous. "Do not do this."

"I thought nobody could ever reach you," Luis continued, his eyes searching her calm face with desperate intensity. "I thought you were completely unreachable. But then I saw the way you looked at that football player tonight. I saw the way you smiled at him from the ice."

Ada’s posture went completely rigid. "My personal life is entirely outside the scope of your concern."

"I just need you to know," Luis whispered, his voice thick with a strange, sudden desperation, "that I would have given you the world if you had let me in first."

Before Ada could step back, before her sharp reflexes could even process the sudden shift in his posture, Luis reached out. His broad hand caught her gently by the nape of her neck, pulling her frame forward.

Ada’s dark eyes widened in shock as Luis leaned down and pressed his lips firmly against hers right there in the empty, dimly lit backstage corridor.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

Leon stood at the mouth of the hallway, a large bottle of water held loosely in his left hand. His blue eyes, wide and shining with the electric residue of joy, froze in their sockets.

He had slipped away from Chris and Carlos the second the score flashed on the giant screen. He had run past the security guards, dodging around media cameras and athletic staff, his custom t-shirt still visible where his crimson windbreaker hung loose over his shoulders. He had wanted to be the very first person to hug her when she walked off the ice. He had wanted to tell her that she was the most breathtaking, magnificent human being to ever step onto a frozen floor.

Instead, his vision caught the sight of a man in a dark leather jacket leaning over her, his broad hand resting near the nape of her neck, pressing a kiss firmly against her lips in the empty space between two storage lockers.

Leon’s heart simply went numb, dropping like a lead weight straight through his chest into the pit of his stomach.

He didn't yell nor throw the kind of loud, messy tantrum just like just three days ago. He had promised himself he would grow up. He had promised himself he would be better for her, that he wouldn't let his childish insecurities drive a stake between them every time someone stood too close to her space.

Leon took a step back, his sneakers making no sound against the rubber floor runner.

A single, hot tear escaped his lashes, trailing a wet path down his flushed cheek, followed instantly by another. He blinked rapidly, his lower lip trembling as he looked down at his own shoes. He felt small. He felt utterly, completely ridiculous standing there in a custom shirt with her face printed on the cotton, holding a plastic water bottle like a loyal dog waiting for a treat.

He turned on his heel, his tall frame bending slightly as he began walking away, heading back down the corridor toward the side parking exit.

"Leon?"

The sound of her voice cut through the hum of the arena like a silver blade.

Leon stopped dead in his tracks, six paces away from the double doors. He didn't turn around immediately. He pressed his palms against his thighs, squeezing his eyes shut as he tried frantically to wipe the wetness from his face with the back of his sleeve.

Behind him, the soft sound of bare skin pressing against cold concrete echoed through the hall. Ada walked toward him, her midnight blue performance dress swaying gently around her bare knees, the sapphire crystals catching the pale fluorescent light of the ceiling fixtures.

"Leon," Ada repeated. She took two fast steps toward his back, her bare soles cold against the floorboards. "What are you doing down here?"

Leon took a slow breath, his broad shoulders rising and falling before he turned his head sideways, keeping his face turned slightly away from her sharp dark eyes. "I... I was just bringing you some water, Ada. Coach said you needed to stay hydrated before the press conference."

Ada stopped three feet away from him, her posture composed, but her dark eyes narrowed as she studied the tense, rigid posture of his spine. "Look at me, Leon."

"I'm fine, Ada," Leon said softly, his voice raspy and thick.

"Leon," Ada commanded gently, her tone leaving no room for refusal. "Turn around and look at me."

Leon swallowed hard, turning his full body around to face her.

The pale fluorescent light illuminated his face, showing his red-rimmed eyes, his wet cheeks, and the helpless, heartbroken expression that he was trying so desperately to hide behind a weak, trembling smile.

Ada’s breath hitched in her throat. Her dark eyes softened instantly, her composure faltering as she took another step forward, her slender hand reaching out toward his arm. "Leon... what is this? What happened?"

Leon immediately took a quick step back, his boots retreating across the rubber runner, keeping that gap firmly between them. "Don't... don't come closer, Ada. Please."

Ada halted, her hand hovering in the empty air between them before she slowly lowered it back to her side. "Leon, talk to me. What is going on in your head right now?"

Leon let out a strange, wet sound that sat somewhere between a bitter laugh and a choked sob. He shook his head, running his broad hand through his messy blonde hair, his fingers tangling into the strands as he looked up at the concrete ceiling before looking back at her.

"I am just... I am so stupid, Ada," Leon said, a breathless, broken chuckle escaping his lips as fresh tears spilled over his lower lashes. "Seriously. I fucking mean it."

"You are not stupid, Leon," Ada said firmly, as he stood there trembling in his varsity clothes. "Stop saying that."

"No, I am!" Leon insisted, his voice cracking on the high note as he laughed again, a sad, self-deprecating sound that made Ada's chest tighten. "I am standing here... wearing this shirt... I dragged the whole squad here... I screamed until my throat hurt because I thought... god, I don't even know what I thought."

"Leon—"

"I am trying, Ada," Leon interrupted softly, stepping back another half-inch as he wiped his eyes with his sleeve, his tone dripping with an overwhelming, gentle vulnerability that was far more painful than any anger. "I swear to you, I am really trying to understand. I am trying to figure out if maybe... maybe I'm just the problem here. Maybe I am just expecting things that aren't mine to expect. Maybe I'm just overwhelming you with all of this... with my feelings, with my noise, with everything."

Ada stood frozen on her bare feet, her dark eyes wide, her lips parted slightly as she listened to him tear his own heart apart right in front of her. "Leon, that isn't true—"

"Stop," Leon whispered gently, holding up a single hand, his blue eyes looking into hers with devotion despite the tears streaming down his face. "Please, Ada. Just let me say it. I don't want to throw another scene. I promised myself I wouldn't ruin your big night. You just set a regional record. You were... you were absolute perfection out there. You looked like an angel on the ice, Ada. Truly."

Ada’s heart slammed against her ribs, her hands curling into tight fists at her sides as she stared at the tall quarterback.

"I love you," Leon said softly, the three words falling from his lips with complete, effortless honesty, free of any demands, expectations, or anger. "I love you so much, Ada. I think I've loved you since the day I saw you. I know that shit sounds crazy but it's true. I really do love you....I love you in ways I can't put into words...because maybe I'm that kind of foolish guy...that with all this loud mouth could say...I still can't...I still...And...And I just... I needed you to know that before you go out there to get your medal."

Ada went completely, utterly still.

The world around them seemed to stop entirely. The distant roar of the stadium crowd, the hum of the ice cooling unit, the echo of the media cameras—everything faded into absolute insignificance.

Ada Wong, who had an answer for every question, who could read every opponent's strategy before they even stepped onto the rink, stood completely mute, her mind blank, her chest aching with a feeling so massive and terrifying that she couldn't formulate a single word.

She stared at him, her dark eyes glossy, her lips trembling slightly as she looked into his blue eyes.

Leon looked down at her bare feet standing on the freezing concrete floor, his expression softening into that familiar, sweet concern that was so inherently 'him'.

Dad...

"Your feet are going to freeze, Ada," Leon murmured softly, his voice steadying slightly.

Before she could react, Leon slowly dropped down onto one knee right there on the rubber runner in front of her. He reached into his deep windbreaker pocket, pulling out a clean, thick pair of white athletic socks he had packed in his gym bag earlier that morning.

Gently, Leon cupped her cold heel in his broad palm, sliding the warm white sock over her small foot, pulling it up securely over her ankle. He reached for her other foot, sliding the second sock on with the exact same tenderness, ensuring her feet were completely covered and warm against the chilly floor.

Ada looked down at the top of his blonde head, her throat tight, a lump forming in her neck so large she couldn't breathe.

Leon stood back up to his full height. He didn't step away this time. He stepped directly into her space, closing the distance between them until his broad chest was inches from her face.

Slowly, Leon reached up with both of his large trembling, warm hands, gently framing the sides of her face, his thumbs lightly stroking over her cheekbones to brush away the stray moisture from her skin.

He leaned down, pressing his lips softly, tenderly, and deeply against the center of her forehead, holding the kiss while Ada closed her eyes, her entire body trembling beneath his touch.

He pulled back just an inch, his blue eyes shining down into hers with warmth and gentle understanding.

"Go back to the rink, champion," Leon whispered, his voice full of genuine pride, a soft, beautiful smile finally breaking across his lips. "Your team is waiting for you. Go get your medal. It's okay. Everything is going to be okay."

He gently let go of her face, offered her one final look, turned on his heel, and walked calmly down the corridor toward the exit doors, leaving Ada standing alone in her midnight blue dress, wearing his warm white socks, her heart completely shattered and remade in the quiet hall.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

The warm, sweet scent of lavender fabric softener drifted through the small laundry room, mingling with the fresh humidity of soapy water spinning inside the white washing machine.

Six years old Ada sat cross-legged on top of a low wooden step stool near the folding table, her tiny knees pulled up to her chest inside her favorite bright red overalls. Her dark hair was styled into two small, neat pigtails that bounced slightly every time she shifted her weight. Her dark eyes, wide and full of endless, innocent curiosity, tracked every single movement of the tall man standing beside the appliance.

Wesker had his sleeves rolled up to his elbows, revealing his muscular forearms as he carefully sorted a stack of white cotton towels. He folded each towel with precise, crisp edges before tossing it smoothly into the open metal drum of the washing machine.

"Daddy?" six years old Ada called out, her voice high, sweet, and chirpy over the hum of the running water.

"Yes, my little sparrow?" Wesker replied, not breaking his methodical pace as he reached for a blue pillowcase.

"How did you and Mommy fall in love?" Ada asked, swinging her little feet back and forth off the edge of the step stool. "Was it like in the fairytale books where the prince rides a huge white horse into the castle?"

Wesker paused for a brief second, his hand resting on the rim of the washing machine door. A subtle, rare twitch of amusement touched the corner of his lips as he looked over his shoulder at his small daughter.

"A white horse?" Wesker echoed softly,"No, Ada. I can assure you there were no horses involved in our introduction."

"Then how did it happen?!" Ada insisted, leaning forward on her stool with her hands pressed against her cheeks, her dark eyes shining with eager anticipation. "Tell me the story! Please, Daddy! Tell me!"

Wesker let out a soft, low sigh, though his sharp eyes sparkled with warmth. He turned around, leaning his broad frame back against the edge of the vibrating washing machine, crossing his arms over his chest as he looked down at her small, expectant face.

"It was not a fairytale, Ada," Wesker began smoothly. "It happened at a very cold, very serious science conference in Geneva many years ago. Your mother was presenting a complex paper on molecular biology, and I was sitting in the front row, completely convinced that my own research was superior to hers."

Ada gasped softly, her little hands covering her mouth. "You were being mean?!"

"I was being competitive," Wesker corrected mildly, a soft smirk gracing his features. "After her lecture finished, I walked straight up to her podium to point out minor mathematical inconsistencies in her second chapter."

"And what did Mommy do?!" Ada squeaked out, her pigtails bouncing as she wiggled in excitement.

"Your mother did not cry, nor did she back down," Wesker said, his voice dropping into a tender register as he remembered the past. "She looked me dead in the eye, adjusted her glasses, and spent the next forty-five minutes politely proving to me that my own calculations were completely wrong."

Ada let out a bright, ringing giggle, clapping her tiny hands together. "Mommy beat you!"

"She did indeed," Wesker admitted without a single shred of hesitation, smiling down at his daughter. "She was sharp, brilliant, and completely unbothered by my reputation. By the time we walked out of that lecture hall to get coffee, I knew I had met my absolute match. We spent six hours sitting in a small cafe talking about chemistry, medicine, and the future. That was the exact afternoon we fell in love."

"Ohhh!" six years old Ada squealed softly, her cheeks turning rosy with delight as she kicked her little feet against the wooden stool. "Daddy, that is so cuteee!"

Wesker reached out with a broad, warm hand, his fingers lightly booping the tip of her small nose, making her let out another happy, high-pitched giggle.

"It was practical, Ada," Wesker noted softly. "But if you think that story is cute, I must inform you that you are infinitely cuter."

"I am?!" Ada beamed, puffing out her chest in her red overalls with immense pride.

"You are the absolute cutest thing in this entire house," Wesker confirmed smoothly, turning back to the laundry basket to grab the last remaining white towel. He tossed it into the machine, closing the glass door with a solid click, before pressing the start button on the digital control panel.

The washing machine hummed to life, soapy water cascading over the spinning clothes inside.

Wesker pulled up a small wooden chair, sitting down right in front of his six years old daughter so they were eye-level. He rested his broad hands on his knees, his sharp dark eyes becoming deeply focused as he looked into her innocent face.

"Ada," Wesker spoke softly, his voice cut through the cozy hum of the laundry room. "I want you to listen to me very carefully."

Ada stopped swinging her feet, her little dark eyes locking onto her father's face with complete, undivided attention. "Okay, Daddy."

"One day," Wesker began, "you are going to grow up. You are going to step out into the world, and someone is going to fall deeply in love with you."

Ada tilted her head slightly, her eyebrows knitting together in cute confusion. "Like a prince?"

"Perhaps," Wesker allowed with a tiny smile. "Or perhaps just someone who looks at you the way I look at you now. And when that day comes, my little sparrow, you might find yourself feeling overwhelmed. You might not know how to say what you feel out loud. You might freeze, or protect yourself, or stay silent. And I need you to know right now that it is completely okay if you cannot find the words immediately."

Ada reached out, her tiny hand sliding into her father's broad palm. "It is?"

"It is," Wesker affirmed firmly, closing his large fingers gently around her small hand. "Expressing deep emotion is a terrifying. But listen to me, Ada. Just because it is okay to struggle with your feelings does not mean you should ever settle for less than complete, total devotion."

"What does settle mean, Dad?" Ada asked softly.

Wesker looked deeply into her eyes, leaning in slightly closer. "It means you must never accept half-hearted love, Ada. You must remember this rule for the rest of your life. Words mean nothing without actions, and actions mean nothing without words."

Ada repeated the phrase slowly in her little voice, her mind trying to process the big concepts. "Words mean nothing... without actions?"

"Exactly," Wesker nodded approvingly. "If someone tells you they love you a thousand times, but their actions show disrespect, distance, or carelessness, those words are completely worthless. They are just empty air."

"And the other part?" Ada prompted, squeezing his thumb.

"And if someone performs grand actions for you, but refuses to speak their truth, refuses to be honest, or leaves you guessing where you stand, those actions lose their meaning as well," Wesker explained patiently. "True, undeniable love requires both. It requires the courage to speak the truth, and the discipline to prove those words every single day through kindness, patience, and loyalty. Do you understand, my little sparrow?"

Six years old Ada nodded her head vigorously, her dark pigtails swaying back and forth against her shoulders. "Both things! Words and actions together!"

"Both things," Wesker confirmed as he reached out and lifted her effortlessly off the wooden stool, pulling her small frame into a warm, secure hug against his chest. "You are a brilliant girl, Ada. Never forget your worth."

"I won't, Daddy!" Ada cheered, wrapping her tiny arms around his neck, burying her face into his collar as the warm, cozy hum of the washing machine filled the peaceful room.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

Ada sat motionless on the edge of the plush mattress. Her dark hair was neatly styled into her sharp bob, but her dark eyes were unblinking, fixed entirely on the small item resting in the palm of her hand.

It was the tiny brown teddy bear keychain wearing the oversized navy hoodie with the number seven printed on the chest.

For five continuous hours, Ada had sat in the faint light of her bedside lamp, simply staring at the plush toy. She ran her thumb over its clumsy plastic eyes, feeling the soft cotton fabric of its tiny hood, remembering the exact sound of Leon’s breathless laughter when he had stood before her in the cold equipment corridor.

I love you.

The three words echoed in her mind with clarity, ringing louder than the thunderous cheers of seven thousand spectators. He had given her those words without demanding a single thing in return. He had knelt on the freezing concrete, placed his warm socks over her bare feet, kissed her forehead, and walked away so she could collect her gold medal.

Ada’s slender fingers tightened around the metal ring of the keychain.

She was going to find him. She was going to see him, look into his bright blue eyes, and say every single word she should have said the night before.

Ada pulled on her outfit for the day, selecting a pair of dark short shorts that showcased her long legs, pairing them with her black knee-length leather boots. To finish the look, she reached deep into her gear trunk, pulling out a vintage black leather jacket that belonged to Wesker. The jacket was slightly oversized on her frame, the rich scent of aged leather and faint cologne still lingering in the lining. She slid her arms into the sleeves, pulling the silver zipper completely up to her collar, sealing herself inside her father's strength.

She slid the teddy bear keychain safely into her jacket pocket, pulled open her room door, and stepped out into the morning air.

The university football stadium was already roaring with energy at eight o'clock in the morning. The bright morning sun beat down on the lush green turf, illuminating the giant scoreboard that displayed the regional opener match between the visiting Wrenwood Wolves and the home state team.

The opening kickoff was scheduled early, and the surrounding bleachers were packed with thousands of shouting students, cheerleaders, and alumni waving school flags.

Ada walked briskly down the wide concourse behind the bleachers, her knee-length black boots making a sound against the concrete. Her dark eyes swept over the crowds of spectators, searching through the sea of university jackets for the familiar faces of the football support squad.

Near the concession stands, she spotted a group of players and athletic staff standing around a high-top table.

Carlos was leaning against the metal railing, holding a plastic sports bottle while talking animatedly with Ethan, who was checking a clipboard crammed with offensive stat sheets.

"I am telling you, Ethan," Carlos was saying, gesturing with his free hand, "if the defensive line doesn't plug the middle gap on third down, we are going to be running backwards all morning!"

"Coach Burton already adjusted the coverage," Ethan replied, adjusting his jacket collar. "We just need the offense to execute."

Ada stepped out of the crowd, walking straight toward the table with an unhurried, commanding presence. "Carlos."

Both men turned their heads instantly.

Carlos blinked in absolute surprise, his eyes widening as he took in the sight of Ada. He immediately raised a hand, stopping Ethan in mid-sentence.

"Hey, Ethan, give me two minutes," Carlos said quickly, offering a apologetic nod to his friend. "I need to talk to Ada."

Ethan looked between them, nodded, and stepped away toward the locker room tunnel with his clipboard.

Carlos turned back to Ada, setting his sports bottle down on the table. His usual laid-back, teasing demeanor was noticeably absent, replaced by a serious, watchful expression.

"Ada," Carlos greeted softly, crossing his arms over his chest. "I didn't expect to see you here so early. Your medal ceremony was late last night."

"Where is Leon?" Ada asked directly.

Carlos let out a long, slow breath, rubbing the back of his neck as he looked out toward the field tunnel. "He's in the locker room with Coach Burton and Chris. They are doing final equipment checks before the team walks out onto the field."

"I need to speak with him, Carlos," Ada stated firmly, taking a half-step forward.

Carlos looked down at her, a gentle, sympathetic expression softening his features. He shook his head slowly, his voice dropping into a low register meant only for her.

"Ada... look," Carlos began gently, picking his words with immense care. "Leon came back to the room late last night. He didn't say a word to anyone. He just sat on his bed, stared at the floor, and then went to sleep with his uniform laid out beside him. He looked completely crushed."

Ada’s chest tightened, a pang of guilt cutting through her composure.

"I don't know what happened between you two after your performance," Carlos continued, looking into her eyes with genuine warmth. "Leon didn't tell us anything, and we didn't push him because we know how much he cares about you. But today is one of the biggest game of his collegiate career. He needs his head in the game, Ada."

Ada remained completely silent, her slender hands resting inside the deep side pockets of her father's leather jacket.

"I really hope you two fix whatever is broken," Carlos added earnestly, offering her a small, supportive smile. "Because that guy is completely crazy about you. But right now, he's about to step onto that field, and he's fighting a massive battle."

"Thank you, Carlos," Ada murmured softly, "I understand."

Carlos nodded, picked up his sports bottle, and turned to walk down the tunnel toward the locker rooms, leaving Ada standing alone beside the concourse railing.

Twenty minutes later, the referee's whistle blew, launching the regional tournament opener into absolute motion.

Ada sat near the middle section of the primary grandstands, surrounded by hundreds of shouting fans. She kept her leather jacket zipped to her throat, her dark eyes fixed entirely on the number seven jersey moving across the green turf below.

From the very first play, it was painfully clear that something was terribly off.

The Wrenwood Wolves were failing miserably.

Leon was playing with an mechanical, distant stiffness that lacked all of his usual brilliant, instinctual magic. His passes were slightly off-target, his read-option decisions were slow, and his positioning looked sluggish. By the middle of the second quarter, the home team had built a crushing fourteen-point lead, intercepting one of Leon's long passes near the endzone and sacking him twice behind the scrimmage line.

The crowd around Ada was groaning in utter frustration.

"What is wrong with Kennedy today?!" a student sitting three rows behind Ada yelled out, throwing his hands in the air. "He looks like he's sleepwalking out there!"

"He's missing every single open receiver!" another fan shouted angrily. "If Coach Burton doesn't pull him out, we are going to lose the entire regional tournament in the first half!"

Down on the sidelines, Coach Burton was pacing back and forth furiously, slamming his clipboard against his leg, while Chris held his hands to his head in sheer disbelief. Leon stood on the sideline near the bench, his helmet in his hands, his blonde hair soaked in sweat, his blue eyes staring blankly at the turf with defeated expression.

Ada watched him from her seat, her dark eyes reflecting the harsh morning sunlight. Her heart ached seeing him like this, carrying the burden of his love and his heartbreak while his team crumbled around him.

She stood up from her plastic stadium seat.

Ada reached for the silver pull tab of her father's black leather jacket. With one swift movement, she unzipped the jacket completely, ripping it off her shoulders and letting it fall loosely over her arm.

Underneath, Ada was wearing the exact same t-shirt Leon had worn the night before—a custom black shirt featuring a huge photo of Leon’s face framed by glittering gold letters that read NUMBER ONE QUARTERBACK and signed by him.

A collective gasp echoed from the fans sitting near her as Ada stepped out into the central aisle.

She walked purposefully down the concrete stairs toward the front row barrier where their cheer section was gathered. Ashley was standing on a wooden bench holding a giant silver megaphone, looking utterly panicked as she watched the scoreboard.

Ada walked straight up to Ashley, extending her hand.

"Give me the megaphone, Ashley," Ada commanded smoothly.

Ashley blinked in utter shock, looking from Ada's striking face down to the t-shirt with Leon's photo, before her jaw dropped open. "Ada?! What are you—"

"Give me the megaphone," Ada repeated, her dark eyes flashing with a fierce, burning light that left no room for argument.

Ashley instantly handed over the silver device without saying another word.

Ada turned to face the entire stadium, stepping right onto the top edge of the concrete barrier wall, balancing effortlessly in her black knee-length boots like a performer on the ice rink. She lifted the silver megaphone to her lips, pressed the power switch, and let her voice ring out across the entire seven-thousand-seat venue.

"WRENWOOD WOLVES!" Ada shouted, her voice booming over the speakers with clarity.

The loud, unexpected sound echoed off the steel rafters, making thousands of fans stop shouting and turn their heads toward the front row.

Down on the field, Leon's head snapped up instantly. His blue eyes widened into massive, disbelieving circles as he looked toward the sideline wall.

Ada stood on the barrier wall, the wind catching her dark bob, her long legs displayed in her short shorts, wearing the shirt with his face printed across her chest.

She pressed the megaphone back to her lips, her voice ringing out with fierce, proud, undeniable passion.

"WRENWOOD WOLVES! STAND UP AND CHEER FOR YOUR QUARTERBACK!" Ada bellowed across the stadium. "CHEER FOR LEON KENNEDY!"

Ashley immediately caught the spark, leaping onto her bench and waving her pom-poms wildly. "LET'S GO WRENWOOD! LET'S GO WOLVES!"

Jill and Claire joined in instantly from two rows back, screaming at the top of their lungs, while Carlos and Chris began pounding their fists against the team bench. Within seconds, the infectious, electric surge of energy swept through the entire grandstand like a massive wildfire.

Slowly at first, then with overwhelming, deafening force, the entire bleachers erupted into a unified, roaring chant that shook the stadium foundation.

"LEON! LEON! LEON! LEON!"

Down on the field, Leon stood completely frozen near the sideline bench. He looked up at Ada standing on the wall, her dark eyes locked directly onto his face, her smile radiant, proud, and completely devoted to him in front of seven thousand people.

A massive surge of adrenaline exploded through Leon’s entire body. The fog in his mind vanished instantly, replaced by a fierce, burning fire. A grin broke across his sweat-streaked face.

He shoved his helmet back over his blonde hair, snapped his chin strap tight, and sprinted back out onto the center of the field like a man reborn.

What followed for the remaining two quarters was nothing short of an absolute athletic miracle.

Leon played with perfection that left the opposing defense completely helpless. He carved through their coverage with razor-sharp passes, executed brilliant read-option plays that left the linebackers scrambling, and threw three consecutive touchdown passes that flipped the scoreboard entirely on its head.

With only fifteen seconds left on the game clock in the fourth quarter, the Wrenwood Wolves held a narrow two-point lead. The opposing team launched one final, desperate pass toward the endzone.

Leon dropped back into coverage, leaped high into the air, caught the football firmly against his chest, and slammed down onto the turf as the final buzzer sounded across the stadium.

GAME OVER: WRENWOOD WOLVES WIN - 28 to 26

The entire stadium erupted into a chaotic, hysterical wall of deafening cheers.

Down on the sideline, Ada stood quietly by the glass wall, holding her father's vintage silver stopwatch in her right hand. As the final horn echoed into silence, Ada pressed her thumb down firmly on the top winding button, stopping the time watch in her hand with a satisfying click.

Her time of waiting was officially over.

The field gate swung open as hundreds of cheering fans, cheerleaders, and teammates poured onto the turf to celebrate the massive championship victory. Chris and Carlos lifted Leon onto their shoulders, the entire team screaming his name as gold confetti rained down over the green field.

Leon immediately slid down from his teammates' shoulders, ignoring the media cameras, ignoring the trophy presentation, and ignoring the cheering crowd.

He took off his helmet, tossing it onto the grass, his bright blue eyes searching frantically through the crowd until they locked onto the sleek figure standing near the sideline wall.

Ada didn't run. She stood calmly near the barrier, her father's black leather jacket draped casually over her arm, the custom t-shirt with his face still proudly on display.

Leon broke into a sprint, pushing past trainers and players, running straight toward her with emotion filling his flushed face. He reached the barrier wall, reached out with his broad arms, and effortlessly lifted her off the concrete ledge, pulling her whole frame tight against his chest.

Ada let out a soft, genuine burst of laughter, her slender arms wrapping tightly around his neck, her dark hair brushing against his cheek as he held her like she was the most precious treasure in the world.

Leon pulled back just an inch, his broad hands framing her face, his blue eyes shining with tears of joy as he looked down into her dark eyes.

"You came," Leon breathed out, his voice cracking. "Ada.... you cheered for me...you...chose..."

Ada looked up into his handsome, sweat-streaked face, her dark eyes filled with radiant tenderness that she no longer had any desire to hide. She reached up, her slender hand gently cupping his cheek, her thumb lightly brushing over his skin.

"Don't be silly, Leon...." Ada murmured softly.

She reached into her jacket pocket, pulling out the small brown teddy bear keychain wearing the number seven hoodie, holding it up between their faces.

Leon looked at the tiny plush bear, then looked back at Ada's beautiful, gentle smile, a breathless, hysterical laugh escaping his lips as fresh tears of happiness spilled over his lashes.

"I love you," Ada said softly, the three words falling from her lips, ringing louder in his heart than the thousands of cheering fans around them

Leon’s breath caught squarely in his throat. A wide smile lit up his entire face as he leaned down, pressing his lips firmly, deeply, and passionately against hers right there on the championship field, sealing their story under the bright morning sun as the stadium cheered their names.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

The human mind is a terrifying gift.

It remembers every wound with impossible precision, files away every humiliation, every warning, every name attached to grief. It learns patterns from pain until survival becomes instinct. It can calculate the safest route home, recognize danger in a stranger's smile, and build walls so high that even sunlight struggles to reach what lives inside. It is clever enough to predict heartbreak before it happens, to rehearse every goodbye, to imagine a thousand futures where choosing love ends with blood on the floor and silence in the rooms afterward.

The mind is built to protect.

It whispers don't. Don't trust. Don't forgive. Don't call them back. Don't become vulnerable enough that another person can carve themselves into your ribs and leave you hollow. It presents evidence like a patient lawyer, laying trauma upon trauma until fear sounds indistinguishable from reason.

God, it tries so hard to keep us safe.

And yet—

The heart has never cared much for evidence.

It reaches anyway.

It loves people it knows it cannot keep. It waits for footsteps that may never return. It forgives without permission from logic, mourns things it never truly possessed, and chooses impossible hopes over guaranteed peace. It knows exactly how stories like this end. It has seen the scars, counted the funerals, memorized absence until it should know better.

Still, it opens the door.

That is the contradiction of being human. The mind can chart every disaster waiting at the end of the road, but it cannot chain desire forever. It can scream until its voice breaks, construct prisons from caution and common sense, but eventually the heart slips through the cracks with bleeding hands, carrying flowers to someone who has only ever offered thorns.

Perhaps that is humanity's greatest curse.

Or perhaps it is its greatest miracle.

Because the same species capable of imagining every conceivable horror is also the only one foolish enough to look directly to it. And no matter how sharp the intellect, no matter how terrifying the capacity to know better, we will always burn ourselves alive for the things we love.

─•~❉᯽❉~•─

Ada rolled smoothly onto her stomach, propping herself up slightly on her elbows while keeping her bare legs tangled with his under the sheets. A lazy, thoroughly satisfied smile touched her lips, her dark eyes sparkling with a soft, feline contentment as she looked down at his handsome, relaxed face.

"You look remarkably pleased with yourself, Miss Wong," Leon teased softly, his fingers lightly tracing small circles over her bare shoulder.

"I am thoroughly satisfied, Kennedy," Ada replied smoothly,"You perform exceptionally well under pressure."

Leon flushed a faint pink, a boyish, happy grin breaking across his face. "I aim to please my target audience."

Ada’s expression softened, her gaze lingering on his blue eyes. The playful atmosphere shifted into something genuine, and long overdue. She shifted slightly, resting her chin on her hand as she looked at him.

"Now," Ada began calmly. "We need to discuss what you saw in the corridor yesterday evening."

Leon’s smile faltered slightly, his blue eyes flickering with a faint, lingering shadow of hesitation. He reached out, his broad hand coming to rest over hers on the bedsheet. "Ada... you don't have to explain anything if you don't want to. I know I reacted badly, and I—"

"I want to explain, Leon," Ada interrupted gently, her fingers turning to lace through his. "You deserve the full truth."

Leon stayed silent, listening intently as his thumb lightly rubbed the back of her hand. "Luis came backstage under the pretense of delivering news about his research grant," Ada explained, her tone detached, and matter-of-fact. "He told me he was leaving the university program to move to Madrid at the end of the week."

Leon raised an eyebrow. "Luis is leaving?"

"He is," Ada confirmed with a small nod. "He chose that moment to express a series of unwanted, misguided sentiments regarding my personal life and my interactions with you."

"What kind of sentiments?" Leon asked softly, his jaw setting slightly.

"He assumed that because I spent two years keeping my distance from everyone, I was entirely unreachable," Ada said, a subtle curve touching her lips. "He was upset because he realized that you were the only person who actually reached me."

Leon blinked, his heart giving a small, happy flutter against his ribs, though he kept his attention focused on her words.

"Before I could step back or address his foolish statements," Ada continued, her dark eyes looking straight into Leon's blue ones with honesty, "he acted entirely without my consent and kissed me. I pushed him away immediately and told him in no uncertain terms that his presence was no longer tolerated."

Leon stared at her, the final lingering pieces of doubt and confusion in his mind dissolving into clarity. "You pushed him away?"

"I pushed him away," Ada repeated firmly, her voice dropping into a low register as she leaned down slightly, pressing her lips softly against his cheek. "There is only one person allowed to kiss me, quarterback. And he happens to be lying right next to me."

A smile broke across Leon’s face, his blue eyes shining. He wrapped both of his broad arms around her waist, pulling her whole body flat against his chest, burying his face in her dark hair as he let out a breathless, joyful laugh.

"I love you, Ada," Leon whispered into her hair, holding her tightly.

"I know, Leon," Ada murmured softly, a rare, breathtakingly sweet smile gracing her face as she rested her head against his chest, listening to the steady beat of his heart under her skin. "I know."

My dearest girl,

There will come a day when my footsteps no longer follow yours.

You will lace your skates with hands that no longer need mine, step onto a sheet of fresh ice, and hear nothing but the scrape of steel beneath your feet. The rink will feel impossibly large then. The lights will seem brighter than they used to, and for a fleeting moment, you'll look toward the stands out of habit.

You may not find me there.

That thought used to frighten me. It still does, if I'm being truthful. Fathers spend their whole lives pretending they are teaching their daughters how to live, when in reality we are trying to prepare our own hearts for the day they no longer need us to catch them.

And I hope—more than anything—I succeeded.

Not because I want you to remember every lesson, every correction, every time I told you to keep your chin up and your knees bent. Those things will fade. They should. Life will teach you better than I ever could.

Instead, I hope you remember the way I watched you.

From the far side of the rink, where I always stood with cold hands tucked into my pockets, pretending I wasn't holding my breath every time you attempted something new. I watched every stumble as though I had fallen myself. I celebrated every landing before you even realized you'd stayed on your feet. I learned the language of your determination long before you knew how to name it.

You probably thought I was only watching you skate.

I wasn't.

I was watching you become.

Become brave enough to rise after every fall. Gentle enough to applaud others. Stubborn enough to try again when the ice felt unforgiving. Strong enough to carry your own dreams.
