One day, when the world feels unbearable, when grief settles beside you like winter refusing to leave, I hope you'll skate again.

Not because you're running from the loneliness.

Because you'll remember me.

Remember the mornings that smelled of sharpened blades and dark coffee. Remember the man whose greatest privilege was standing far enough away to let you shine, yet close enough to catch every precious moment.

If, by then, I've become nothing more than a memory tucked between old photographs and faded scarves, let that memory be a warm one.

Let it rest against your heart like hands that never truly left.

And if you ever glance toward the empty stands, wondering whether I can still see you...

Smile.

Take your place at center ice.

Lift your chin.

Then skate.

I have loved you from every distance life has ever asked of me, and I suspect eternity will ask for nothing less.

A.W.

The bright afternoon sun poured down over the vast, green expanses of the complex, washing the concrete bleachers in a brilliant, golden warmth. A crisp autumn breeze swept across the open field, carrying the faint, sweet scent of freshly cut grass, popcorn from the distant concession stands, and the energetic noise of whistles blowing across the turf.

High up in the grandstand seats, Ada sat with perfect posture.

She was in her second year of college, her reputation as a rising star in the figure skating division already firmly established across campus. She wore a sleek, black fitted jacket zipped halfway up her throat, dark jeans, and her red-framed glasses resting neatly on the bridge of her nose. Her dark hair was cut into its sharp, immaculate bob, her dark eyes scanning the field below with detachment that kept most passing students at a respectful distance.

Down on the lower track, the university cheerleading squad was conducting their daily afternoon conditioning practice under the bright sky.

Ashley was standing near the center of the rubber track line, wearing her yellow cheer practice fleece and pleated skirt. She was holding a megaphone, her high-pitched, commanding voice echoing across the stadium as she guided her squad through a complex sequence of split jumps and synchronized motions.

"Keep those arms sharp! Extension! Extension!" Ashley barked out through the megaphone, clapping her hands together twice. "If your toes aren't pointed on the landing, you are repeating the entire sequence!"

Ada watched the performance with a subtle, entertained curve touching the corner of her lips. She enjoyed the structure of athletic routines, the dedication to repetition, even if cheerleading lacked the frozen grace of the ice rink.

"I swear, Ashley has enough volume to replace the entire stadium sound system," a soft voice chimed in from the side aisle.

Mia walked up the concrete steps, holding two cold paper cups of iced lemonade in her hands. She wore a casual university hoodie, her light brown hair tied back into a loose, comfortable clip. Mia stepped into the top row, sliding smoothly into the empty plastic seat right beside Ada, handing one of the lemonade cups toward her.

"Thank you, Mia," Ada murmured smoothly, taking the cold cup and resting it lightly over her knee without breaking her gaze from the field below.

"How was your afternoon ice session?" Mia asked, taking a sip from her own drink as she leaned back against the plastic chair. "Marvin said you were testing a new jump sequence today."

"The triple loop is consistent," Ada replied calmly. "The landing edge requires a minor adjustment, but otherwise, the program is proceeding according to schedule."

"You always make it sound like a math problem, Ada," Mia chuckled softly, her eyes sweeping over the track section where Ashley was currently scolding a freshman cheerleader for a sloppy arm placement. "Look at Ashley down there. She's been out there since one o'clock. I don't know where she gets that level of stamina."

"She possesses ambition," Ada noted smoothly, taking a slow sip of her lemonade. "It is an effective quality for a team captain."

As they spoke, a sudden, loud commotion erupted near the main entrance gates leading onto the central football field.

A group of varsity football players in matching crimson practice jerseys came pouring out of the locker room tunnel, tossing a leather football back and forth as they made their way toward the primary bench area. They were laughing, shouting, and shoving each other with high-spirited energy.

Among them was a tall, remarkably handsome young man who instantly drew the eye.

He wore a dark blue athletic shirt that hugged his broad shoulders, fitted athletic shorts, and fresh cleats that clicked crisply against the track pavement. His bright blonde hair was slightly messy, falling over his forehead in effortless fringe that caught the warm sunlight with every turn of his head. He carried a large gym bag slung casually over one shoulder, a bright, easy smile spreading across his face as he conversed with varsity offensive linemen walking beside him.

Ada’s sharp dark eyes caught the movement immediately.

She didn't usually pay attention to the football roster. To her, most of the varsity players were loud, predictable, and entirely uninteresting. But there was something distinctly different about this particular individual.

He didn't walk with the arrogant, towering swagger of the senior defensive captains. His posture was open and surprisingly gentle. As they walked past the track, one of the freshman cheerleaders dropped a pom-pom near the fence line. Without missing a single beat, the blonde athlete stepped out of his group, picked up the pom-pom with effortless grace, handed it back to the blushing girl with a warm, genuine grin, and jogged right back to his friends, who immediately began teasing him with good-natured shoulder shoves.

Ada’s dark eyes narrowed slightly behind her glasses, her attention thoroughly caught. She watched him interact with the rest of the team, noticing how effortlessly he seemed to fit into the group despite the obvious fact that no one had seen him on the field during the previous semester. He was laughing, tossing the football with impressive, fluid precision, his bright blue eyes crinkling at the corners as he chatted with the head trainer.

He was a complete stranger, yet he moved through the space with an effortless, magnetic warmth that was entirely impossible to ignore.

Ada tilted her head, her fingers resting against the cold paper of her lemonade cup as she leaned a fraction of an inch forward in her seat.

"Mia," Ada spoke, her voice dropping into a low register that cut cleanly through the surrounding noise of the breeze and the cheering down below.

"Hmm?" Mia responded, looking up from her drink.

Ada didn't take her eyes off the blonde athlete down on the turf. "Who is that?"

Mia followed Ada’s line of sight down to the field, her gaze landing on the tall blonde player who was currently executing a crisp, thirty-yard pass straight into the hands of a receiver, prompting a chorus of loud cheers from the coaching staff standing on the sideline. "Oh!" Mia’s face lit up with immediate recognition, a knowing, bright smile spreading across her lips. "You haven't heard about him yet? He just transferred here at the start of the week."

"A transferee?" Ada repeated softly.

"Yeah," Mia nodded, leaning forward with her elbows on her knees. "He transferred in from a college up north. The athletic board was fighting over his division transfer papers for two months because his high school stats were absolutely insane. Coach Burton was practically throwing a party in his office when the transfer was finalized."

Ada watched as the blonde athlete caught a return pass, spinning around a defensive dummy with rapid, fluid agility, his blonde hair catching the bright afternoon light. "He moves well."

"He's a quarterback," Mia explained, her tone filled with genuine admiration. "Carlos and Chris were talking to Ethan about him in the student union yesterday. They said he memorized half the offensive playbook in two days. But what's really crazy is how friendly he is. Usually, guys with his talent level are completely insufferable, but he's been helping the junior staff carry gear tubs all week."

"Friendly," Ada murmured softly, the word tasting strange and novel on her tongue as she observed the way the tall athlete laughed at a joke made by one of the junior trainers, clapping the man warmly on the shoulder.

"Extremely friendly," Mia confirmed with a light giggle. "Half the cheer section is already swooning over him, though I hear he's completely oblivious to it. He just spends all his time practicing, studying, or hanging out with his friends."

Ada’s dark eyes tracked him as he stepped toward the team bench, pulling a water bottle from his bag, his posture relaxed, handsome, and captivating under the open sky.

For the very first time since she had arrived at the university, Ada felt a strange spark of genuine curiosity blooming within her chest. A complete stranger, so bright, so open, and so utterly unburdened by the cold calculations of the competitive world she inhabited. "What is his name?" Ada asked, her voice calm and quiet against the autumn breeze.

Mia turned her head, looking at Ada's composed side profile, noticing the rare, focused intensity in her dark eyes behind her glasses.

"His name is Leon," Mia answered softly, a warm smile touching her face. "Leon Kennedy."
